## CrewAI Documentation Summary

I've completed a thorough search of the kix-platform-docs repository. Here's what I found:

### **CrewAI Documentation Location & Structure**

All CrewAI documentation is organized in a dedicated subdirectory:
- **Location:** `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-docs/docs/crewai/`

### **CrewAI Documentation Files (7 files total)**

| File | Size | Purpose |
|------|------|---------|
| **index.md** | 11K | Main overview and platform architecture |
| **gcp-setup.md** | 11K | Terraform infrastructure configuration |
| **install.md** | 12K | ArgoCD deployment workflow |
| **post-install.md** | 6.9K | Post-deployment setup and configuration |
| **troubleshooting.md** | 12K | Common issues and debug commands |

### **Sidebar Configuration**

The CrewAI section is registered in `/sidebars.js` under **Platform Services** category as a nested subcategory:

```javascript
{
  type: 'category',

  ],

```

**Sidebar position:** Listed under "Platform Services" (lines 72-94 in sidebars.js)

### **Documentation Content Overview**

#### **1. index.md** (CrewAI Enterprise Overview)
- Overview of CrewAI Platform as enterprise AI orchestration framework
- Deployment model (GitOps with Terraform + ArgoCD)
- Environment information (dev, qa, prod with specific domains)
- Quick start deployment workflow
- Key features and architecture diagram
- Repository links to `kix-services-crewai`, `kix-platform-modules`, `kix-platform-infrastructure`

#### **2. gcp-setup.md** (Infrastructure Setup)
- Terraform repository structure
- Infrastructure components created (service accounts, namespaces, storage, databases)
- Remote modules used from `kix-platform-modules`
- Per-environment configuration (dev/qa/prod)
- Secrets management (dual-layer: GCP Secret Manager + K8s Secrets)
- Kubernetes resources (namespaces, network policies, resource quotas)
- Gateway API configuration
- Making infrastructure changes workflow

#### **3. install.md** (Deployment Guide)
- Deployment architecture overview
- Prerequisites and environments
- ArgoCD multi-source application pattern
- Deployment workflow (6 steps)
- Helm values configuration
- CrewAI version upgrade process
- Monitoring deployment via ArgoCD and GitHub Actions
- Rollback procedures
- Nightly quality checks
- Troubleshooting

#### **4. post-install.md** (Post-Installation Setup)
- Initial setup tasks (4 main sections)
- Kubernetes rake commands for organization setup
- Permission configuration
- Studio V2 enablement (optional)
- Application access via Gateway API and port forwarding
- Verification checklist

#### **5. troubleshooting.md** (Troubleshooting Guide)
- ArgoCD & GitOps issues (sync failures, terraform errors)
- Deployment issues (ImagePullBackOff, database connections)
- Authentication & authorization (WorkOS, Google OIDC)
- Network & gateway issues (502 errors, DNS resolution)
- Storage issues (GCS access)
- Kubernetes pod issues (pending, CrashLoopBackOff)
- Comprehensive debug commands reference
- Support resources and issue reporting guidance

#### **6. third-party.md** (Third-Party Integrations)
- WorkOS authentication provider setup
- OAuth configuration variables
- WorkOS AuthKit integration details
- WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD generation
- Current setup summary
- Integration best practices
- Security recommendations

#### **7. faq.md** (Frequently Asked Questions)
- General questions about CrewAI Platform
- System requirements (dev & production)
- Cost estimates per environment
- Deployment process and timeline
- Authentication methods supported
- Custom domain configuration
- Scaling, storage, and resource customization
- Operations & troubleshooting
- Security & compliance features
- Integration & development

### **Services Integration**

CrewAI is referenced in `/docs/services/index.md` as a platform service, though not as a main service but as a nested subcategory under "Platform Services" alongside Kong, LiteLLM, Cloud SQL, and Redis.

### **Key Repository References**

All documentation links to the main deployment repository:
- **Primary:** https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai
- **Modules:** https://github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules
- **Infrastructure:** https://github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-infrastructure

### **Environment Domains**

| Environment | Domain |
|-------------|--------|
| Development | `crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com` |
| QA | `crewai.qa.ix.konecta-digital.com` |
| Production | `crewai.ix.konecta-digital.com` |

### **Documentation Statistics**

- **Total files:** 7 markdown files
- **Total size:** ~83K
- **Topics covered:** 
  - Infrastructure (Terraform)
  - Deployment (ArgoCD + GitHub Actions)
  - Post-deployment setup
  - Troubleshooting (70+ specific issues covered)
  - Third-party integrations
  - FAQs (50+ questions answered)

# CrewAI Crew Namespace Configuration

## Overview

The CrewAI platform uses **separate namespaces** for platform components and deployed crew workloads:

- **Platform Namespace** (`crewai-{env}`): Contains the platform components (web, worker, buildkit)
- **Crew Namespace** (`crewai-crews-{env}`): Contains deployed crew workloads

This separation provides:
- **Resource isolation**: Crews run in their own namespace with dedicated quotas
- **Security boundaries**: Network policies control cross-namespace communication
- **RBAC granularity**: Platform service account has elevated permissions in crew namespace
- **Operational clarity**: Easier monitoring, debugging, and management of crew workloads

## Configuration

### Helm Values

Each environment's Helm values file (`helm/values/values-{env}.yaml`) includes:

```yaml
# Crew workloads namespace (separate from platform namespace)
# This is where deployed crews will execute
crewNamespace: "crewai-crews-{env}"
```

### Environment-Specific Namespaces

| Environment | Platform Namespace | Crew Namespace         |
|-------------|-------------------|------------------------|
| Dev         | `crewai-dev`      | `crewai-crews-dev`    |
| QA          | `crewai-qa`       | `crewai-crews-qa`     |
| Prod        | `crewai-prod`     | `crewai-crews-prod`   |

## Architecture Components

### 1. Namespace Manifests

**Base Configuration:**
- `kubernetes/kustomize/base/namespace/namespace.yaml` - Platform namespace
- `kubernetes/kustomize/base/namespace/crew-namespace.yaml` - Crew namespace

**Labels:**
- Platform namespace: `component: platform`
- Crew namespace: `component: crews`

### 2. RBAC Configuration

**Location:** `kubernetes/kustomize/base/rbac/crew-access-role.yaml`

The platform service account (`crewai-platform-sa`) has:
- **In platform namespace**: Limited permissions (ConfigMaps, Secrets read-only)
- **In crew namespace**: Full management permissions for crew workloads

**Granted Permissions in Crew Namespace:**
- Full Pod management (create, delete, exec, logs)
- ConfigMap and Secret management
- Deployment, StatefulSet, Job management
- Service and PVC management

### 3. Network Policies

**Location:** `kubernetes/kustomize/base/network-policies/allow-crews.yaml`

**Default Deny:** All traffic is denied by default in crew namespace

**Allowed Ingress (to crews):**
- From platform web pods → crew pods (port 8080)
- From platform worker pods → crew pods (port 8080)

**Allowed Egress (from crews):**
- To DNS (kube-system namespace)
- To GCP metadata server (169.254.169.254) - for Workload Identity
- To external HTTPS (GCP APIs, external services)
- To platform buildkit (for container builds)
- To platform worker (for job management)
- To platform web (for API callbacks)

### 4. Resource Quotas

**Location:** `kubernetes/kustomize/base/resource-limits/crew-resource-quota.yaml`

#### Development/QA (Default)
```yaml
CPU: 20 cores request / 40 cores limit
Memory: 40Gi request / 80Gi limit
Pods: 100
Storage: 200Gi
```

#### Production (Scaled)
```yaml
CPU: 80 cores request / 160 cores limit
Memory: 160Gi request / 320Gi limit
Pods: 300
Storage: 1Ti
```

**Per-Container Limits:**
```yaml
Max: 4 CPU / 8Gi memory
Default: 500m CPU / 1Gi memory
Default Request: 250m CPU / 512Mi memory
```

## Pod Security Standards

Both namespaces enforce Pod Security Admission policies:

| Environment | Platform Namespace | Crew Namespace    |
|-------------|-------------------|-------------------|
| Dev         | Baseline          | Baseline          |
| QA          | Baseline/Audit Restricted | Baseline/Audit Restricted |
| Prod        | Restricted        | Restricted        |

## Deployment Workflow

### 1. Namespace Creation

Namespaces are created via Kustomize overlays during ArgoCD sync:

```bash
# Created in sync-wave -5 (very early)
kubectl get namespaces -l platform=crewai
```

### 2. RBAC Setup

Platform service account binding to crew namespace happens in sync-wave -1:

```bash
# Verify RBAC
kubectl get rolebinding -n crewai-crews-dev crewai-platform-crew-manager-binding
```

### 3. Network Policy Application

Network policies are applied in sync-wave 0:

```bash
# Verify network policies
kubectl get networkpolicies -n crewai-crews-dev
```

### 4. Crew Deployment

Platform components can now deploy crews to the crew namespace:

```bash
# Example: Platform creates a crew deployment
kubectl create deployment my-crew \
  --image=registry.crewai.com/crew:latest \
  --namespace=crewai-crews-dev
```

## Monitoring and Operations

### Check Crew Namespace Status

```bash
# List all resources in crew namespace
kubectl get all -n crewai-crews-dev

# Check resource quota usage
kubectl describe resourcequota crew-quota -n crewai-crews-dev

# View network policies
kubectl describe networkpolicy -n crewai-crews-dev
```

### Debug Crew Connectivity

```bash
# Test DNS resolution from a crew pod
kubectl exec -it <crew-pod> -n crewai-crews-dev -- nslookup crewai-web.crewai-dev.svc.cluster.local

# Check if crew can reach platform web
kubectl exec -it <crew-pod> -n crewai-crews-dev -- curl -v http://crewai-web.crewai-dev.svc.cluster.local:443
```

### View RBAC Permissions

```bash
# Check what platform SA can do in crew namespace
kubectl auth can-i --list --as=system:serviceaccount:crewai-dev:crewai-platform-sa -n crewai-crews-dev
```

## Troubleshooting

### Issue: Crew pods cannot reach platform services

**Cause:** Network policy blocking traffic

**Solution:**
1. Verify crew pods have correct labels: `platform: crewai`, `component: crews`
2. Check network policy allows egress to platform namespace:
   ```bash
   kubectl describe networkpolicy allow-crew-egress -n crewai-crews-dev
   ```

### Issue: Platform cannot create crews

**Cause:** Missing RBAC permissions

**Solution:**
1. Verify RoleBinding exists:
   ```bash
   kubectl get rolebinding crewai-platform-crew-manager-binding -n crewai-crews-dev
   ```
2. Check service account is correct:
   ```bash
   kubectl get rolebinding crewai-platform-crew-manager-binding -n crewai-crews-dev -o yaml
   ```

### Issue: Resource quota exceeded

**Cause:** Too many crews running

**Solution:**
1. Check current usage:
   ```bash
   kubectl describe resourcequota crew-quota -n crewai-crews-dev
   ```
2. Scale down or delete unused crews:
   ```bash
   kubectl get deployments -n crewai-crews-dev
   kubectl delete deployment <unused-crew> -n crewai-crews-dev
   ```

## Security Considerations

1. **Namespace Isolation**: Crews cannot access platform namespace resources directly
2. **Network Segmentation**: Default deny policy prevents unauthorized communication
3. **Resource Limits**: Prevents resource exhaustion attacks
4. **RBAC Separation**: Platform components have minimal permissions in their own namespace, elevated only in crew namespace
5. **Pod Security**: Enforces baseline/restricted standards to prevent privilege escalation

## References

- [CrewAI Configuration Documentation](https://enterprise-docs.crewai.com/reference/chart-values#param-crew-namespace)
- [Kubernetes RBAC](https://kubernetes.io/docs/reference/access-authn-authz/rbac/)
- [Kubernetes Network Policies](https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/services-networking/network-policies/)
- [Pod Security Standards](https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/security/pod-security-standards/)
```

# Gateway API and Network Policy Documentation

This document describes the GKE Gateway API configuration and network policies used for CrewAI Platform ingress and traffic control.

## Overview

CrewAI uses the **Kubernetes Gateway API** (not legacy Ingress) for external traffic routing. The Gateway API provides:
- More expressive routing capabilities
- Better support for multi-tenancy
- Native TLS termination with GCP-managed certificates
- Integration with GKE's Global External Application Load Balancer

## Architecture

```
                    ┌─────────────────────────────────────┐
                    │         Global Load Balancer         │
                    │   (gke-l7-global-external-managed)   │
                    └─────────────────┬───────────────────┘
                                      │
                    ┌─────────────────▼───────────────────┐
                    │              Gateway                 │
                    │    (crewai-gateway-dev-gateway)     │
                    │         Namespace: crewai-dev        │
                    └─────────────────┬───────────────────┘
                                      │
                    ┌─────────────────▼───────────────────┐
                    │            HTTPRoute                 │
                    │    Routes traffic to backends        │
                    └───────────────┬─┬───────────────────┘
                                    │ │
                 ┌──────────────────┘ └──────────────────┐
                 ▼                                        ▼
    ┌────────────────────────┐            ┌────────────────────────┐
    │     crewai-web:443     │            │   crewai-oauth:8080    │
    │   (main application)   │            │  (OAuth integrations)  │
    └────────────────────────┘            └────────────────────────┘
```

## Helm Chart Templates

The Gateway API resources are defined in Helm templates under `environments/{env}/helm/crewai/templates/`:

| File | Resource | Purpose |
|------|----------|---------|
| `gateway.yaml` | Gateway | L7 load balancer configuration |
| `httproute.yaml` | HTTPRoute | Traffic routing rules |
| `networkpolicy.yaml` | NetworkPolicy | Pod-to-pod traffic control |
| `healthcheckpolicy.yaml` | HealthCheckPolicy | Backend health configuration |
| `backendconfig.yaml` | BackendConfig | GKE-specific backend settings |
| `poddisruptionbudget.yaml` | PodDisruptionBudget | Availability during updates |
| `referencegrant.yaml` | ReferenceGrant | Cross-namespace access |

## Gateway Configuration

### Gateway Class

The Gateway uses `gke-l7-global-external-managed` which provisions:
- Global External Application Load Balancer
- Automatic SSL certificate management
- Cloud CDN integration (optional)
- Cloud Armor integration (optional)

### TLS Configuration

TLS termination happens at the Gateway level. Options:

1. **GCP-managed certificates** (recommended):
   ```yaml
   gateway:
     listeners:
       https:
         tls:
           options:
             networking.gke.io/pre-shared-certs: "crewai-cert"
   ```

2. **Kubernetes Secret** (for custom certificates):
   ```yaml
   gateway:
     listeners:
       https:
         tls:
           certificateRefs:
             - name: crewai-tls-secret
               kind: Secret
   ```

## HTTPRoute Rules

The HTTPRoute defines path-based routing:

| Path | Backend Service | Port | Description |
|------|-----------------|------|-------------|
| `/` (default) | crewai-web | 443 | Main application |
| `/oauthsvc/*` | crewai-oauth | 8080 | OAuth integrations |

### Enabling the Gateway

To enable Gateway resources, add to your `values.yaml`:

```yaml
gateway:
  enabled: true
  name: "crewai-gateway"
  gatewayClassName: "gke-l7-global-external-managed"
  staticIP: "crewai-global-ip"  # Optional: pre-reserved IP
  sslPolicy: "modern-ssl-policy"  # Optional: Cloud Armor SSL policy

  listeners:
    https:
      enabled: true
      name: "https"
      port: 443
      protocol: "HTTPS"
      hostname: "crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com"
      tls:
        mode: Terminate
        options:
          networking.gke.io/pre-shared-certs: "crewai-cert"
      allowedRoutes:
        namespaces:
          from: Same

httpRoute:
  enabled: true
  hostnames:
    - "crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com"
  parentRefs:
    - name: ""  # Uses gateway.name if empty
      sectionName: "https"
  rules:
    - matches:
        - path:
            type: PathPrefix
            value: /
      backendRefs:
        - name: crewai-web
          port: 443

crewai:
  namespace: "crewai-dev"
  serviceName: "crewai-web"
  servicePort: 443
```

## Network Policies

Network policies restrict pod-to-pod communication for defense in depth.

### Default Deny

The base network policy denies all ingress by default. Only explicitly allowed traffic can reach pods.

### Allowed Traffic

| Source | Destination | Port | Purpose |
|--------|-------------|------|---------|
| Gateway namespace | crewai-web | 443 | External traffic via Gateway |
| Gateway namespace | crewai-oauth | 8080 | OAuth service traffic |
| crewai-dev | cloud-sql-proxy | 5432 | Database connectivity |
| kube-system (DNS) | All pods | 53/UDP | DNS resolution |

### Enabling Network Policies

```yaml
networkPolicy:
  enabled: true
  podSelector:
    app.kubernetes.io/name: crewai
  gatewayNamespaceLabels:
    kubernetes.io/metadata.name: crewai-dev
  egress:
    enabled: true
    allowExternal: true
    externalPorts:
      - protocol: TCP
        port: 443
      - protocol: TCP
        port: 5432
  isolateEnvironments: true  # Block traffic from other env namespaces
```

## Pod Disruption Budget

Ensures minimum availability during voluntary disruptions (node upgrades, deployments):

```yaml
podDisruptionBudget:
  enabled: true
  minAvailable: 1
  # OR
  maxUnavailable: "25%"
```

## Health Checks

GKE uses health checks to determine backend readiness:

```yaml
healthCheckPolicy:
  enabled: true
  port: 443
  path: /health
  interval: 15s
  timeout: 5s
  healthyThreshold: 2
  unhealthyThreshold: 3
```

## Cross-Namespace Access

If the Gateway and backend services are in different namespaces, a ReferenceGrant is required:

```yaml
referenceGrant:
  enabled: true
  fromNamespace: "gateway-namespace"
  toKind: "Service"
```

## Troubleshooting

### Gateway not provisioning

1. Check Gateway status:
   ```bash
   kubectl get gateway -n crewai-dev
   kubectl describe gateway crewai-gateway -n crewai-dev
   ```

2. Verify GatewayClass exists:
   ```bash
   kubectl get gatewayclass gke-l7-global-external-managed
   ```

### HTTPRoute not routing traffic

1. Check route status:
   ```bash
   kubectl get httproute -n crewai-dev
   kubectl describe httproute crewai-route -n crewai-dev
   ```

2. Verify backend service exists and has endpoints:
   ```bash
   kubectl get svc crewai-web -n crewai-dev
   kubectl get endpoints crewai-web -n crewai-dev
   ```

### Network policy blocking traffic

1. Test connectivity from a debug pod:
   ```bash
   kubectl run debug --rm -it --image=busybox -n crewai-dev -- wget -qO- http://crewai-web:443/health
   ```

2. Check network policy logs (if enabled):
   ```bash
   kubectl logs -l app=crewai-web -n crewai-dev | grep -i network
   ```

## References

- [Kubernetes Gateway API](https://gateway-api.sigs.k8s.io/)
- [GKE Gateway Controller](https://cloud.google.com/kubernetes-engine/docs/concepts/gateway-api)
- [GKE Network Policies](https://cloud.google.com/kubernetes-engine/docs/how-to/network-policy)
- [Cloud Armor with GKE Gateway](https://cloud.google.com/kubernetes-engine/docs/how-to/configure-cloud-armor-gateway)
```

# GCP Uptime Checks - Native Monitoring Replacement

This document describes how to replace GitHub Actions-based health checks with native GCP Uptime Checks for better reliability, lower latency, and integration with GCP monitoring.

## Background

**Current approach** (GitHub Actions `.github/workflows/health-checks.yaml`):
- Runs external health checks from GitHub Actions runners
- Limited to 5-minute intervals
- No native integration with GCP alerting
- Consumes GitHub Actions minutes
- External dependency on GitHub infrastructure

**Recommended approach** (GCP Uptime Checks):
- Native GCP monitoring service
- 1-minute minimum interval (5x more frequent)
- Integrated with Cloud Monitoring alerts
- No cost for basic uptime checks
- Lower latency (runs from GCP regions)
- Better reliability (Google's infrastructure)

## Migration Benefits

| Feature | GitHub Actions | GCP Uptime Checks |
|---------|---------------|-------------------|
| **Check Interval** | 5 minutes (scheduled) | 1 minute (configurable) |
| **Alert Integration** | Manual webhook/email | Native Monitoring alerts |
| **Monitoring Dashboard** | None | Cloud Monitoring console |
| **Cost** | GitHub Actions minutes | Free (basic checks) |
| **Latency** | External to GCP | Within GCP network |
| **Multi-region** | Manual setup | Built-in from 6+ regions |
| **Historical Data** | Custom logging | 6-month retention |

## Implementation

### 1. Terraform Module for Uptime Checks

Create `terraform/modules/uptime-checks/main.tf`:

```hcl
# Uptime Checks Module
# Creates GCP Monitoring uptime checks for HTTP/HTTPS endpoints

variable "project_id" {
  description = "GCP project ID"
  type        = string
}

variable "environment" {
  description = "Environment name (dev, qa, prod)"
  type        = string
}

variable "endpoints" {
  description = "Map of endpoints to monitor"
  type = map(object({
    host         = string
    path         = string
    port         = number
    use_ssl      = bool
    timeout      = string
    period       = string
    regions      = list(string)
  }))
}

# Uptime check for each endpoint
resource "google_monitoring_uptime_check_config" "endpoint" {
  for_each = var.endpoints

  display_name = "${var.environment}-${each.key}-uptime"
  project      = var.project_id

  timeout = each.value.timeout
  period  = each.value.period

  http_check {
    path         = each.value.path
    port         = each.value.port
    use_ssl      = each.value.use_ssl
    validate_ssl = each.value.use_ssl

    # Optional: Add authentication headers
    # headers = {
    #   "Authorization" = "Bearer ${var.health_check_token}"
    # }

    # Expected response
    accepted_response_status_codes {
      status_class = "STATUS_CLASS_2XX"
    }
  }

  monitored_resource {
    type = "uptime_url"
    labels = {
      project_id = var.project_id
      host       = each.value.host
    }
  }

  # Check from multiple regions for redundancy
  dynamic "selected_regions" {
    for_each = each.value.regions
    content {
      region = selected_regions.value
    }
  }

  # Labels for organization
  user_labels = {
    environment = var.environment
    managed_by  = "terraform"
    app         = "crewai"
  }
}

# Alert policy for uptime check failures
resource "google_monitoring_alert_policy" "uptime_alert" {
  for_each = var.endpoints

  display_name = "${var.environment}-${each.key}-uptime-alert"
  project      = var.project_id

  combiner = "OR"

  conditions {
    display_name = "Uptime check failed"

    condition_threshold {
      filter          = "resource.type = \"uptime_url\" AND metric.type = \"monitoring.googleapis.com/uptime_check/check_passed\" AND resource.labels.host = \"${each.value.host}\""
      duration        = "60s"
      comparison      = "COMPARISON_LT"
      threshold_value = 1

      aggregations {
        alignment_period   = "60s"
        per_series_aligner = "ALIGN_NEXT_OLDER"
      }
    }
  }

  # Notification channels (configure separately)
  notification_channels = var.notification_channels

  alert_strategy {
    auto_close = "1800s"  # Auto-close after 30 minutes

    notification_rate_limit {
      period = "300s"  # Max 1 alert per 5 minutes
    }
  }

  user_labels = {
    environment = var.environment
    severity    = var.environment == "prod" ? "critical" : "warning"
  }
}

output "uptime_check_ids" {
  description = "IDs of created uptime checks"
  value       = { for k, v in google_monitoring_uptime_check_config.endpoint : k => v.id }
}

output "alert_policy_ids" {
  description = "IDs of created alert policies"
  value       = { for k, v in google_monitoring_alert_policy.uptime_alert : k => v.id }
}
```

### 2. Environment Configuration

Add to `terraform/configs/prod.json`:

```json
{
  "uptime_checks": {
    "api": {
      "host": "api.crewai.example.com",
      "path": "/health",
      "port": 443,
      "use_ssl": true,
      "timeout": "10s",
      "period": "60s",
      "regions": [
        "USA",
        "EUROPE",
        "ASIA_PACIFIC"
      ]
    },
    "web": {
      "host": "app.crewai.example.com",
      "path": "/",
      "port": 443,
      "use_ssl": true,
      "timeout": "10s",
      "period": "60s",
      "regions": [
        "USA",
        "EUROPE"
      ]
    }
  }
}
```

### 3. Add Module to Root Configuration

In `terraform/root/main.tf`:

```hcl
# Uptime Checks Module
module "uptime_checks" {
  source = "../modules/uptime-checks"

  project_id             = local.global.project_id
  environment            = var.environment
  endpoints              = local.config.uptime_checks
  notification_channels  = [google_monitoring_notification_channel.ops_team.id]
}

# Notification channel for alerts
resource "google_monitoring_notification_channel" "ops_team" {
  display_name = "${var.environment}-ops-team"
  type         = "email"

  labels = {
    email_address = local.config.ops_email
  }

  user_labels = {
    environment = var.environment
  }
}
```

### 4. Create Uptime Checks

```bash
cd terraform/root
terraform workspace select prod
terraform plan -var="environment=prod"
terraform apply -var="environment=prod"
```

### 5. Verify in GCP Console

Navigate to:
- **Cloud Monitoring** → **Uptime checks**
- View check status, latency graphs, and availability percentage
- **Alerting** → **Policies** to verify alert configuration

## Monitoring Dashboard

Create a dashboard to visualize all uptime checks:

```hcl
resource "google_monitoring_dashboard" "uptime_dashboard" {
  dashboard_json = jsonencode({
    displayName = "${var.environment} - Uptime Monitoring"

    gridLayout = {
      widgets = [
        {
          title = "Uptime Check Availability"
          xyChart = {
            dataSets = [{
              timeSeriesQuery = {
                timeSeriesFilter = {
                  filter = "resource.type=\"uptime_url\" AND metric.type=\"monitoring.googleapis.com/uptime_check/check_passed\""
                  aggregation = {
                    alignmentPeriod    = "60s"
                    perSeriesAligner   = "ALIGN_FRACTION_TRUE"
                    crossSeriesReducer = "REDUCE_MEAN"
                    groupByFields      = ["resource.host"]
                  }
                }
              }
            }]
          }
        },
        {
          title = "Check Latency"
          xyChart = {
            dataSets = [{
              timeSeriesQuery = {
                timeSeriesFilter = {
                  filter = "resource.type=\"uptime_url\" AND metric.type=\"monitoring.googleapis.com/uptime_check/request_latency\""
                  aggregation = {
                    alignmentPeriod    = "60s"
                    perSeriesAligner   = "ALIGN_DELTA"
                    crossSeriesReducer = "REDUCE_MEAN"
                    groupByFields      = ["resource.host"]
                  }
                }
              }
            }]
          }
        }
      ]
    }
  })
}
```

## Alert Configuration

### Critical Services (Production)

```hcl
# Alert after 2 consecutive failures (2 minutes downtime)
resource "google_monitoring_alert_policy" "critical_uptime" {
  display_name = "prod-critical-service-down"

  conditions {
    display_name = "Service unavailable"

    condition_threshold {
      filter          = "resource.type=\"uptime_url\" AND metric.type=\"monitoring.googleapis.com/uptime_check/check_passed\" AND resource.labels.host=\"api.crewai.example.com\""
      duration        = "120s"
      comparison      = "COMPARISON_LT"
      threshold_value = 1
    }
  }

  notification_channels = [
    google_monitoring_notification_channel.pagerduty.id,
    google_monitoring_notification_channel.ops_email.id,
    google_monitoring_notification_channel.slack.id
  ]

  alert_strategy {
    auto_close = "600s"  # Auto-close after 10 minutes of recovery
  }
}
```

### Non-Critical Services (Dev/QA)

```hcl
# Alert after 5 consecutive failures (5 minutes downtime)
resource "google_monitoring_alert_policy" "dev_uptime" {
  display_name = "dev-service-degraded"

  conditions {
    display_name = "Service degraded"

    condition_threshold {
      duration        = "300s"
      comparison      = "COMPARISON_LT"
      threshold_value = 1
    }
  }

  notification_channels = [
    google_monitoring_notification_channel.ops_email.id
  ]
}
```

## Notification Channels

### Email

```hcl
resource "google_monitoring_notification_channel" "ops_email" {
  display_name = "Ops Team Email"
  type         = "email"

  labels = {
    email_address = "ops@example.com"
  }
}
```

### Slack

```hcl
resource "google_monitoring_notification_channel" "slack" {
  display_name = "Slack #alerts"
  type         = "slack"

  labels = {
    channel_name = "#alerts"
  }

  sensitive_labels {
    auth_token = var.slack_webhook_token
  }
}
```

### PagerDuty

```hcl
resource "google_monitoring_notification_channel" "pagerduty" {
  display_name = "PagerDuty Production"
  type         = "pagerduty"

  sensitive_labels {
    service_key = var.pagerduty_service_key
  }
}
```

## Migration Steps

### Phase 1: Parallel Running (Week 1)

1. Deploy GCP Uptime Checks alongside existing GitHub Actions
2. Verify alerts are triggered correctly
3. Compare reliability and latency

### Phase 2: Validation (Week 2)

1. Ensure all critical endpoints are covered
2. Test alert escalation paths
3. Validate dashboard shows correct data

### Phase 3: Cutover (Week 3)

1. Disable GitHub Actions health check workflow
2. Archive `.github/workflows/health-checks.yaml`
3. Update runbooks to reference GCP Uptime Checks

## Comparison: Before vs After

### GitHub Actions Health Check
```yaml
# .github/workflows/health-checks.yaml
- name: Check API Health
  run: |
    response=$(curl -s -o /dev/null -w "%{http_code}" https://api.crewai.example.com/health)
    if [ $response -ne 200 ]; then
      # Send alert via webhook
      curl -X POST $ALERT_WEBHOOK -d "API health check failed"
    fi
```

**Limitations**:
- ❌ Runs every 5 minutes (cron schedule limitation)
- ❌ No built-in retry logic
- ❌ Manual alert routing
- ❌ No historical trending
- ❌ Limited to GitHub-hosted runners
- ❌ Consumes GitHub Actions minutes

### GCP Uptime Check
```hcl
resource "google_monitoring_uptime_check_config" "api" {
  display_name = "prod-api-health"
  period       = "60s"  # Every minute
  timeout      = "10s"

  http_check {
    path    = "/health"
    port    = 443
    use_ssl = true
  }
}
```

**Benefits**:
- ✅ Runs every minute (12x more frequent)
- ✅ Automatic retries from multiple regions
- ✅ Native integration with Cloud Monitoring
- ✅ 6 months of historical data
- ✅ Checks from 6+ global locations
- ✅ Free for basic HTTP/HTTPS checks

## Cost Analysis

### GitHub Actions (Current)

```
Cost per month:
- Health checks workflow: ~2,000 minutes/month
- At $0.008/minute: $16/month
```

### GCP Uptime Checks (Proposed)

```
Cost per month:
- Basic HTTP/HTTPS checks: FREE
- Advanced checks (10+): ~$0.30 per check per month
- Total for 5 endpoints: ~$1.50/month

Savings: $14.50/month (~90% reduction)
```

## Best Practices

1. **Check Intervals**
   - Production critical: 60 seconds
   - Production non-critical: 300 seconds (5 minutes)
   - Dev/QA: 300-600 seconds

2. **Timeout Configuration**
   - API endpoints: 10 seconds
   - Web pages: 15 seconds
   - Complex operations: 30 seconds

3. **Multi-Region Checks**
   - Production: 3+ regions (USA, EUROPE, ASIA_PACIFIC)
   - QA: 2 regions (USA, EUROPE)
   - Dev: 1 region (USA)

4. **Alert Thresholds**
   - Production: 2 consecutive failures (120s downtime)
   - QA: 3 consecutive failures (180s downtime)
   - Dev: 5 consecutive failures (300s downtime)

5. **Auto-Close Timers**
   - Critical services: 600s (10 minutes)
   - Non-critical: 1800s (30 minutes)

## Troubleshooting

### Uptime Check Shows False Failures

**Symptom**: Check fails intermittently, but service is actually up

**Solutions**:
1. Increase timeout from 10s to 15s
2. Check from fewer regions (reduce to 1-2)
3. Verify firewall rules allow GCP health checker IPs
4. Review application logs for slow responses

### Alerts Not Firing

**Symptom**: Service is down but no alert received

**Solutions**:
1. Verify notification channel is configured correctly
2. Check alert policy is enabled
3. Review alerting dashboard for muted policies
4. Ensure email/Slack/PagerDuty integration is working

### Too Many Alerts

**Symptom**: Alert fatigue from frequent notifications

**Solutions**:
1. Increase alert threshold (e.g., 2 failures → 3 failures)
2. Enable notification rate limiting (max 1 per 5 minutes)
3. Use auto-close to prevent re-alerting
4. Adjust check period to be less frequent

## Resources

- [GCP Uptime Checks Documentation](https://cloud.google.com/monitoring/uptime-checks)
- [Terraform google_monitoring_uptime_check_config](https://registry.terraform.io/providers/hashicorp/google/latest/docs/resources/monitoring_uptime_check_config)
- [Cloud Monitoring Alerting](https://cloud.google.com/monitoring/alerts)
- [Notification Channel Types](https://cloud.google.com/monitoring/support/notification-options)

## Next Steps

1. Create `terraform/modules/uptime-checks/` module
2. Add uptime check configuration to environment configs
3. Deploy to dev environment first for testing
4. Validate alerts fire correctly
5. Roll out to qa and prod
6. Archive GitHub Actions health check workflow
7. Update runbooks and documentation
```

# CrewAI Platform - GCP Deployment

Multi-environment deployment infrastructure for CrewAI Platform on Google Kubernetes Engine (GKE). 

## Overview

This repository contains Infrastructure as Code (Terraform) and GitOps (ArgoCD) configurations for deploying CrewAI Platform across three environments:

- **Development**: `crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com` (kd-ix-eur-dev-gke)
- **QA**: `crewai.qa.ix.konecta-digital.com` (kd-ix-eur-qa-gke)
- **Production**: `crewai.ix.konecta-digital.com` (kd-ix-eur-prod-gke)

## Repository Structure

```
kix-services-crewai/
├── environments/
│   ├── dev/
│   │   ├── argocd/
│   │   │   └── application.yaml      # ArgoCD Application for dev
│   │   ├── helm/
│   │   │   └── values.yaml           # Helm values for OCI chart
│   │   └── terraform/
│   │       ├── backend.tf            # GCS state backend (kd-tfstate-dev)
│   │       ├── main.tf               # Infrastructure + Secrets + IAM
│   │       ├── outputs.tf
│   │       ├── variables.tf
│   │       ├── versions.tf
│   │       └── README.md             # Auto-generated terraform-docs
│   ├── qa/
│   │   ├── argocd/application.yaml
│   │   ├── helm/values.yaml
│   │   └── terraform/               # Same structure as dev
│   └── prod/
│       ├── argocd/application.yaml
│       ├── helm/values.yaml
│       └── terraform/               # Same structure as dev
│
└── docs/
    ├── prps/                         # Product Requirements & Plans
    └── tasks/                        # Task breakdown documents
```

## Architecture

- **Infrastructure**: Terraform per environment composing remote modules from [kix-platform-modules](https://github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules)
- **Deployment**: External OCI Helm chart (`oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable`) deployed via ArgoCD
- **CI/CD**: GitHub Actions for automated testing, security scanning, and progressive deployment
- **Secrets**: Terraform creates secrets in GCP Secret Manager and K8s secrets for application consumption
- **Database**: Cloud SQL databases on existing PostgreSQL instances with Cloud SQL Proxy sidecar
- **Storage**: GCS buckets (data + logs) with S3-compatible HMAC access
- **Service Accounts**: GCP service account with Workload Identity binding to K8s service account
- **Namespaces**: Dual namespaces per environment (`crewai-{env}` + `crewai-crews-{env}`) with resource quotas and network policies

## Prerequisites

### Required Tools

- Terraform >= 1.5.0
- kubectl >= 1.28
- gcloud CLI
- helm >= 3.12
- argocd CLI (optional)

### GCP Resources (Pre-existing)

| Environment | GKE Cluster | Cloud SQL Instance | Project |
|------------|-------------|-------------------|---------|
| Dev | kd-ix-eur-dev-gke | kd-ix-eur-dev-cloudsql-platform | kd-ix-eur-dev-platform-data |
| QA | kd-ix-eur-qa-gke | kd-ix-eur-qa-cloudsql-platform | kd-ix-eur-qa-platform-data |
| Prod | kd-ix-eur-prod-gke | kd-ix-eur-prod-cloudsql-platform | kd-ix-eur-prod-platform-data |

### GitHub Secrets

The following secrets must be configured in GitHub for CI/CD workflows to function. Secrets can be set at the **repository level** (applies to all environments) or as **environment-scoped secrets** (per GitHub Environment) for environment-specific credentials.

**Settings → Secrets and variables → Actions**

#### Repository Secrets (all environments)

| Secret | Used By | Description |
|--------|---------|-------------|
| `GCP_WORKLOAD_IDENTITY_PROVIDER` | terraform-plan, terraform-apply, terraform-drift | GCP Workload Identity Federation provider ID (e.g. `projects/PROJECT_NUMBER/locations/global/workloadIdentityPools/POOL/providers/PROVIDER`). Set up via the `github-actions-oidc` module in [kix-platform-infrastructure](https://github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-infrastructure). |
| `GCP_SERVICE_ACCOUNT` | terraform-plan, terraform-apply, terraform-drift | GCP service account email for GitHub Actions (e.g. `github-kix-services-crewai-dev@kd-ix-eur-dev-gke.iam.gserviceaccount.com`). |
| `TERRAFORM_MODULES_APP_ID` | terraform-plan, terraform-apply, terraform-drift, terraform-validate | GitHub App ID for accessing private Terraform modules in [kix-platform-modules](https://github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules). |
| `TERRAFORM_MODULES_APP_PRIVATE_KEY` | terraform-plan, terraform-apply, terraform-drift, terraform-validate | GitHub App private key (PEM) for the above App. |

> **Note:** If you need different GCP credentials per environment (e.g. separate workload identity pools for dev/qa/prod), configure `GCP_WORKLOAD_IDENTITY_PROVIDER` and `GCP_SERVICE_ACCOUNT` as environment-scoped secrets on the `dev`, `qa`, and `prod` GitHub Environments instead of repository-level secrets.

#### Terraform Variables (sensitive inputs)

These are passed to Terraform at plan/apply time via `TF_VAR_*` environment variables. They should be stored as GitHub Actions secrets and need to be added to the terraform-plan and terraform-apply workflows.

| Secret | Terraform Variable | Description |
|--------|--------------------|-------------|
| `TF_VAR_oauth_client_id` | `oauth_client_id` | WorkOS OAuth client ID |
| `TF_VAR_oauth_api_key` | `oauth_api_key` | WorkOS OAuth API key |
| `TF_VAR_oauth_domain` | `oauth_domain` | WorkOS OAuth domain |
| `TF_VAR_helm_registry_token` | `helm_registry_token` | CrewAI Helm OCI registry (`registry.crewai.com`) authentication token |

> **Note:** These are currently **not wired** into the GitHub Actions workflows. They must be added as `env:` blocks on the Terraform Init/Plan/Apply steps, or set as environment-scoped secrets with `TF_VAR_` prefix so Terraform picks them up automatically.

#### GitHub Environments (for approval gates)

The `validate-plan-apply` workflow uses GitHub Environment protection rules for deployment approvals. Create these environments in **Settings → Environments**:

| Environment | Purpose |
|-------------|---------|
| `dev-approval` | Approval gate before applying to dev |
| `qa-approval` | Approval gate before applying to QA |
| `prod-approval` | Approval gate before applying to production |

## CI/CD Pipeline

This repository uses **GitHub Actions** for continuous integration and deployment.

### Automated Workflows

| Workflow | Trigger | Description |
|----------|---------|-------------|
| **Auto Validate and Plan** | PR to `main`/`develop` modifying `environments/*/terraform/**` | Detects changed Terraform directories, runs validation + security scan + plan |
| **Validate, Plan and Apply** | Manual (`workflow_dispatch`) | Full deploy pipeline: validate → plan → approval gate → apply |
| **Validate and Plan** | Manual / called by nightly | Validate + plan for a single environment (no apply) |
| **Nightly Quality Checks** | Schedule (02:00 UTC daily) | Validates develop→dev, main→qa and runs drift detection on all environments |

### Reusable Workflows

| Workflow | Description |
|----------|-------------|
| `terraform-validate.yml` | Terraform fmt, validate, and Trivy security scan |
| `terraform-plan.yml` | Terraform init + plan with statistics extraction |
| `terraform-apply.yml` | Terraform apply with pre/post validation |
| `terraform-drift.yml` | Drift detection with GitHub issue creation |

### Branch-Environment Rules

| Branch | Allowed Environments |
|--------|---------------------|
| `develop` | dev |
| `main` | dev, qa |
| `v*` tags | prod |

### Deployment Flow

```
Developer → PR → Validate + Plan → Merge to develop
                                            ↓
                          Manual dispatch → Dev Apply (with approval)
                                            ↓
                          Manual dispatch → QA Apply (with approval)
                                            ↓
                        Tag release v* → Prod Apply (with approval, change window enforced)
```

## Quick Start

### 1. Prerequisites Setup

```bash
gcloud auth login
gcloud auth application-default login
gcloud config set project kd-ix-eur-dev-gke
gcloud container clusters get-credentials kd-ix-eur-dev-gke \
  --region=europe-west1 \
  --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-gke
```

### 2. Deploy Infrastructure (Dev)

```bash
cd environments/dev/terraform
terraform init
terraform plan
terraform apply
```

This creates:
- Cloud SQL databases (production, cable, oauth) and user on the existing instance
- GCS buckets for data and logs storage
- GCP service account with HMAC keys for S3-compatible GCS access
- Secrets in GCP Secret Manager (database, GCS, OAuth, Helm registry)
- IAM bindings (Cloud SQL client, Secret Manager accessor, GCS access, Workload Identity)
- Kubernetes namespaces with resource quotas and network policies
- K8s secrets for application credential consumption

### 3. Deploy Application via ArgoCD

Apply the ArgoCD application manifest:

```bash
kubectl apply -f environments/dev/argocd/application.yaml -n argocd
```

ArgoCD will deploy the CrewAI Platform Helm chart with environment-specific values.

### 4. Verify Deployment

```bash
kubectl get pods -n crewai-dev
kubectl get svc -n crewai-dev
curl https://crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com/health
```

## Environment Configuration

### Development
- 1 replica for web and worker pods
- CPU: 1000m requests, 2000m limits
- Memory: 2Gi requests, 4Gi limits
- Auto-sync enabled in ArgoCD

### QA
- 2 replicas for web and worker pods
- CPU: 1500m requests, 3000m limits
- Memory: 4Gi requests, 8Gi limits
- Auto-sync enabled in ArgoCD

### Production
- 3+ replicas with HPA (max 10)
- CPU: 2000m requests, 4000m limits
- Memory: 6Gi requests, 12Gi limits
- Pod Disruption Budget: min 2 available
- Pod anti-affinity and topology spread
- Manual sync in ArgoCD (production safety)

## Terraform

Each environment's Terraform composes remote modules from [kix-platform-modules](https://github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules) with inline resources:

**Remote modules:**
- `data-services/gcs` - GCS buckets (data + logs)
- `secret-manager` - GCP Secret Manager secrets (DB, GCS, OAuth, Helm registry)
- `gke-config` - Kubernetes namespaces, resource quotas, network policies, service accounts

**Inline resources:**
- Cloud SQL databases and user on existing instance
- GCP service account (inline to avoid circular dependency with HMAC keys)
- HMAC keys for S3-compatible GCS access
- IAM bindings (Cloud SQL, Secret Manager, GCS, Workload Identity)
- Kubernetes secrets for application credential consumption

State is stored in per-environment GCS buckets (`kd-tfstate-{env}`) with prefix `services/crewai/resources`.

## Maintenance

### Rotating Secrets

Re-run `terraform apply` in the relevant environment. Terraform manages the database password and HMAC keys - it will regenerate and update both Secret Manager and K8s secrets:

```bash
cd environments/dev/terraform
terraform apply
```

### Upgrading CrewAI Version

Update `targetRevision` in the environment's ArgoCD application:

```bash
# Edit environments/{env}/argocd/application.yaml
# Change targetRevision from 0.3.10 to new version
# Commit and push - ArgoCD auto-syncs (dev/qa) or manually sync (prod)
```

## Troubleshooting

### Pods not starting

```bash
kubectl describe pod <pod-name> -n crewai-dev
kubectl logs <pod-name> -n crewai-dev
kubectl logs <pod-name> -c cloud-sql-proxy -n crewai-dev
```

### Secrets not available

```bash
kubectl get secrets -n crewai-dev
cd environments/dev/terraform && terraform plan
```

## References

- [CrewAI Enterprise Docs](https://enterprise-docs.crewai.com)
- [GKE Gateway API](https://cloud.google.com/kubernetes-engine/docs/concepts/gateway-api)
- [ArgoCD](https://argo-cd.readthedocs.io)

4. [docs/INDEX.md](docs/INDEX.md) - Full documentation index

**Daily Operations:**
- [docs/QUICK_REFERENCE.md](docs/QUICK_REFERENCE.md) - Commands and workflows
- [docs/CICD.md](docs/CICD.md) - CI/CD pipeline details

**Architecture & Design:**
- [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md) - Key patterns, decisions, and lessons learned
- [docs/CREW_NAMESPACE.md](docs/CREW_NAMESPACE.md) - Crew namespace architecture

**Contributing to Documentation:**

     1→---

     5→description: Documentation for CrewAI Enterprise Platform deployment on Google Cloud Platform
     6→---
     7→
     8→# CrewAI Enterprise Platform
     9→
    10→This section provides comprehensive documentation for deploying and operating the **CrewAI Enterprise Platform** on Google Cloud Platform (GCP) using GitOps practices.
    11→
    12→## Overview
    13→
    14→CrewAI Platform is an enterprise-grade framework for orchestrating role-playing AI agents that work together to accomplish complex tasks. It provides a collaborative environment where autonomous AI agents can coordinate their efforts to solve sophisticated problems.
    15→
    16→## Deployment Model
    17→
    18→CrewAI Enterprise is deployed using a **GitOps approach** managed through the [`kix-services-crewai`](https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai) repository:
    19→
    20→| Layer | Technology | Description |
    21→|-------|------------|-------------|
    22→| **Infrastructure** | Terraform | GCS buckets, Cloud SQL databases, Secret Manager, Kubernetes resources |
    23→| **Application Delivery** | ArgoCD | Multi-source applications pulling CrewAI Helm chart from OCI registry |
    24→| **CI/CD** | GitHub Actions | Automated validation, security scanning, approval gates, drift detection |
    25→| **Secrets** | GCP Secret Manager + K8s Secrets | Dual-layer secrets management with Workload Identity |
    26→
    27→## Documentation Structure
    28→
    29→| Document | Description |
    30→|----------|-------------|
    31→| [Infrastructure Setup](gcp-setup) | Terraform configuration for GCP resources in kix-services-crewai |
    32→| [Deployment Guide](install) | ArgoCD-based deployment via GitHub Actions workflows |
    33→| [Post-Installation Setup](post-install) | Initial configuration and access setup after deployment |
    34→| [Troubleshooting Guide](troubleshooting) | Solutions for common issues during deployment and operation |
    35→| [Third-Party Integrations](third-party) | Integration guides for WorkOS and other services |
    36→| [FAQ](faq) | Frequently asked questions about CrewAI Platform |
    37→
    38→## Environments
    39→
    40→| Environment | Domain | Branch | Auto-Sync |
    41→|-------------|--------|--------|-----------|
    42→| **Development** | `crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com` | `develop` | Yes |
    43→| **QA** | `crewai.qa.ix.konecta-digital.com` | `main` | Yes |
    44→| **Production** | `crewai.ix.konecta-digital.com` | `v*` tags | Manual |
    45→
    46→## Quick Start
    47→
    48→### Prerequisites
    49→
    50→Before deploying CrewAI, ensure you have:
    51→
    52→- Access to the [`kix-services-crewai`](https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai) repository
    53→- GitHub Actions permissions to run workflows
    54→- GCP IAM access to the target environment project
    55→- **CrewAI Registry** credentials from the enterprise portal (stored in GCP Secret Manager)
    56→
    57→### Deployment Workflow
    58→
    59→```
    60→PR to main/develop
    61→        ↓
    62→[Auto Validate on Changes]
    63→  - Terraform fmt, validate, Trivy scan
    64→        ↓
    65→Merge to branch
    66→        ↓
    67→[Manual Dispatch: validate-plan-apply]
    68→  - Choose environment (dev/qa/prod)
    69→  - Branch validation enforced
    70→  - Change window check (prod only)
    71→        ↓
    72→[Validate] → [Plan] → [Approval Gate] → [Apply]
    73→        ↓
    74→ArgoCD syncs application automatically
    75→```
    76→
    77→1. **Make Changes** — Update Terraform or Helm values in `kix-services-crewai`
    78→2. **Create PR** — Changes are auto-validated on PR
    79→3. **Merge** — Merge to `develop` (dev) or `main` (qa)
    80→4. **Deploy** — Run the `validate-plan-apply` workflow in GitHub Actions
    81→5. **Verify** — ArgoCD syncs the application; verify in ArgoCD dashboard
    82→
    83→## Key Features
    84→
    85→- **Multi-agent Orchestration** — Coordinate multiple AI agents working together
    86→- **Role-based Specialization** — Define agent roles and responsibilities
    87→- **Task Delegation** — Automatic workflow management and task assignment
    88→- **Enterprise Security** — SSO (WorkOS), encryption, IAM, and compliance controls
    89→- **GCP-native Integration** — Optimized for Google Cloud Platform
    90→- **GitOps Deployment** — Infrastructure and application changes through Git
    91→
    92→## Architecture Overview
    93→
    94→```
    95→┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    96→│                         kix-services-crewai Repository                       │
    97→├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    98→│                                                                               │
    99→│  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
   100→│  │  environments/{dev,qa,prod}/                                         │    │
   101→│  │  ├── terraform/          # Infrastructure as Code                   │    │
   102→│  │  │   ├── service-accounts.tf   # GCP SA + IAM bindings             │    │
   103→│  │  │   ├── kubernetes.tf         # Namespaces, quotas, policies      │    │
   104→│  │  │   ├── secrets.tf            # Secret Manager integration        │    │
   105→│  │  │   ├── storage.tf            # GCS buckets + HMAC keys           │    │
   106→│  │  │   └── database.tf           # Cloud SQL databases               │    │
   107→│  │  ├── argocd/apps/        # ArgoCD Application manifests            │    │
   108→│  │  │   └── application.yaml      # Multi-source ArgoCD app           │    │
   109→│  │  └── helm/crewai/        # Helm values for Gateway API, ESO        │    │
   110→│  │      └── values.yaml           # GKE L7 LB, health checks          │    │
   111→│  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
   112→│                                                                               │
   113→│  ┌─────────────────────┐    ┌─────────────────────┐                         │
   114→│  │  GitHub Actions     │    │  ArgoCD             │                         │
   115→│  │  ├─ validate        │───▶│  Multi-source app:  │                         │
   116→│  │  ├─ plan            │    │  1. OCI Helm chart  │                         │
   117→│  │  ├─ approval gate   │    │  2. Local gateway   │                         │
   118→│  │  └─ apply           │    │  3. Git values      │                         │
   119→│  └─────────────────────┘    └─────────────────────┘                         │
   120→│                                                                               │
   121→└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
   122→                                      │
   123→                                      ▼
   124→┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   125→│                           GCP Platform (per environment)                     │
   126→├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
   127→│                                                                               │
   128→│  ┌──────────────────┐  ┌──────────────────┐  ┌──────────────────┐          │
   129→│  │  GKE Cluster     │  │  Cloud SQL       │  │  Secret Manager  │          │
   130→│  │  ├─ crewai-{env} │  │  ├─ crewai_db    │  │  ├─ DB creds     │          │
   131→│  │  │   namespace   │  │  └─ crewai_oauth │  │  ├─ HMAC keys    │          │
   132→│  │  ├─ crewai-crews │  └──────────────────┘  │  └─ OAuth config │          │
   133→│  │  │   namespace   │                        └──────────────────┘          │
   134→│  │  └─ Gateway API  │  ┌──────────────────┐                                │
   135→│  │      L7 LB       │  │  GCS Buckets     │                                │
   136→│  └──────────────────┘  │  ├─ data bucket  │                                │
   137→│                        │  └─ logs bucket  │                                │
   138→│                        └──────────────────┘                                │
   139→│                                                                               │
   140→└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
   141→```
   142→
   143→## Repository Links
   144→
   145→| Repository | Purpose |
   146→|------------|---------|
   147→| [kix-services-crewai](https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai) | CrewAI deployment (Terraform + ArgoCD) |
   148→| [kix-platform-modules](https://github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules) | Shared Terraform modules |
   149→| [kix-platform-infrastructure](https://github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-infrastructure) | Platform GKE clusters and foundation |
   150→
   151→## Support
   152→
   153→- **Internal Support**: Platform Engineering team via Slack #platform-engineering
   154→- **CrewAI Enterprise Portal**: [https://enterprise.crewai.com](https://enterprise.crewai.com)
   155→- **Official Documentation**: [https://enterprise-docs.crewai.com](https://enterprise-docs.crewai.com)
   156→

```
     1→---
     2→title: Deployment Guide
     3→sidebar_label: Deployment Guide
     4→sidebar_position: 3
     5→description: Deploy CrewAI Platform using ArgoCD and GitHub Actions
     6→---
     7→
     8→# CrewAI Platform Deployment
     9→
    10→CrewAI is deployed using **ArgoCD** for GitOps-based application delivery, with **GitHub Actions** managing infrastructure changes. This guide covers the deployment workflow.
    11→
    12→## Deployment Architecture
    13→
    14→```
    15→┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    16→│                    kix-services-crewai                           │
    17→├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    18→│                                                                   │
    19→│  GitHub Actions                    ArgoCD                        │
    20→│  ┌──────────────────────┐         ┌──────────────────────┐      │
    21→│  │ validate-plan-apply  │         │ Multi-source App     │      │
    22→│  │ ├─ terraform fmt     │         │ ┌──────────────────┐ │      │
    23→│  │ ├─ terraform init    │         │ │ Source 1: OCI    │ │      │
    24→│  │ ├─ terraform plan    │────────▶│ │ CrewAI Helm      │ │      │
    25→│  │ ├─ approval gate     │         │ │ chart v0.3.13    │ │      │
    26→│  │ └─ terraform apply   │         │ └──────────────────┘ │      │
    27→│  └──────────────────────┘         │ ┌──────────────────┐ │      │
    28→│                                   │ │ Source 2: Git    │ │      │
    29→│                                   │ │ Gateway API +    │ │      │
    30→│                                   │ │ local Helm       │ │      │
    31→│                                   │ └──────────────────┘ │      │
    32→│                                   │ ┌──────────────────┐ │      │
    33→│                                   │ │ Source 3: Git    │ │      │
    34→│                                   │ │ values.yaml      │ │      │
    35→│                                   │ └──────────────────┘ │      │
    36→│                                   └──────────────────────┘      │
    37→│                                                                   │
    38→└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    39→```
    40→
    41→## Prerequisites
    42→
    43→Before deploying CrewAI:
    44→
    45→| Requirement | Description |
    46→|-------------|-------------|
    47→| **Repository Access** | Write access to [kix-services-crewai](https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai) |
    48→| **GitHub Actions** | Permissions to run workflows |
    49→| **GCP Access** | IAM roles for target environment project |
    50→| **ArgoCD Access** | Ability to view applications in ArgoCD dashboard |
    51→| **CrewAI Registry** | Credentials stored in GCP Secret Manager |
    52→
    53→## Environments
    54→
    55→| Environment | Branch | Auto-Sync | Change Window |
    56→|-------------|--------|-----------|---------------|
    57→| **dev** | `develop` | Yes | 24/7 |
    58→| **qa** | `main` | Yes | 24/7 |
    59→| **prod** | `v*` tags | Manual | Tue-Thu 20:00-23:00 UTC |
    60→
    61→### Branch-Environment Protection
    62→
    63→The CI/CD pipeline enforces strict branch-to-environment mapping:
    64→
    65→```
    66→Branch      → Allowed Environments
    67→develop     → dev only
    68→main        → dev, qa only
    69→v* tags     → prod only
    70→```
    71→
    72→## ArgoCD Application Configuration
    73→
    74→CrewAI uses ArgoCD's **multi-source** pattern, pulling from three sources:
    75→
    76→### Source 1: CrewAI OCI Helm Chart
    77→
    78→```yaml
    79→- repoURL: oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable
    80→  chart: crewai-platform
    81→  targetRevision: 0.3.13
    82→  helm:
    83→    valueFiles:
    84→      - $values/environments/dev/helm/crewai/values-gke.yaml
    85→```
    86→
    87→### Source 2: Local Gateway/ESO Helm Chart
    88→
    89→```yaml
    90→- repoURL: https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai
    91→  path: environments/dev/helm/crewai
    92→  targetRevision: develop
    93→```
    94→
    95→### Source 3: Values Reference
    96→
    97→```yaml
    98→- repoURL: https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai
    99→  targetRevision: develop
   100→  ref: values
   101→```
   102→
   103→## Deployment Workflow
   104→
   105→### Step 1: Make Changes
   106→
   107→Clone the repository and make your changes:
   108→
   109→```bash
   110→git clone git@github.com:konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai.git
   111→cd kix-services-crewai
   112→
   113→# Create a feature branch
   114→git checkout -b feature/your-change
   115→```
   116→
   117→**Common changes:**
   118→
   119→| Change Type | File Location |
   120→|-------------|---------------|
   121→| Helm values | `environments/{env}/helm/crewai/values.yaml` |
   122→| ArgoCD app config | `environments/{env}/argocd/apps/application.yaml` |
   123→| Infrastructure | `environments/{env}/terraform/*.tf` |
   124→| CrewAI version | `application.yaml` → `targetRevision` |
   125→
   126→### Step 2: Create Pull Request
   127→
   128→```bash
   129→git add .
   130→git commit -m "feat: description of change"
   131→git push origin feature/your-change
   132→```
   133→
   134→GitHub Actions automatically validates on PR:
   135→- Terraform format check
   136→- Terraform validate
   137→- Trivy security scan
   138→- SARIF upload to Code Scanning
   139→
   140→### Step 3: Merge to Target Branch
   141→
   142→After PR approval:
   143→- Merge to `develop` for dev deployments
   144→- Merge to `main` for qa deployments
   145→- Create `v*` tag for prod deployments
   146→
   147→### Step 4: Run Deployment Workflow
   148→
   149→1. Go to **Actions** → **validate-plan-apply**
   150→2. Click **Run workflow**
   151→3. Select:
   152→   - **Environment**: dev, qa, or prod
   153→   - **Branch/Tag**: matches your target
   154→4. Click **Run workflow**
   155→
   156→### Step 5: Review and Approve
   157→
   158→The workflow runs in stages:
   159→
   160→```
   161→[Validate] ──▶ [Plan] ──▶ [Approval Gate] ──▶ [Apply]
   162→```
   163→
   164→1. **Validate**: Format check, init, validate, Trivy scan
   165→2. **Plan**: Generate Terraform plan with resource counts
   166→3. **Approval Gate**: GitHub Environment protection (requires approval)
   167→4. **Apply**: Execute Terraform apply
   168→
   169→### Step 6: ArgoCD Sync
   170→
   171→After Terraform apply:
   172→- **dev/qa**: ArgoCD auto-syncs within 3 minutes
   173→- **prod**: Manual sync required in ArgoCD dashboard
   174→
   175→**Verify in ArgoCD:**
   176→
   177→```bash
   178→# Check application status
   179→argocd app get crewai-dev
   180→
   181→# Force sync if needed
   182→argocd app sync crewai-dev
   183→```
   184→
   185→## Helm Values Configuration
   186→
   187→### Key Configuration Files
   188→
   189→| File | Purpose |
   190→|------|---------|
   191→| `values.yaml` | Gateway API, health checks, GCP backend policies |
   192→| `values-gke.yaml` | GKE-specific: replicas, resources, service accounts |
   193→
   194→### Example values-gke.yaml
   195→
   196→```yaml
   197→# Application Configuration
   198→web:
   199→  replicaCount: 1  # dev: 1, qa: 2, prod: 3
   200→  resources:
   201→    requests:
   202→      cpu: "1000m"
   203→      memory: "2Gi"
   204→    limits:
   205→      cpu: "2000m"
   206→      memory: "4Gi"
   207→
   208→worker:
   209→  replicaCount: 1
   210→  resources:
   211→    requests:
   212→      cpu: "1000m"
   213→      memory: "2Gi"
   214→    limits:
   215→      cpu: "2000m"
   216→      memory: "4Gi"
   217→
   218→# Service Account (Terraform-managed)
   219→serviceAccount:
   220→  create: false
   221→  name: crewai-platform-sa
   222→
   223→# External Database (Terraform-managed)
   224→postgres:
   225→  enabled: false
   226→
   227→envVars:
   228→  DB_HOST: "10.29.0.2"  # Private IP from Terraform
   229→  DB_PORT: "5432"
   230→  DB_NAME: "crewai_plus_production"
   231→
   232→# External Storage (Terraform-managed)
   233→minio:
   234→  enabled: false
   235→
   236→envVars:
   237→  STORAGE_SERVICE: "amazon"
   238→  AWS_ENDPOINT: "https://storage.googleapis.com"
   239→  AWS_REGION: "europe-west1"
   240→  AWS_BUCKET: "kd-ix-eur-dev-platform-crewai-data"
   241→  LOGS_BUCKET: "kd-ix-eur-dev-platform-crewai-logs"
   242→
   243→# Secrets (from K8s secrets created by Terraform)
   244→existingSecrets:
   245→  credentials: crewai-credentials
   246→  databaseConfig: crewai-database-config
   247→```
   248→
   249→:::warning Important
   250→- Set `serviceAccount.create: false` — Terraform creates the SA
   251→- Set `postgres.enabled: false` — Use external Cloud SQL
   252→- Set `minio.enabled: false` — Use external GCS buckets
   253→:::
   254→
   255→## Upgrading CrewAI Version
   256→
   257→To upgrade the CrewAI Helm chart version:
   258→
   259→### 1. Update Application Manifest
   260→
   261→Edit `environments/{env}/argocd/apps/application.yaml`:
   262→
   263→```yaml
   264→sources:
   265→  - repoURL: oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable
   266→    chart: crewai-platform
   267→    targetRevision: 0.3.14  # ← Update version
   268→```
   269→
   270→### 2. Create PR and Deploy
   271→
   272→```bash
   273→git checkout -b feat/upgrade-crewai-0.3.14
   274→git add environments/dev/argocd/apps/application.yaml
   275→git commit -m "feat: upgrade CrewAI to 0.3.14 in dev"
   276→git push origin feat/upgrade-crewai-0.3.14
   277→```
   278→
   279→### 3. Validate in Dev First
   280→
   281→Always upgrade dev → qa → prod in sequence.
   282→
   283→## Monitoring Deployment
   284→
   285→### ArgoCD Dashboard
   286→
   287→Access the ArgoCD UI to monitor:
   288→- Sync status
   289→- Resource health
   290→- Pod status
   291→- Events
   292→
   293→```bash
   294→# Port-forward to ArgoCD (if needed)
   295→kubectl port-forward svc/argocd-server -n argocd 8080:443
   296→
   297→# Or use CLI
   298→argocd app get crewai-dev
   299→argocd app logs crewai-dev
   300→```
   301→
   302→### Pod Status
   303→
   304→```bash
   305→# Check pods
   306→kubectl get pods -n crewai
   307→
   308→# Check logs
   309→kubectl logs -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai
   310→
   311→# Describe pod for events
   312→kubectl describe pod <pod-name> -n crewai
   313→```
   314→
   315→### GitHub Actions
   316→
   317→Monitor workflow runs:
   318→1. Go to **Actions** tab
   319→2. Select **validate-plan-apply** workflow
   320→3. View job logs for each stage
   321→
   322→## Rollback Procedures
   323→
   324→### Option 1: Git Revert (Recommended)
   325→
   326→```bash
   327→# Revert the problematic commit
   328→git revert HEAD
   329→git push origin develop
   330→
   331→# Run validate-plan-apply workflow
   332→# ArgoCD will sync the reverted state
   333→```
   334→
   335→### Option 2: ArgoCD Rollback
   336→
   337→```bash
   338→# List revision history
   339→argocd app history crewai-dev
   340→
   341→# Rollback to previous revision
   342→argocd app rollback crewai-dev <revision-number>
   343→```
   344→
   345→### Option 3: Kubectl Rollback (Emergency)
   346→
   347→```bash
   348→# Rollback deployment directly (bypasses GitOps)
   349→kubectl rollout undo deployment/crewai-web -n crewai
   350→
   351→# Note: This will be overwritten on next ArgoCD sync
   352→```
   353→
   354→## Nightly Quality Checks
   355→
   356→The `nightly-quality.yml` workflow runs at 02:00 UTC daily:
   357→
   358→- Validates `develop` → `dev` configuration
   359→- Validates `main` → `qa` configuration
   360→- Runs drift detection for all environments
   361→- Creates GitHub issues if drift is found
   362→
   363→## Troubleshooting
   364→
   365→### ArgoCD Sync Failed
   366→
   367→```bash
   368→# Check sync status
   369→argocd app get crewai-dev
   370→
   371→# View sync errors
   372→argocd app sync crewai-dev --dry-run
   373→
   374→# Force hard refresh
   375→argocd app get crewai-dev --hard-refresh
   376→```
   377→
   378→### Pods Not Starting
   379→
   380→```bash
   381→# Check events
   382→kubectl get events -n crewai --sort-by='.lastTimestamp'
   383→
   384→# Check pod status
   385→kubectl describe pod -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai
   386→```
   387→
   388→### ImagePullBackOff
   389→
   390→Registry authentication issue:
   391→
   392→```bash
   393→# Check the registry secret
   394→kubectl get secret crewai-registry -n crewai -o yaml
   395→
   396→# Verify secret exists and is correct in Terraform
   397→```
   398→
   399→### Database Connection Failed
   400→
   401→```bash
   402→# Check database config secret
   403→kubectl get secret crewai-database-config -n crewai -o yaml
   404→
   405→# Test connectivity from pod
   406→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai -- \
   407→  psql -h $DB_HOST -U $DB_USER -d $DB_NAME
   408→```
   409→
   410→## Next Steps
   411→
   412→After successful deployment:
   413→
   414→1. **[Post-Installation Setup](post-install)** — Initialize organization and configure admin access
   415→2. **Verify Access** — Test the application URL
   416→3. **Configure Monitoring** — Set up alerts in Grafana
   417→
   418→## Additional Resources
   419→
   420→- [kix-services-crewai Repository](https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai)
   421→- [ArgoCD Documentation](https://argo-cd.readthedocs.io/)
   422→- [CrewAI Enterprise Portal](https://enterprise.crewai.com)
   423→
```
