```
     1→---
     2→title: Infrastructure Setup
     3→sidebar_label: Infrastructure Setup
     4→sidebar_position: 2
     5→description: Terraform infrastructure configuration for CrewAI in kix-services-crewai
     6→---
     7→
     8→# CrewAI Infrastructure Setup
     9→
    10→CrewAI infrastructure is managed through Terraform in the [`kix-services-crewai`](https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai) repository. This guide explains the infrastructure components and how they're configured.
    11→
    12→## Repository Structure
    13→
    14→```
    15→kix-services-crewai/
    16→├── environments/
    17→│   ├── dev/
    18→│   │   ├── terraform/           # Infrastructure as Code
    19→│   │   │   ├── main.tf              # Providers & data sources
    20→│   │   │   ├── service-accounts.tf  # GCP SA & IAM bindings
    21→│   │   │   ├── kubernetes.tf        # K8s namespaces, quotas, policies
    22→│   │   │   ├── secrets.tf           # Secret Manager integration
    23→│   │   │   ├── storage.tf           # GCS buckets & HMAC keys
    24→│   │   │   ├── database.tf          # Cloud SQL configuration
    25→│   │   │   ├── artifact-registry.tf # Container image registries
    26→│   │   │   ├── gateway-certificate.tf # TLS certificates
    27→│   │   │   └── backend.tf           # Remote state in GCS
    28→│   │   ├── argocd/apps/
    29→│   │   │   └── application.yaml # ArgoCD Application manifest
    30→│   │   └── helm/crewai/
    31→│   │       ├── values.yaml      # Gateway API & health check config
    32→│   │       └── values-gke.yaml  # GKE-specific configuration
    33→│   ├── qa/                      # Same structure as dev
    34→│   └── prod/                    # Same structure as dev
    35→└── .github/workflows/           # CI/CD pipelines
    36→```
    37→
    38→## Infrastructure Components
    39→
    40→### What Terraform Creates
    41→
    42→| Component | Resource | Description |
    43→|-----------|----------|-------------|
    44→| **Service Accounts** | `google_service_account` | GCP SA for CrewAI workloads with Workload Identity |
    45→| **IAM Bindings** | `google_*_iam_member` | Cloud SQL, GCS, Secret Manager, Workload Identity |
    46→| **Namespaces** | `kubernetes_namespace` | `crewai` (dev), `crewai-qa`, `crewai-prod` |
    47→| **Network Policies** | `kubernetes_network_policy` | Default-deny with GCP health check IPs allowed |
    48→| **Resource Quotas** | `kubernetes_resource_quota` | Per-namespace CPU/memory limits |
    49→| **GCS Buckets** | `google_storage_bucket` | Data bucket + logs bucket with versioning |
    50→| **HMAC Keys** | `google_storage_hmac_key` | S3-compatible access for CrewAI |
    51→| **Cloud SQL DB** | `google_sql_database` | Database on platform Cloud SQL instance |
    52→| **Cloud SQL User** | `google_sql_user` | Service account-based IAM authentication |
    53→| **Secrets** | `google_secret_manager_secret` | DB credentials, HMAC keys, OAuth config |
    54→| **K8s Secrets** | `kubernetes_secret` | Populated from Secret Manager for pod consumption |
    55→| **TLS Certificates** | `google_certificate_manager_*` | Managed SSL certificates for Gateway API |
    56→
    57→### Remote Modules Used
    58→
    59→Terraform composes modules from [`kix-platform-modules`](https://github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules):
    60→
    61→```hcl
    62→# GCS bucket with versioning and lifecycle policies
    63→module "gcs_data" {
    64→  source = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/data-services/gcs?ref=main"
    65→
    66→  project_id  = var.gke_project_id
    67→  bucket_name = "${var.gke_project_id}-crewai-data"
    68→  location    = var.region
    69→  # ...
    70→}
    71→
    72→# Single-secret-per-call pattern for Secret Manager
    73→module "secret_db_password" {
    74→  source = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/secret-manager?ref=main"
    75→
    76→  project_id  = var.gke_project_id
    77→  secret_id   = "crewai-db-password"
    78→  secret_data = random_password.db.result
    79→}
    80→
    81→# Note: Namespaces are created by ArgoCD (CreateNamespace=true)
    82→# Terraform creates resources within the namespace
    83→```
    84→
    85→## Environment Configuration
    86→
    87→### Development
    88→
    89→| Setting | Value |
    90→|---------|-------|
    91→| **GKE Cluster** | `kd-ix-eur-dev-gke` |
    92→| **Domain** | `crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com` |
    93→| **Namespace** | `crewai` |
    94→| **Replicas** | 1 web, 1 worker |
    95→| **Resources** | 1000m/2Gi requests, 2000m/4Gi limits |
    96→| **State Bucket** | `kd-tfstate-dev` |
    97→| **State Prefix** | `services/crewai/resources` |
    98→
    99→### QA
   100→
   101→| Setting | Value |
   102→|---------|-------|
   103→| **GKE Cluster** | `kd-ix-eur-qa-gke` |
   104→| **Domain** | `crewai.qa.ix.konecta-digital.com` |
   105→| **Namespace** | `crewai-qa` |
   106→| **Replicas** | 2 web, 2 worker |
   107→| **Resources** | 1500m/4Gi requests, 3000m/8Gi limits |
   108→| **State Bucket** | `kd-tfstate-qa` |
   109→| **State Prefix** | `services/crewai/resources` |
   110→
   111→### Production
   112→
   113→| Setting | Value |
   114→|---------|-------|
   115→| **GKE Cluster** | `kd-ix-eur-prod-gke` |
   116→| **Domain** | `crewai.ix.konecta-digital.com` |
   117→| **Namespace** | `crewai-prod` |
   118→| **Replicas** | 3+ web, 3+ worker (HPA max 10) |
   119→| **Resources** | 2000m/6Gi requests, 4000m/12Gi limits |
   120→| **PDB** | Min 2 available |
   121→| **State Bucket** | `kd-tfstate-prod` |
   122→| **State Prefix** | `services/crewai/resources` |
   123→
   124→## Secrets Management
   125→
   126→CrewAI uses a dual-layer secrets architecture:
   127→
   128→### Layer 1: GCP Secret Manager (Terraform-managed)
   129→
   130→Terraform creates secrets in GCP Secret Manager:
   131→
   132→```hcl
   133→resource "google_secret_manager_secret" "db_password" {
   134→  secret_id = "crewai-db-password-${var.environment}"
   135→
   136→  replication {
   137→    auto {}
   138→  }
   139→}
   140→
   141→resource "google_secret_manager_secret_version" "db_password" {
   142→  secret=[REDACTED].db_password.id
   143→  secret_data = random_password.db.result
   144→}
   145→```
   146→
   147→### Layer 2: Kubernetes Secrets (Terraform-created)
   148→
   149→Terraform creates K8s secrets populated from Secret Manager:
   150→
   151→```hcl
   152→resource "kubernetes_secret" "crewai_credentials" {
   153→  metadata {
   154→    name      = "crewai-credentials"
   155→    namespace = local.namespace  # crewai (dev), crewai-qa, crewai-prod
   156→  }
   157→
   158→  data = {
   159→    AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID     = google_storage_hmac_key.crewai.access_id
   160→    AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY = google_storage_hmac_key.crewai.secret
   161→    DB_PASSWORD           = random_password.db.result
   162→  }
   163→}
   164→```
   165→
   166→### Secrets Created
   167→
   168→| Secret Name | Contents | Used By |
   169→|-------------|----------|---------|
   170→| `crewai-credentials` | HMAC keys, DB password | Web, Worker pods |
   171→| `crewai-database-config` | DB host, port, name, user | Web, Worker pods |
   172→| `crewai-registry` | CrewAI OCI registry auth | BuildKit, image pulls |
   173→
   174→## Kubernetes Resources
   175→
   176→### Namespaces
   177→
   178→ArgoCD creates namespaces automatically (via `CreateNamespace=true` sync option):
   179→
   180→| Environment | Namespace | Purpose |
   181→|-------------|-----------|---------|
   182→| **dev** | `crewai` | Platform services (web, worker, scheduler) |
   183→| **qa** | `crewai-qa` | Platform services |
   184→| **prod** | `crewai-prod` | Platform services |
   185→
   186→### Network Policies
   187→
   188→Default-deny-ingress with specific allowances:
   189→
   190→```yaml
   191→# GCP health check IPs (mandatory for load balancer)
   192→ingress:
   193→  - from:
   194→      - ipBlock:
   195→          cidr: 35.191.0.0/16
   196→      - ipBlock:
   197→          cidr: 130.211.0.0/22
   198→    ports:
   199→      - port: 8080
   200→        protocol: TCP
   201→```
   202→
   203→### Resource Quotas
   204→
   205→Per-namespace limits prevent resource exhaustion:
   206→
   207→```yaml
   208→spec:
   209→  hard:
   210→    requests.cpu: "10"
   211→    requests.memory: "20Gi"
   212→    limits.cpu: "20"
   213→    limits.memory: "40Gi"
   214→    pods: "50"
   215→```
   216→
   217→### Pod Security Standards
   218→
   219→CrewAI uses `baseline` Pod Security Standards (not `restricted`) to support BuildKit's Linux capabilities requirements.
   220→
   221→## Gateway API Configuration
   222→
   223→CrewAI uses GKE Gateway API (not Ingress) for traffic management:
   224→
   225→```yaml
   226→# Gateway (GKE L7 Global External Load Balancer)
   227→apiVersion: gateway.networking.k8s.io/v1
   228→kind: Gateway
   229→metadata:
   230→  name: crewai-gateway
   231→spec:
   232→  gatewayClassName: gke-l7-global-external-managed
   233→  listeners:
   234→    - name: https
   235→      protocol: HTTPS
   236→      port: 443
   237→      tls:
   238→        mode: Terminate
   239→        certificateRefs:
   240→          - name: crewai-certificate
   241→
   242→# HTTPRoute for traffic routing
   243→apiVersion: gateway.networking.k8s.io/v1
   244→kind: HTTPRoute
   245→metadata:
   246→  name: crewai-route
   247→spec:
   248→  parentRefs:
   249→    - name: crewai-gateway
   250→  rules:
   251→    - backendRefs:
   252→        - name: crewai-web
   253→          port: 80
   254→```
   255→
   256→## Making Infrastructure Changes
   257→
   258→### 1. Clone the Repository
   259→
   260→```bash
   261→git clone git@github.com:konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai.git
   262→cd kix-services-crewai
   263→```
   264→
   265→### 2. Make Changes
   266→
   267→Edit the appropriate files in `environments/{env}/terraform/`:
   268→
   269→```bash
   270→# Example: Update resource quotas for dev
   271→vim environments/dev/terraform/kubernetes.tf
   272→```
   273→
   274→### 3. Create a Pull Request
   275→
   276→```bash
   277→git checkout -b feature/update-quotas
   278→git add .
   279→git commit -m "feat: increase dev resource quotas"
   280→git push origin feature/update-quotas
   281→```
   282→
   283→### 4. PR Validation
   284→
   285→GitHub Actions automatically runs on PR:
   286→- `terraform fmt` check
   287→- `terraform init` (no backend)
   288→- `terraform validate`
   289→- Trivy security scan
   290→
   291→### 5. Merge and Deploy
   292→
   293→After PR approval:
   294→1. Merge to `develop` (for dev) or `main` (for qa)
   295→2. Go to **Actions** → **validate-plan-apply**
   296→3. Click **Run workflow**
   297→4. Select environment and branch
   298→5. Review plan output
   299→6. Approve deployment at the approval gate
   300→
   301→## Terraform State Management
   302→
   303→| Environment | State Bucket | Prefix |
   304→|-------------|--------------|--------|
   305→| dev | `kd-tfstate-dev` | `services/crewai/resources` |
   306→| qa | `kd-tfstate-qa` | `services/crewai/resources` |
   307→| prod | `kd-tfstate-prod` | `services/crewai/resources` |
   308→
   309→```hcl
   310→# backend.tf
   311→terraform {
   312→  backend "gcs" {
   313→    bucket = "kd-tfstate-dev"  # Environment-specific
   314→    prefix = "services/crewai/resources"
   315→  }
   316→}
   317→```
   318→
   319→## Required Providers
   320→
   321→```hcl
   322→terraform {
   323→  required_version = ">= 1.5.0"
   324→
   325→  required_providers {
   326→    google = {
   327→      source  = "hashicorp/google"
   328→      version = "~> 5.0"
   329→    }
   330→    kubernetes = {
   331→      source  = "hashicorp/kubernetes"
   332→      version = "~> 2.23"
   333→    }
   334→    random = {
   335→      source  = "hashicorp/random"
   336→      version = "~> 3.5"
   337→    }
   338→  }
   339→}
   340→```
   341→
   342→## Troubleshooting
   343→
   344→### Terraform Plan Fails
   345→
   346→```bash
   347→# Check formatting
   348→terraform fmt -check -recursive
   349→
   350→# Validate configuration
   351→terraform validate
   352→
   353→# Check for module version issues
   354→terraform init -upgrade
   355→```
   356→
   357→### Secret Manager Access Denied
   358→
   359→Verify the GitHub Actions service account has permissions:
   360→
   361→```bash
   362→gcloud secrets get-iam-policy crewai-db-password-dev \
   363→  --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-platform
   364→```
   365→
   366→### Namespace Already Exists
   367→
   368→If Terraform tries to create a namespace that exists:
   369→
   370→```bash
   371→# Import existing resource
   372→terraform import 'module.gke_config.kubernetes_namespace.main' crewai
   373→```
   374→
   375→## Additional Resources
   376→
   377→- [kix-services-crewai Repository](https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai)
   378→- [kix-platform-modules Repository](https://github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules)
   379→- [Terraform GCP Provider Docs](https://registry.terraform.io/providers/hashicorp/google/latest/docs)
   380→- [GKE Gateway API Docs](https://cloud.google.com/kubernetes-engine/docs/concepts/gateway-api)
   381→
```

     1→---
     2→title: Post-Installation Setup
     3→sidebar_label: Post-Installation Setup
     4→sidebar_position: 4
     5→description: Post installation steps for CrewAI Platform
     6→---
     7→
     8→# Post-Installation Setup for CrewAI Platform
     9→
    10→## Overview
    11→
    12→After successfully deploying the CrewAI Platform via ArgoCD, these initial setup steps are necessary to configure the core system, set up permissions, and prepare the environment for use.
    13→
    14→:::warning Important
    15→Complete these setup steps immediately after deployment. Skipping any steps may result in missing functionality or access issues.
    16→:::
    17→
    18→## Environment Namespaces
    19→
    20→Commands in this guide use environment-specific namespaces:
    21→
    22→| Environment | Namespace | Domain |
    23→|-------------|-----------|--------|
    24→| **dev** | `crewai` | `crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com` |
    25→| **qa** | `crewai-qa` | `crewai.qa.ix.konecta-digital.com` |
    26→| **prod** | `crewai-prod` | `crewai.ix.konecta-digital.com` |
    27→
    28→Replace `crewai` with the appropriate namespace for your environment.
    29→
    30→## Initial Setup Tasks
    31→
    32→These tasks must be run immediately after installation to configure the internal organization and administration access.
    33→
    34→### Step 1: Initialize Internal Organization
    35→
    36→Run the following command to initialize the required internal organization, typically named "Default."
    37→
    38→```bash
    39→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai -- \
    40→  rake studio:install_internal_organization
    41→```
    42→
    43→### Step 2: Set Up Default Permissions
    44→
    45→Configure the default permission set and assign the initial owner role. **Replace `admin@company.com` with your desired administrator's email address.**
    46→
    47→```bash
    48→# Replace with your admin email
    49→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai -- \
    50→  rake factory:setup_permissions_defaults 'factory:add_owner[2,admin@company.com]'
    51→```
    52→
    53→### Step 3: Fix Missing 'Operate' Tab (Troubleshoot)
    54→
    55→If the **"Operate"** tab is missing in the Web UI, you may need to manually assign the `Owner` role to your user via the Rails console.
    56→
    57→**1. Shell into the `crewai-web` pod:**
    58→```bash
    59→kubectl exec --stdin --tty deploy/crewai-web -n crewai -- /bin/bash
    60→```
    61→
    62→**2. Start the Rails console:**
    63→```bash
    64→bin/rails c
    65→```
    66→
    67→**3. Define the role assignment function (paste this code block):**
    68→```ruby
    69→def assign_owner_role(user_email)
    70→  user = User.find_by(email: user_email)
    71→  organization = Organization.find_by(name: "Default")
    72→  owner_role = organization.roles.find_by(name: "Owner")
    73→  membership = organization.memberships.find_by(user_id: user.id)
    74→  membership.update!(role_id: owner_role.id)
    75→end
    76→```
    77→
    78→**4. Run the assignment function (replace with your user email):**
    79→```ruby
    80→assign_owner_role("nathan.booth@konecta.com")
    81→```
    82→
    83→**5. Exit the Rails shell (Ctrl+D) and refresh your browser.**
    84→
    85→## Enable Studio V2 (Optional)
    86→
    87→:::info What is Studio V2?
    88→Studio V2 is the next-generation interface and agent capability for CrewAI, providing enhanced features and improved user experience.
    89→:::
    90→
    91→### Step 1: Check LLM Connection and Delete Old Crews
    92→
    93→Ensure you have an LLM connection named **`studio-v2`** configured. Then, shell into the web-service pod and delete any old Studio crew deployments.
    94→
    95→**1. Shell into the web-service pod and run:**
    96→```bash
    97→kubectl exec --stdin --tty deploy/crewai-web -n crewai -- /bin/bash
    98→bin/rails c
    99→```
   100→
   101→**2. Paste and run the following commands in the Rails console:**
   102→```ruby
   103→Provisioner::DeleteProvisionedCrewJob.perform_later(Deployment.find_by_slug(Studio::Install::Const::STUDIO_V2_AGENT_SLUG).id)
   104→Provisioner::DeleteProvisionedCrewJob.perform_later(Deployment.find_by_slug(Studio::Install::Const::STUDIO_RUNNER_AGENT_SLUG).id)
   105→```
   106→
   107→### Step 2: Install the Studio Agent
   108→
   109→Exit the Rails shell (Ctrl+D) and install the core Studio Agent. This process can take up to **~10 minutes**.
   110→
   111→```bash
   112→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai -- \
   113→  bash -c "INTERNAL_ORGANIZATION_NAME=CrewAI ORGANIZATION_ID=1 rake studio:agent:install"
   114→```
   115→
   116→### Step 3: Verify Agent Status and Sync Tools
   117→
   118→Continuously check the agent status until it reports **"Crew is Online"**. Then, sync and re-index the available tools.
   119→
   120→```bash
   121→# Check status - wait until it says "Crew is Online"
   122→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai -- rake studio:agent:status
   123→
   124→# Sync and re-index tools
   125→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai -- rake studio:tools:sync_crewai_tools
   126→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai -- rake studio:tools:reindex_tools
   127→```
   128→
   129→### Step 4: Install the Studio Runner
   130→
   131→Install the Studio Runner agent, which handles the execution environment. Wait for 10 minutes for the deployment process to fully complete before testing.
   132→
   133→```bash
   134→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai -- \
   135→  bash -c "INTERNAL_ORGANIZATION_NAME=CrewAI rake studio:runner:install"
   136→```
   137→
   138→## Accessing the Application
   139→
   140→### Production URLs (Gateway API)
   141→
   142→Access CrewAI using the configured domain:
   143→
   144→| Environment | URL |
   145→|-------------|-----|
   146→| **dev** | https://crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com |
   147→| **qa** | https://crewai.qa.ix.konecta-digital.com |
   148→| **prod** | https://crewai.ix.konecta-digital.com |
   149→
   150→### Development Access (Port Forwarding)
   151→
   152→For local testing without going through the load balancer:
   153→
   154→```bash
   155→# Forward web service port
   156→kubectl port-forward svc/crewai-web -n crewai 2603:80
   157→
   158→# Access in browser
   159→open http://localhost:2603
   160→```
   161→
   162→### Check Gateway Status
   163→
   164→Verify the Gateway API is configured correctly:
   165→
   166→```bash
   167→# Check Gateway status
   168→kubectl get gateway -n crewai
   169→
   170→# Check HTTPRoute
   171→kubectl get httproute -n crewai
   172→
   173→# Check backend health
   174→kubectl get gcpbackendpolicy -n crewai
   175→```
   176→
   177→## Verification Steps
   178→
   179→After completing all setup tasks, verify the platform's readiness:
   180→
   181→1. **Access the Web UI** at the environment URL
   182→2. **Log in** with your configured authentication provider (WorkOS)
   183→3. **Verify admin access** by confirming the presence of the "Operate" tab
   184→4. **Test basic functionality** by creating a test crew or project
   185→5. **Check pods are healthy:**
   186→   ```bash
   187→   kubectl get pods -n crewai
   188→   ```
   189→6. **Check background workers** for successful job processing:
   190→   ```bash
   191→   kubectl logs -l app=crewai-worker -n crewai --tail=50
   192→   ```
   193→
   194→## Verification Checklist
   195→
   196→```markdown
   197→## Post-Installation Verification
   198→
   199→### Core Setup
   200→- [ ] Internal organization initialized
   201→- [ ] Default permissions configured
   202→- [ ] Admin user has Owner role
   203→- [ ] "Operate" tab visible in Web UI
   204→
   205→### Application Access
   206→- [ ] Web UI accessible via domain URL
   207→- [ ] Authentication working (WorkOS login)
   208→- [ ] Can create test project/crew
   209→
   210→### Pods & Services
   211→- [ ] All pods in Running state
   212→- [ ] No CrashLoopBackOff errors
   213→- [ ] Worker pods processing jobs
   214→- [ ] Gateway API routing traffic correctly
   215→
   216→### Studio V2 (if enabled)
   217→- [ ] LLM connection "studio-v2" configured
   218→- [ ] Studio Agent status: "Crew is Online"
   219→- [ ] Tools synced and indexed
   220→- [ ] Studio Runner installed
   221→```
   222→
   223→:::success Congratulations!
   224→Your CrewAI platform is now fully installed and configured. Start creating and deploying crews!
   225→
   226→For more information, visit the [Troubleshooting Guide](troubleshooting) or the [FAQ](faq).
   227→:::
   228→
```

     1→---
     2→title: Troubleshooting Guide
     3→sidebar_label: Troubleshooting
     4→sidebar_position: 5
     5→description: Solutions for common issues encountered during CrewAI deployment and operation
     6→---
     7→
     8→# Troubleshooting Guide
     9→
    10→This guide provides solutions for common issues encountered during CrewAI deployment and operation.
    11→
    12→:::tip
    13→Use the debug commands reference at the bottom of this page for quick troubleshooting commands. Remember to use the correct namespace: `crewai` (dev), `crewai-qa`, or `crewai-prod`.
    14→:::
    15→
    16→## ArgoCD & GitOps Issues
    17→
    18→### ArgoCD Sync Failed
    19→
    20→#### Issue: Application stuck in 'OutOfSync' or 'Degraded' state
    21→
    22→**Cause**: Resource conflict, invalid manifest, or dependency issue
    23→
    24→**Solution**:
    25→```bash
    26→# Check sync status and errors
    27→argocd app get crewai-dev
    28→
    29→# View detailed sync errors
    30→argocd app sync crewai-dev --dry-run
    31→
    32→# Force hard refresh from Git
    33→argocd app get crewai-dev --hard-refresh
    34→
    35→# Check ArgoCD logs
    36→kubectl logs -l app.kubernetes.io/name=argocd-application-controller -n argocd
    37→```
    38→
    39→#### Issue: 'ComparisonError' in ArgoCD
    40→
    41→**Cause**: Resource managed by both ArgoCD and another controller (e.g., Terraform)
    42→
    43→**Solution**:
    44→- Check `application.yaml` for proper `ignoreDifferences` configuration
    45→- Verify the resource isn't being modified by Terraform and ArgoCD simultaneously
    46→- Add ignored fields:
    47→  ```yaml
    48→  spec:
    49→    ignoreDifferences:
    50→      - group: ""
    51→        kind: Secret
    52→        jsonPointers:
    53→          - /data
    54→  ```
    55→
    56→### Terraform Issues
    57→
    58→#### Issue: 'Resource already exists' error
    59→
    60→**Cause**: Resource created outside of Terraform or state mismatch
    61→
    62→**Solution**:
    63→```bash
    64→# Import existing resource
    65→terraform import 'kubernetes_namespace.crewai' crewai
    66→
    67→# Or for module resources
    68→terraform import 'module.gke_config.kubernetes_namespace.main' crewai
    69→```
    70→
    71→#### Issue: 'Secret Manager access denied'
    72→
    73→**Cause**: GitHub Actions service account lacks permissions
    74→
    75→**Solution**:
    76→```bash
    77→# Verify IAM policy
    78→gcloud secrets get-iam-policy crewai-db-password-dev \
    79→  --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-platform
    80→
    81→# Add missing binding
    82→gcloud secrets add-iam-policy-binding crewai-db-password-dev \
    83→  --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-platform \
    84→  --role=roles/secretmanager.secretAccessor \
    85→  --member="serviceAccount:github-actions@kd-ix-eur-dev-platform.iam.gserviceaccount.com"
    86→```
    87→
    88→## Deployment Issues
    89→
    90→### ImagePullBackOff
    91→
    92→#### Issue: Pods stuck in 'ImagePullBackOff' state
    93→
    94→**Cause**: Registry authentication failure or image doesn't exist
    95→
    96→**Solution**:
    97→```bash
    98→# Check the registry secret exists
    99→kubectl get secret crewai-registry -n crewai
   100→
   101→# Verify secret has correct data
   102→kubectl get secret crewai-registry -n crewai -o jsonpath='{.data.\.dockerconfigjson}' | base64 -d
   103→
   104→# Check pod events for specific error
   105→kubectl describe pod -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai
   106→
   107→# Verify image exists in CrewAI registry (requires auth)
   108→```
   109→
   110→**Common causes**:
   111→- `crewai-registry` secret not created by Terraform
   112→- CrewAI registry credentials expired
   113→- Wrong image tag in ArgoCD application
   114→
   115→### Database Connection Issues
   116→
   117→#### Issue: 'Unable to connect to database' errors
   118→
   119→**Cause**: Database credentials incorrect or database unreachable
   120→
   121→**Solution**:
   122→```bash
   123→# Check database config secret
   124→kubectl get secret crewai-database-config -n crewai -o yaml
   125→
   126→# Verify DB_HOST is correct (should be Cloud SQL private IP)
   127→kubectl get secret crewai-database-config -n crewai \
   128→  -o jsonpath='{.data.DB_HOST}' | base64 -d
   129→
   130→# Test connection from pod
   131→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai -- \
   132→  psql -h $DB_HOST -U $DB_USER -d $DB_NAME
   133→
   134→# Check Cloud SQL instance is running
   135→gcloud sql instances list --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-platform
   136→```
   137→
   138→#### Issue: 'crewai-database-config' ConfigMap not found
   139→
   140→**Cause**: Terraform hasn't created the Kubernetes secret
   141→
   142→**Solution**:
   143→```bash
   144→# Verify secret exists
   145→kubectl get secret crewai-database-config -n crewai
   146→
   147→# If missing, check Terraform state
   148→cd kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/terraform
   149→terraform state list | grep secret
   150→
   151→# Re-run Terraform apply if needed
   152→# Use GitHub Actions validate-plan-apply workflow
   153→```
   154→
   155→## Authentication & Authorization Issues
   156→
   157→### WorkOS Authentication
   158→
   159→#### Issue: 'Authentication failed' or blank login page
   160→
   161→**Cause**: WorkOS credentials incorrect or misconfigured
   162→
   163→**Solution**:
   164→- Verify `WORKOS_CLIENT_ID` matches the WorkOS dashboard value
   165→- Confirm `WORKOS_AUTHKIT_DOMAIN` is correctly formatted (e.g., `your-domain.authkit.app`)
   166→- Check that `WORKOS_API_KEY` is properly stored in Kubernetes secrets
   167→- Verify WorkOS organization is active and AuthKit is enabled
   168→- Check browser console for authentication errors
   169→- Review application logs:
   170→  ```bash
   171→  kubectl logs -f deploy/crewai-web -n crewai
   172→  ```
   173→
   174→#### Issue: 'Invalid or expired session' errors or 'key must be 32 bytes' error
   175→
   176→**Cause**: `WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD` incorrect format or session key mismatch
   177→
   178→:::warning IMPORTANT
   179→`WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD` requires a **32-character string**, NOT 32 bytes
   180→:::
   181→
   182→**Solution**:
   183→- Generate correctly using: `openssl rand -hex 16` (generates 16 bytes as 32 hex characters)
   184→- **Do NOT use**: `openssl rand -base64 32` (generates 32 bytes, encodes to 44 characters - will fail)
   185→- Verify the password is exactly 32 characters long
   186→- Ensure the same password is used across all replicas
   187→- Check that secrets are properly synced from Cloud Secret Manager
   188→- Restart pods to pick up updated secrets:
   189→  ```bash
   190→  kubectl rollout restart deployment/crewai-web -n crewai
   191→  ```
   192→
   193→#### Issue: 'Magic Auth' not appearing as sign-in option
   194→
   195→**Cause**: AuthKit not configured with Magic Auth method
   196→
   197→**Solution**:
   198→- Enable Magic Auth in WorkOS AuthKit settings
   199→- Refresh browser cache or open in incognito mode
   200→- Verify AuthKit domain is correctly configured
   201→
   202→#### Issue: Google Workspace OIDC sign-in fails
   203→
   204→**Cause**: Google OAuth not properly configured on GCP Project
   205→
   206→**Solution**:
   207→- Verify Google OAuth consent screen is configured
   208→- Check OAuth 2.0 credentials are created and authorized for AuthKit domain
   209→- Confirm `your-domain.authkit.app` is added to authorized redirect URIs
   210→- Review GCP Console > APIs & Services > Credentials
   211→
   212→## Network & Gateway Issues
   213→
   214→### Gateway API Issues
   215→
   216→#### Issue: Application not accessible via domain
   217→
   218→**Cause**: Gateway, HTTPRoute, or certificate misconfiguration
   219→
   220→**Solution**:
   221→```bash
   222→# Check Gateway status
   223→kubectl get gateway -n crewai
   224→
   225→# Check HTTPRoute
   226→kubectl get httproute -n crewai
   227→
   228→# Check GCPBackendPolicy for health check configuration
   229→kubectl get gcpbackendpolicy -n crewai -o yaml
   230→
   231→# Check certificate status
   232→kubectl get managedcertificate -n crewai
   233→
   234→# View Gateway events
   235→kubectl describe gateway crewai-gateway -n crewai
   236→```
   237→
   238→#### Issue: 502 Bad Gateway errors
   239→
   240→**Cause**: Backend pods not healthy or health check failing
   241→
   242→**Solution**:
   243→```bash
   244→# Check pod health
   245→kubectl get pods -n crewai
   246→
   247→# Check if pods are passing health checks
   248→kubectl describe pod -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai
   249→
   250→# Verify health check endpoint works
   251→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai -- curl localhost:8080/health
   252→
   253→# Check GCPBackendPolicy health check configuration
   254→kubectl get gcpbackendpolicy -n crewai -o yaml
   255→```
   256→
   257→### DNS Resolution
   258→
   259→#### Issue: Domain name not resolving
   260→
   261→**Cause**: DNS records not configured or propagation delay
   262→
   263→**Solution**:
   264→```bash
   265→# Test DNS resolution
   266→nslookup crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com
   267→dig crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com
   268→
   269→# Check Cloud DNS records
   270→gcloud dns record-sets list --zone=ix-konecta-digital-com
   271→
   272→# Verify Gateway external IP
   273→kubectl get gateway crewai-gateway -n crewai -o jsonpath='{.status.addresses[0].value}'
   274→```
   275→
   276→## Storage Issues
   277→
   278→### GCS Access Issues
   279→
   280→#### Issue: 'Access Denied' when writing to storage bucket
   281→
   282→**Cause**: HMAC credentials invalid or service account lacks permissions
   283→
   284→**Solution**:
   285→```bash
   286→# Check credentials secret
   287→kubectl get secret crewai-credentials -n crewai -o yaml
   288→
   289→# Verify HMAC key is valid
   290→gcloud storage hmac list --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-platform
   291→
   292→# Test access from pod
   293→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai -- \
   294→  aws s3 ls s3://kd-ix-eur-dev-platform-crewai-data/
   295→
   296→# Check bucket exists
   297→gcloud storage buckets describe gs://kd-ix-eur-dev-platform-crewai-data
   298→```
   299→
   300→## Kubernetes Pod Issues
   301→
   302→### Pods Not Starting
   303→
   304→#### Issue: Pods stuck in 'Pending' state
   305→
   306→**Cause**: Insufficient resources or node issues
   307→
   308→**Solution**:
   309→```bash
   310→# Check node availability
   311→kubectl get nodes
   312→
   313→# Describe pod to see pending reason
   314→kubectl describe pod -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai
   315→
   316→# Check resource requests vs available
   317→kubectl describe nodes | grep -A 5 "Allocated resources"
   318→
   319→# Check namespace quota
   320→kubectl get resourcequota -n crewai
   321→```
   322→
   323→#### Issue: Pods crashing with 'CrashLoopBackOff'
   324→
   325→**Cause**: Application startup failure or configuration error
   326→
   327→**Solution**:
   328→```bash
   329→# Check pod logs
   330→kubectl logs -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai
   331→
   332→# Check previous logs (if restarted)
   333→kubectl logs -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai --previous
   334→
   335→# Check environment variables
   336→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai -- env | sort
   337→
   338→# Check events
   339→kubectl get events -n crewai --sort-by='.lastTimestamp'
   340→```
   341→
   342→### Secrets Issues
   343→
   344→#### Issue: Environment variables not populated from secrets
   345→
   346→**Cause**: Secret not created or not mounted correctly
   347→
   348→**Solution**:
   349→```bash
   350→# List secrets in namespace
   351→kubectl get secrets -n crewai
   352→
   353→# Check specific secret exists and has data
   354→kubectl describe secret crewai-credentials -n crewai
   355→
   356→# Verify pod has secret mounted
   357→kubectl get pod -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai -o yaml | grep -A 10 envFrom
   358→
   359→# Restart pods to pick up secret changes
   360→kubectl rollout restart deployment/crewai-web -n crewai
   361→```
   362→
   363→## Debug Commands Reference
   364→
   365→:::tip Quick Reference
   366→Quick reference for common debugging commands
   367→:::
   368→
   369→```bash
   370→# ============== ArgoCD ==============
   371→argocd app get crewai-dev
   372→argocd app sync crewai-dev
   373→argocd app logs crewai-dev
   374→argocd app history crewai-dev
   375→
   376→# ============== Pods ==============
   377→kubectl get pods -n crewai
   378→kubectl logs -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai
   379→kubectl logs deploy/crewai-web -n crewai --previous
   380→kubectl describe pod -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai
   381→
   382→# ============== Services ==============
   383→kubectl get svc -n crewai
   384→kubectl get gateway -n crewai
   385→kubectl get httproute -n crewai
   386→
   387→# ============== Secrets ==============
   388→kubectl get secrets -n crewai
   389→kubectl describe secret crewai-credentials -n crewai
   390→kubectl describe secret crewai-database-config -n crewai
   391→
   392→# ============== Events ==============
   393→kubectl get events -n crewai --sort-by='.lastTimestamp'
   394→
   395→# ============== Exec into Pod ==============
   396→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai -- /bin/bash
   397→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai -- env
   398→
   399→# ============== Port Forward ==============
   400→kubectl port-forward svc/crewai-web -n crewai 2603:80
   401→
   402→# ============== Rollout ==============
   403→kubectl rollout status deployment/crewai-web -n crewai
   404→kubectl rollout restart deployment/crewai-web -n crewai
   405→kubectl rollout undo deployment/crewai-web -n crewai
   406→```
   407→
   408→## Support Resources
   409→
   410→| Resource | Link |
   411→|----------|------|
   412→| kix-services-crewai Repository | [GitHub](https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai) |
   413→| Infrastructure Setup | [Infrastructure Guide](gcp-setup) |
   414→| Deployment Guide | [Deployment Guide](install) |
   415→| ArgoCD Documentation | [argo-cd.readthedocs.io](https://argo-cd.readthedocs.io/) |
   416→| kubectl Cheat Sheet | [Kubernetes Docs](https://kubernetes.io/docs/reference/kubectl/cheatsheet/) |
   417→
   418→### Reporting Issues
   419→
   420→When reporting issues, include:
   421→- Complete error messages and stack traces
   422→- Output of relevant kubectl commands
   423→- Kubernetes events (`kubectl get events -n crewai`)
   424→- Application logs (sanitized for sensitive data)
   425→- ArgoCD application status
   426→- Terraform plan output (if infrastructure-related)
   427→- CrewAI version and environment (dev/qa/prod)
   428→
```

     1→---
     2→title: Third-Party Integrations
     3→sidebar_label: Third-Party Integrations
     4→sidebar_position: 6
     5→description: Integration guidance for third-party services and platforms with CrewAI
     6→---
     7→
     8→# Third-Party Integrations
     9→
    10→This section covers integration guidance for popular third-party services and platforms with CrewAI installations.
    11→
    12→## WorkOS
    13→
    14→WorkOS is the authentication provider for CrewAI installations, offering secure enterprise-grade SSO and user management capabilities integrated with Google Workspace authentication.
    15→
    16→### Overview
    17→
    18→- Enterprise authentication provider for CrewAI using **AuthKit**
    19→- AuthKit configured to use **Google Auth platform** on the GCP Project hosting CrewAI
    20→- Sign-in methods:
    21→  - Google Workspace OIDC
    22→  - Magic Auth (passwordless email authentication)
    23→- **WorkOS Connect** provides OIDC middleware for CLI login to CrewAI via WorkOS
    24→- User and organization management
    25→- Seamless integration with Kubernetes deployments
    26→- Supports both Web UI and CLI authentication flows
    27→
    28→### Prerequisites
    29→
    30→- Active WorkOS account and organization
    31→- API credentials from WorkOS dashboard
    32→- AuthKit domain setup (e.g., `your-domain.authkit.app`)
    33→
    34→### Configuration
    35→
    36→#### CrewAI Configuration Variables
    37→
    38→These environment variables configure how CrewAI connects to WorkOS:
    39→
    40→```yaml
    41→# Authentication Provider Selection
    42→AUTH_PROVIDER: "workos"  # or "entra_id", "google", "local"
    43→
    44→# OAuth Configuration
    45→oauth:
    46→  enabled: true  # Enables OAuth flow for user authentication
    47→OAUTH_COOKIE_SECRET: "[REDACTED]"  # Secure cookie encryption key
    48→```
    49→
    50→#### WorkOS Configuration Variables
    51→
    52→These environment variables configure WorkOS AuthKit integration. See the [Deployment Guide](install) for Helm values configuration.
    53→
    54→```yaml
    55→# Public Credentials (safe to commit)
    56→WORKOS_CLIENT_ID: "client_XXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXX"  # from WorkOS dashboard
    57→WORKOS_AUTHKIT_DOMAIN: "your-domain.authkit.app"    # AuthKit domain
    58→
    59→# Secrets (MUST be stored in Cloud Secret Manager - NEVER commit)
    60→WORKOS_API_KEY: "[REDACTED]"  # API key from WorkOS dashboard
    61→WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD: "[REDACTED]"  # 32-character random string
    62→```
    63→
    64→#### WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD Generation
    65→
    66→The `WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD` must be a secure, randomly generated 32-character string:
    67→
    68→```bash
    69→# Generate using OpenSSL
    70→openssl rand -hex 16
    71→
    72→# Or using Python
    73→python -c "import secrets; print(secrets.token_hex(16))"
    74→
    75→# Or using Node.js
    76→node -e "console.log(require('crypto').randomBytes(16).toString('hex'))"
    77→```
    78→
    79→Example output: `a3f7d8c2e4b9f1a6c8d3e7b2f9a4c1d5`
    80→
    81→### Setup Steps
    82→
    83→1. **Create WorkOS Account and Organization** — Register in WorkOS dashboard
    84→
    85→2. **Configure AuthKit with Google Auth:**
    86→   - Set up Google OAuth provider on your GCP Project
    87→   - Configure AuthKit to authenticate against Google Workspace
    88→   - Configure sign-in methods: Google Workspace OIDC and Magic Auth
    89→
    90→3. **Generate Credentials in WorkOS Dashboard:**
    91→   - Copy Client ID (public - safe to commit)
    92→   - Generate API Key (secret - store in Cloud Secret Manager)
    93→   - Note AuthKit Domain (`your-domain.authkit.app`)
    94→
    95→4. **Generate Session Encryption Key:**
    96→   - Generate a 32-character random string for `WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD`
    97→   - Use command: `openssl rand -hex 16`
    98→
    99→5. **Configure WorkOS Connect for CLI Usage:**
   100→   - Enable WorkOS Connect for OIDC middleware
   101→   - Configure CLI authentication endpoint
   102→   - Enables passwordless CLI login via `crew ai login` command
   103→
   104→6. **Add Credentials to Kubernetes Values File:**
   105→   - Public values: `WORKOS_CLIENT_ID`, `WORKOS_AUTHKIT_DOMAIN`, `AUTH_PROVIDER`
   106→   - Store secrets in Google Cloud Secret Manager (not in values file)
   107→
   108→### Current Setup
   109→
   110→Based on workspace configuration:
   111→
   112→| Setting | Value |
   113→|---------|-------|
   114→| **Provider** | WorkOS with AuthKit |
   115→| **Identity Provider** | Google Auth on GCP Project |
   116→| **Sign-In Methods** | Google Workspace OIDC + Magic Auth |
   117→| **CLI Access** | WorkOS Connect OIDC middleware enabled |
   118→| **Authentication Domain** | `your-domain.authkit.app` |
   119→| **Deployment** | Kubernetes with Helm (GCP) |
   120→| **Secrets Management** | Google Cloud Secret Manager |
   121→| **Cookie Security** | Password-protected session cookies (32-character key) |
   122→| **Web UI** | OAuth-enabled authentication |
   123→| **CLI** | OIDC-based login via `crew ai login` |
   124→
   125→### Common Integrations
   126→
   127→- **Web UI**: Single Sign-On (SSO) with Google Workspace OIDC
   128→- **Web UI**: Passwordless authentication via Magic Auth
   129→- **CLI**: OIDC-based login for `crew ai` command-line interface
   130→- **CLI**: Secure token management via WorkOS Connect
   131→- User and organization provisioning
   132→- Directory synchronization with Google Workspace
   133→- Multi-tenant user management
   134→
   135→### Security Best Practices
   136→
   137→- Never commit `WORKOS_API_KEY` or `WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD` to version control
   138→- Store all secrets in Cloud Secret Manager (GCP) or equivalent
   139→- Rotate API keys regularly
   140→- Use environment-specific configurations (dev/prod)
   141→- Enable HTTPS for production deployments
   142→
   143→### Testing
   144→
   145→- Local testing with AuthKit domain
   146→- Test SSO flows in development environment
   147→- Validate user provisioning and organization sync
   148→- Integration testing with Kubernetes deployment
   149→
   150→### Troubleshooting
   151→
   152→- Verify `WORKOS_CLIENT_ID` matches dashboard configuration
   153→- Check `WORKOS_AUTHKIT_DOMAIN` is correctly formatted
   154→- Ensure `WORKOS_API_KEY` is properly stored and accessible
   155→- Review Kubernetes secret mounts and volume mappings
   156→- Check application logs for authentication errors
   157→
   158→### Related Documentation
   159→
   160→- See [GCP Setup Guide](gcp-setup) for Cloud Secret Manager configuration
   161→- Refer to [Install Guide](install) for complete Helm values setup
   162→- WorkOS official documentation: https://workos.com/docs
   163→
   164→## Integration Best Practices
   165→
   166→### Security
   167→
   168→- API key management
   169→- Credential rotation
   170→- Scope and permission minimization
   171→- Audit logging
   172→
   173→### Performance
   174→
   175→- Rate limiting strategies
   176→- Caching considerations
   177→- Async operations
   178→- Monitoring and alerting
   179→
   180→### Reliability
   181→
   182→- Error handling patterns
   183→- Retry logic
   184→- Fallback mechanisms
   185→- Health checks
   186→
   187→## Monitoring and Maintenance
   188→
   189→- Health checks and status monitoring
   190→- Logging integration events
   191→- Metrics and analytics
   192→- Update and version management
   193→
   194→## Support and Resources
   195→
   196→- [WorkOS Documentation](https://workos.com/docs)
   197→- [CrewAI Enterprise Documentation](https://enterprise-docs.crewai.com)
   198→- Community forums and discussions
   199→- Support channels: support@konecta.com
   200→

     1→---
     2→title: Frequently Asked Questions
     3→sidebar_label: FAQ
     4→sidebar_position: 7
     5→description: Common questions and answers about CrewAI Platform on Google Cloud
     6→---
     7→
     8→# Frequently Asked Questions
     9→
    10→:::info
    11→This FAQ covers common questions about installing, configuring, and operating CrewAI Platform on Google Cloud Platform.
    12→:::
    13→
    14→## General Questions
    15→
    16→### What is CrewAI Platform?
    17→
    18→CrewAI Platform is an enterprise-grade framework for orchestrating role-playing AI agents that work together to accomplish complex tasks. It provides a collaborative environment where autonomous AI agents can coordinate their efforts to solve sophisticated problems.
    19→
    20→**Key Features:**
    21→- Multi-agent orchestration and coordination
    22→- Role-based agent specialization
    23→- Task delegation and workflow management
    24→- Enterprise security and compliance
    25→- GCP-native integration
    26→
    27→### What are the system requirements?
    28→
    29→**Minimum Requirements for Development:**
    30→- GKE cluster with 3 nodes (e2-standard-2 or higher)
    31→- 8 vCPUs and 16 GB RAM total
    32→- 100 GB persistent storage
    33→- Cloud SQL PostgreSQL instance (db-f1-micro minimum)
    34→- 2 Cloud Storage buckets
    35→
    36→**Recommended Requirements for Production:**
    37→- GKE cluster with 6+ nodes (e2-standard-4 or higher)
    38→- 24+ vCPUs and 48+ GB RAM total
    39→- 500 GB persistent storage
    40→- Cloud SQL PostgreSQL instance (db-n1-standard-2 or higher)
    41→- Multi-regional Cloud Storage buckets
    42→- High availability configuration across 2+ regions
    43→
    44→### How much does it cost to run CrewAI on GCP?
    45→
    46→Costs vary based on environment and usage patterns:
    47→
    48→**Development Environment (estimated):**
    49→| Component | Cost |
    50→|-----------|------|
    51→| GKE cluster | $150-200/month |
    52→| Cloud SQL | $25-50/month |
    53→| Cloud Storage | $5-15/month |
    54→| Networking | $10-20/month |
    55→| **Total** | **~$190-285/month** |
    56→
    57→**Production Environment (estimated):**
    58→| Component | Cost |
    59→|-----------|------|
    60→| GKE cluster | $500-800/month |
    61→| Cloud SQL with HA | $200-400/month |
    62→| Cloud Storage multi-region | $30-60/month |
    63→| Load balancing and networking | $50-100/month |
    64→| Cloud Armor and security | $20-40/month |
    65→| **Total** | **~$800-1,400/month** |
    66→
    67→:::tip
    68→Use committed use discounts (CUDs) and sustained use discounts to reduce costs by 30-50%.
    69→:::
    70→
    71→## Deployment & Setup
    72→
    73→### How is CrewAI deployed?
    74→
    75→CrewAI uses a **GitOps deployment model** managed through the [`kix-services-crewai`](https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai) repository:
    76→
    77→| Layer | Technology | Description |
    78→|-------|------------|-------------|
    79→| **Infrastructure** | Terraform | GCS buckets, Cloud SQL, Secret Manager, K8s resources |
    80→| **Application** | ArgoCD | Multi-source apps pulling CrewAI Helm chart from OCI registry |
    81→| **CI/CD** | GitHub Actions | Validation, security scanning, approval gates |
    82→
    83→### How long does deployment take?
    84→
    85→**Typical Deployment Timeline:**
    86→- Terraform apply (via GitHub Actions): 5-10 minutes
    87→- ArgoCD sync: 2-5 minutes
    88→- Post-installation configuration: 5-10 minutes
    89→- **Total: 15-25 minutes**
    90→
    91→First-time installations may take longer due to:
    92→- API enablement (5-10 minutes)
    93→- Initial image pulls from registry
    94→- DNS propagation for custom domains (up to 48 hours, typically 5-10 minutes)
    95→
    96→### Can I install CrewAI without using Terraform?
    97→
    98→While Terraform is the recommended approach for reproducible infrastructure, you can manually provision resources through the GCP Console:
    99→
   100→**Manual Setup Steps:**
   101→1. Create VPC network and subnets
   102→2. Provision GKE cluster
   103→3. Create Cloud SQL instance
   104→4. Create Cloud Storage buckets
   105→5. Configure IAM roles and service accounts
   106→6. Deploy CrewAI via Helm
   107→
   108→:::warning
   109→Manual setup is **not recommended** for production environments due to:
   110→- Lack of version control and auditability
   111→- Higher risk of configuration drift
   112→- Difficulty reproducing environments
   113→- Complex rollback procedures
   114→:::
   115→
   116→### What authentication methods are supported?
   117→
   118→CrewAI Platform supports multiple authentication providers:
   119→
   120→| Provider | Description |
   121→|----------|-------------|
   122→| **WorkOS** | Recommended for enterprise SSO |
   123→| **Microsoft Entra ID** | Formerly Azure AD |
   124→| **Google Workspace** | Google OIDC authentication |
   125→| **Local authentication** | Development only |
   126→
   127→Configure the provider in the Helm values file in `kix-services-crewai`:
   128→```yaml
   129→# environments/{env}/helm/crewai/values-gke.yaml
   130→envVars:
   131→  AUTH_PROVIDER: "workos"  # or "entra_id", "google", "local"
   132→```
   133→
   134→:::note
   135→Local authentication should only be used for development and testing. Production environments must use SSO-based authentication.
   136→:::
   137→
   138→### Do I need a custom domain?
   139→
   140→**Development:** Not required - you can use port-forwarding or a temporary LoadBalancer IP
   141→
   142→**Production:** **Strongly recommended** for:
   143→- Professional appearance
   144→- SSL/TLS certificate management
   145→- OAuth callback URL configuration
   146→- Stable endpoint for integrations
   147→
   148→**Current Domains (managed by Terraform):**
   149→
   150→| Environment | Domain |
   151→|-------------|--------|
   152→| **dev** | `crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com` |
   153→| **qa** | `crewai.qa.ix.konecta-digital.com` |
   154→| **prod** | `crewai.ix.konecta-digital.com` |
   155→
   156→Terraform configures Gateway API with managed SSL certificates automatically.
   157→
   158→## Configuration & Customization
   159→
   160→### How do I scale my deployment?
   161→
   162→**Horizontal Scaling (Recommended):**
   163→```yaml
   164→# In my-values.yaml
   165→web:
   166→  replicaCount: 3  # Increase for more capacity
   167→
   168→worker:
   169→  replicaCount: 5  # Scale workers independently
   170→```
   171→
   172→**Auto-scaling with HPA:**
   173→```yaml
   174→web:
   175→  autoscaling:
   176→    enabled: true
   177→    minReplicas: 3
   178→    maxReplicas: 10
   179→    targetCPUUtilizationPercentage: 70
   180→```
   181→
   182→After updating values:
   183→```bash
   184→helm upgrade crewai-platform oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable/crewai-platform \
   185→  --values my-values.yaml
   186→```
   187→
   188→### Can I use an existing database?
   189→
   190→**Yes!** CrewAI supports using an external PostgreSQL database:
   191→
   192→```yaml
   193→# Disable bundled PostgreSQL
   194→postgres:
   195→  enabled: false
   196→
   197→# Configure external database
   198→envVars:
   199→  DB_HOST: "your-db-instance.example.com"
   200→  DB_PORT: "5432"
   201→  DB_USER: "crewai_user"
   202→  DB_NAME: "crewai_production"
   203→
   204→secrets:
   205→  DB_PASSWORD=[REDACTED]"
   206→```
   207→
   208→**Requirements:**
   209→- PostgreSQL 12 or higher
   210→- Network connectivity from GKE to database
   211→- Database created with UTF8 encoding
   212→
   213→### How do I configure custom storage?
   214→
   215→CrewAI uses S3-compatible storage. For GCP, configure Cloud Storage with HMAC keys:
   216→
   217→**Step 1: Create HMAC Keys**
   218→```bash
   219→gcloud storage hmac create SERVICE_ACCOUNT_EMAIL
   220→```
   221→
   222→**Step 2: Configure Storage**
   223→```yaml
   224→envVars:
   225→  STORAGE_SERVICE: "amazon"
   226→  AWS_ENDPOINT: "https://storage.googleapis.com"
   227→  AWS_REGION: "europe-west1"
   228→  AWS_BUCKET: "your-project-crewai-data"
   229→  LOGS_BUCKET: "your-project-crewai-logs"
   230→
   231→secrets:
   232→  AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID: "GOOG1E..."
   233→  AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY: "your-secret-key"
   234→```
   235→
   236→### Can I customize resource limits?
   237→
   238→**Yes!** Resource requests and limits can be customized per service:
   239→
   240→```yaml
   241→web:
   242→  resources:
   243→    requests:
   244→      cpu: "500m"
   245→      memory: "1Gi"
   246→    limits:
   247→      cpu: "2000m"
   248→      memory: "4Gi"
   249→
   250→worker:
   251→  resources:
   252→    requests:
   253→      cpu: "1000m"
   254→      memory: "2Gi"
   255→    limits:
   256→      cpu: "4000m"
   257→      memory: "8Gi"
   258→```
   259→
   260→:::tip
   261→Set requests to typical usage and limits to peak capacity. Monitor actual usage with `kubectl top pods` and adjust accordingly.
   262→:::
   263→
   264→## Operations & Troubleshooting
   265→
   266→### How do I upgrade to a new version?
   267→
   268→**Step 1: Backup Configuration**
   269→```bash
   270→cp my-values.yaml my-values.yaml.backup.$(date +%Y%m%d)
   271→```
   272→
   273→**Step 2: Check Available Versions**
   274→```bash
   275→helm search repo registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable --versions
   276→```
   277→
   278→**Step 3: Perform Upgrade**
   279→```bash
   280→helm upgrade crewai-platform oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable/crewai-platform \
   281→  --version NEW_VERSION \
   282→  --values my-values.yaml
   283→```
   284→
   285→**Step 4: Monitor Rollout**
   286→```bash
   287→kubectl rollout status deployment/crewai-platform-web
   288→kubectl get pods -w
   289→```
   290→
   291→See the [Deployment Guide - Upgrading CrewAI Version](install#upgrading-crewai-version) for detailed upgrade instructions.
   292→
   293→### How do I back up my data?
   294→
   295→**Database Backups:**
   296→- Cloud SQL automated daily backups (enabled by default)
   297→- On-demand backup: `gcloud sql backups create --instance=INSTANCE_NAME`
   298→- Point-in-time recovery (PITR) available within 7 days
   299→
   300→**Configuration Backups:**
   301→```bash
   302→# Backup Helm values
   303→kubectl get secret -n default -o yaml > secrets-backup.yaml
   304→
   305→# Backup entire namespace
   306→kubectl get all -n default -o yaml > namespace-backup.yaml
   307→```
   308→
   309→**Storage Backups:**
   310→- Cloud Storage versioning enabled
   311→- Cross-region replication configured
   312→- Lifecycle policies for retention
   313→
   314→### What monitoring is available?
   315→
   316→**Built-in Monitoring:**
   317→- Cloud Monitoring dashboards (GKE and Cloud SQL)
   318→- Cloud Logging for centralized logs
   319→- Cloud Trace for distributed tracing
   320→- Uptime checks for availability
   321→
   322→**Access Metrics:**
   323→```bash
   324→# View pod metrics
   325→kubectl top pods
   326→
   327→# View logs
   328→kubectl logs -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform --tail=100
   329→
   330→# Check cluster status
   331→kubectl get nodes
   332→kubectl get pods
   333→```
   334→
   335→### How do I troubleshoot pod failures?
   336→
   337→**Step 1: Check Pod Status**
   338→```bash
   339→kubectl get pods
   340→kubectl describe pod POD_NAME
   341→```
   342→
   343→**Step 2: View Logs**
   344→```bash
   345→kubectl logs POD_NAME
   346→kubectl logs POD_NAME --previous  # For crashed pods
   347→```
   348→
   349→**Step 3: Check Events**
   350→```bash
   351→kubectl get events --sort-by='.lastTimestamp'
   352→```
   353→
   354→**Common Issues:**
   355→| Issue | Cause |
   356→|-------|-------|
   357→| **ImagePullBackOff** | Registry authentication failed |
   358→| **CrashLoopBackOff** | Application error - check logs |
   359→| **Pending** | Insufficient cluster resources |
   360→| **OOMKilled** | Out of memory - increase limits |
   361→
   362→See [Troubleshooting Guide](troubleshooting) for detailed solutions.
   363→
   364→## Security & Compliance
   365→
   366→### Is CrewAI Platform secure?
   367→
   368→**Security Features:**
   369→- End-to-end encryption (TLS 1.2+)
   370→- Encryption at rest (Cloud SQL, Cloud Storage)
   371→- IAM-based access control
   372→- Network isolation via VPC
   373→- Cloud Armor DDoS protection
   374→- Identity-Aware Proxy (IAP) support
   375→- Regular security updates
   376→- Audit logging enabled
   377→
   378→**Best Practices:**
   379→- Use Workload Identity for service account authentication
   380→- Store secrets in Cloud Secret Manager
   381→- Enable binary authorization for container images
   382→- Implement least-privilege IAM roles
   383→- Regular vulnerability scanning
   384→
   385→### How do I manage secrets securely?
   386→
   387→**Recommended Approach: Cloud Secret Manager**
   388→
   389→**Step 1: Store Secret**
   390→```bash
   391→echo -n "your-secret-value" | gcloud secrets create SECRET_NAME --data-file=-
   392→```
   393→
   394→**Step 2: Grant Access**
   395→```bash
   396→gcloud secrets add-iam-policy-binding SECRET_NAME \
   397→  --member="serviceAccount:SA_EMAIL" \
   398→  --role="roles/secretmanager.secretAccessor"
   399→```
   400→
   401→:::warning
   402→**Never commit secrets to version control!** Always use Secret Manager or external secret management solutions.
   403→:::
   404→
   405→### Is CrewAI compliant with regulations?
   406→
   407→CrewAI Platform can be configured to meet various compliance requirements:
   408→
   409→**Supported Compliance Frameworks:**
   410→- **SOC 2 Type II**: With proper GCP organization policies
   411→- **GDPR**: Data residency and privacy controls available
   412→- **HIPAA**: Available with Business Associate Agreement (BAA)
   413→- **ISO 27001**: GCP infrastructure is certified
   414→
   415→**Compliance Configuration:**
   416→- Data residency: Deploy in specific GCP regions
   417→- Audit logging: Enabled by default
   418→- Data encryption: At rest and in transit
   419→- Access controls: IAM and RBAC
   420→
   421→:::note
   422→Compliance is a shared responsibility. While GCP and CrewAI provide compliant infrastructure, your implementation and operational practices must also meet regulatory requirements.
   423→:::
   424→
   425→## Integration & Development
   426→
   427→### How do I integrate with external services?
   428→
   429→CrewAI supports integrations through:
   430→
   431→**1. OAuth Configuration**
   432→```yaml
   433→oauth:
   434→  enabled: true
   435→
   436→secrets:
   437→  OAUTH_DB_HOST: "10.29.0.2"
   438→```
   439→
   440→**2. API Access**
   441→- RESTful API for programmatic access
   442→- Webhook support for event-driven workflows
   443→- SDK support (Python, Node.js)
   444→
   445→**3. Third-Party Integrations**
   446→- Vertex AI for ML models
   447→- 11Labs for voice synthesis
   448→- Custom integrations via API
   449→
   450→See [Third-Party Integrations](third-party) for detailed guides.
   451→
   452→### Can I use custom AI models?
   453→
   454→**Yes!** CrewAI supports custom model integration:
   455→
   456→**Vertex AI Models:**
   457→- Deploy custom models to Vertex AI
   458→- Configure endpoint URLs in CrewAI
   459→- Use Workload Identity for authentication
   460→
   461→**External Models:**
   462→- OpenAI API
   463→- Anthropic Claude API
   464→- Custom inference endpoints
   465→
   466→### How do I access the API?
   467→
   468→**1. Port Forwarding (Development)**
   469→```bash
   470→kubectl port-forward svc/crewai-platform-web 8080:80
   471→# Access at http://localhost:8080
   472→```
   473→
   474→**2. LoadBalancer (Production)**
   475→```bash
   476→kubectl get svc crewai-platform-web
   477→# Access at LoadBalancer IP or configured domain
   478→```
   479→
   480→**3. API Authentication**
   481→- Obtain API key from CrewAI admin console
   482→- Include in request headers: `Authorization: Bearer YOUR_API_KEY`
   483→
   484→**Example API Call:**
   485→```bash
   486→curl -X GET "https://crewai.example.com/api/v1/agents" \
   487→  -H "Authorization: Bearer YOUR_API_KEY"
   488→```
   489→
   490→## Additional Resources
   491→
   492→| Resource | Link |
   493→|----------|------|
   494→| Installation Guide | [Installation Guide](install) |
   495→| Troubleshooting | [Troubleshooting Guide](troubleshooting) |
   496→| GCP Setup Guide | [GCP Infrastructure Setup](gcp-setup) |
   497→| Post-Installation | [Post-Installation Setup](post-install) |
   498→
   499→:::note Can't find your question?
   500→Contact support at support@konecta.com or check the [CrewAI Enterprise Documentation](https://enterprise-docs.crewai.com).
   501→:::
   502→
```

Deployed crew pods with isolated resources |\n\nThis separation provides resource isolation, security boundaries, RBAC granularity, and operational clarity. Network policies control cross-namespace communication, and each namespace has its own resource quotas. See the [Infrastructure Setup](gcp-setup) guide for details."

  "content": "---\ntitle: CrewAI Enterprise\nsidebar_label: CrewAI Enterprise\nsidebar_position: 1\ndescription: Documentation for CrewAI Enterprise Platform deployment on Google Cloud Platform\n---\n\n# CrewAI Enterprise Platform\n\nThis section provides comprehensive documentation for deploying and operating the **CrewAI Enterprise Platform** on Google Cloud Platform (GCP) using GitOps practices.\n\n## Overview\n\nCrewAI Platform is an enterprise-grade framework for orchestrating role-playing AI agents that work together to accomplish complex tasks. It provides a collaborative environment where autonomous AI agents can coordinate their efforts to solve sophisticated problems.\n\n## Deployment Model\n\nCrewAI Enterprise is deployed using a **GitOps approach** managed through the [`kix-services-crewai`](https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai) repository:\n\n| Layer | Technology | Description |\n|-------|------------|-------------|\n| **Infrastructure** | Terraform | GCS buckets, Cloud SQL databases, Secret Manager, Kubernetes resources |\n| **Application Delivery** | ArgoCD | Multi-source applications pulling CrewAI Helm chart from OCI registry |\n| **CI/CD** | GitHub Actions | Automated validation, security scanning, approval gates, drift detection |\n| **Secrets** | GCP Secret Manager + ESO + K8s Secrets | Dual-layer secrets with External Secrets Operator and Workload Identity |\n\n## Documentation Structure\n\n| Document | Description |\n|----------|-------------|\n| [Infrastructure Setup](gcp-setup) | Terraform configuration for GCP resources in kix-services-crewai |\n| [Deployment Guide](install) | ArgoCD-based deployment via GitHub Actions workflows |\n| [Post-Installation Setup](post-install) | Initial configuration and access setup after deployment |\n| [Troubleshooting Guide](troubleshooting) | Solutions for common issues during deployment and operation |\n| [Third-Party Integrations](third-party) | Integration guides for WorkOS and other services |\n| [FAQ](faq) | Frequently asked questions about CrewAI Platform |\n\n## Environments\n\n| Environment | Domain | Branch | Auto-Sync |\n|-------------|--------|--------|-----------|\n| **Development** | `crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com` | `develop` | Yes |\n| **QA** | `crewai.qa.ix.konecta-digital.com` | `main` | Yes |\n| **Production** | `crewai.ix.konecta-digital.com` | `v*` tags | Manual |\n\n## Quick Start\n\n### Prerequisites\n\nBefore deploying CrewAI, ensure you have:\n\n- Access to the [`kix-services-crewai`](https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai) repository\n- GitHub Actions permissions to run workflows\n- GCP IAM access to the target environment project\n- **CrewAI Registry** credentials from the enterprise portal (stored in GCP Secret Manager)\n\n### Deployment Workflow\n\n```\nPR to main/develop\n        ↓\n[Auto Validate on Changes]\n  - Terraform fmt, validate, Trivy scan\n        ↓\nMerge to branch\n        ↓\n[Manual Dispatch: validate-plan-apply]\n  - Choose environment (dev/qa/prod)\n  - Branch validation enforced\n  - Change window check (prod only)\n        ↓\n[Validate] → [Plan] → [Approval Gate] → [Apply]\n        ↓\nArgoCD syncs application automatically\n```\n\n1. **Make Changes** — Update Terraform or Helm values in `kix-services-crewai`\n2. **Create PR** — Changes are auto-validated on PR\n3. **Merge** — Merge to `develop` (dev) or `main` (qa)\n4. **Deploy** — Run the `validate-plan-apply` workflow in GitHub Actions\n5. **Verify** — ArgoCD syncs the application; verify in ArgoCD dashboard\n\n## Key Features\n\n- **Multi-agent Orchestration** — Coordinate multiple AI agents working together\n- **Role-based Specialization** — Define agent roles and responsibilities\n- **Task Delegation** — Automatic workflow management and task assignment\n- **Enterprise Security** — SSO (WorkOS), encryption, IAM, and compliance controls\n- **GCP-native Integration** — Optimized for Google Cloud Platform\n- **GitOps Deployment** — Infrastructure and application changes through Git\n- **Dual Namespace Isolation** — Separate namespaces for platform and crew workloads\n- **Autoscaling** — HPA-based scaling with PodDisruptionBudgets for availability\n- **GitHub App Integration** — Native GitHub connectivity for crew source management\n\n## Architecture Overview\n\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│ Network policies control cross-namespace communication using a discrete per-concern pattern, and each namespace has its own resource quotas. See the [Infrastructure Setup](gcp-setup) guide for details.\n\n## Repository Links\n\n| Repository | Purpose |\n|------------|---------|\n| [kix-services-crewai](https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai) | CrewAI deployment (Terraform + ArgoCD) |\n| [kix-platform-modules](https://github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules) | Shared Terraform modules |\n| [kix-platform-infrastructure](https://github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-infrastructure) | Platform GKE clusters and foundation |\n\n## Support\n\n- **Internal Support**: Platform Engineering team via Slack #platform-engineering\n- **CrewAI Enterprise Portal**: [https://enterprise.crewai.com](https://enterprise.crewai.com)\n- **Official Documentation**: [https://enterprise-docs.crewai.com](https://enterprise-docs.crewai.com)\n\n---\n\n**Contact**: nathan.booth@konecta.com, mischa.vanoijen@konecta.com\n"

     6→---
     7→
     8→# CrewAI Enterprise Platform
     9→
    10→This section provides comprehensive documentation for deploying and operating the **CrewAI Enterprise Platform** on Google Cloud Platform (GCP) using GitOps practices.
    11→
    12→## Overview
    13→
    14→CrewAI Platform is an enterprise-grade framework for orchestrating role-playing AI agents that work together to accomplish complex tasks. It provides a collaborative environment where autonomous AI agents can coordinate their efforts to solve sophisticated problems.
    15→
    16→## Deployment Model
    17→
    18→CrewAI Enterprise is deployed using a **GitOps approach** managed through the [`kix-services-crewai`](https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai) repository:
    19→
    20→| Layer | Technology | Description |
    21→|-------|------------|-------------|
    22→| **Infrastructure** | Terraform | GCS buckets, Cloud SQL databases, Secret Manager, Kubernetes resources |
    23→| **Application Delivery** | ArgoCD | Multi-source applications pulling CrewAI Helm chart from OCI registry |
    24→| **CI/CD** | GitHub Actions | Automated validation, security scanning, approval gates, drift detection |
    25→| **Secrets** | GCP Secret Manager + ESO + K8s Secrets | Dual-layer secrets with External Secrets Operator and Workload Identity |
    26→
    27→## Documentation Structure
    28→
    29→| Document | Description |
    30→|----------|-------------|
    31→| [Infrastructure Setup](gcp-setup) | Terraform configuration for GCP resources in kix-services-crewai |
    32→| [Deployment Guide](install) | ArgoCD-based deployment via GitHub Actions workflows |
    33→| [Post-Installation Setup](post-install) | Initial configuration and access setup after deployment |
    34→| [Troubleshooting Guide](troubleshooting) | Solutions for common issues during deployment and operation |
    35→| [Third-Party Integrations](third-party) | Integration guides for WorkOS and other services |
    36→| [FAQ](faq) | Frequently asked questions about CrewAI Platform |
    37→
    38→## Environments
    39→
    40→| Environment | Domain | Branch | Auto-Sync |
    41→|-------------|--------|--------|-----------|
    42→| **Development** | `crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com` | `develop` | Yes |
    43→| **QA** | `crewai.qa.ix.konecta-digital.com` | `main` | Yes |
    44→| **Production** | `crewai.ix.konecta-digital.com` | `v*` tags | Manual |
    45→
    46→## Quick Start
    47→
    48→### Prerequisites
    49→
    50→Before deploying CrewAI, ensure you have:
    51→
    52→- Access to the [`kix-services-crewai`](https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai) repository
    53→- GitHub Actions permissions to run workflows
    54→- GCP IAM access to the target environment project
    55→- **CrewAI Registry** credentials from the enterprise portal (stored in GCP Secret Manager)
    56→
    57→### Deployment Workflow
    58→
    59→```
    60→PR to main/develop
    61→        ↓
    62→[Auto Validate on Changes]
    63→  - Terraform fmt, validate, Trivy scan
    64→        ↓
    65→Merge to branch
    66→        ↓
    67→[Manual Dispatch: validate-plan-apply]
    68→  - Choose environment (dev/qa/prod)
    69→  - Branch validation enforced
    70→  - Change window check (prod only)
    71→        ↓
    72→[Validate] → [Plan] → [Approval Gate] → [Apply]
    73→        ↓
    74→ArgoCD syncs application automatically
    75→```
    76→
    77→1. **Make Changes** — Update Terraform or Helm values in `kix-services-crewai`
    78→2. **Create PR** — Changes are auto-validated on PR
    79→3. **Merge** — Merge to `develop` (dev) or `main` (qa)
    80→4. **Deploy** — Run the `validate-plan-apply` workflow in GitHub Actions
    81→5. **Verify** — ArgoCD syncs the application; verify in ArgoCD dashboard
    82→
    83→## Key Features
    84→
    85→- **Multi-agent Orchestration** — Coordinate multiple AI agents working together
    86→- **Role-based Specialization** — Define agent roles and responsibilities
    87→- **Task Delegation** — Automatic workflow management and task assignment
    88→- **Enterprise Security** — SSO (WorkOS), encryption, IAM, and compliance controls
    89→- **GCP-native Integration** — Optimized for Google Cloud Platform
    90→- **GitOps Deployment** — Infrastructure and application changes through Git
    91→- **Dual Namespace Isolation** — Separate namespaces for platform and crew workloads
    92→- **Autoscaling** — HPA-based scaling with PodDisruptionBudgets for availability
    93→- **GitHub App Integration** — Native GitHub connectivity for crew source management
    94→

{
