  "content": "---\ntitle: Infrastructure Setup\nsidebar_label: Infrastructure Setup\nsidebar_position: 2\ndescription: Terraform infrastructure configuration for CrewAI in kix-services-crewai\n---\n\n# CrewAI Infrastructure Setup\n\nCrewAI infrastructure is managed through Terraform in the [`kix-services-crewai`](https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai) repository. This guide explains the infrastructure components and how they're configured.\n\n## Repository Structure\n\n```\nkix-services-crewai/\n├── environments/\n│   ├── dev/\n│   │   ├── terraform/           # Consolidated Infrastructure as Code\n│   │   │   ├── main.tf              # Infrastructure + Secrets + IAM\n│   │   │   ├── variables.tf         # Input variables\n│   │   │   ├── outputs.tf           # Terraform outputs\n│   │   │   ├── versions.tf          # Provider versions\n│   │   │   └── backend.tf           # Remote state in GCS\n│   │   ├── argocd/\n│   │   │   └── application.yaml # ArgoCD Application manifest\n│   │   └── helm/\n│   │       └── values.yaml      # Gateway API, HPA, network policies\n│   ├── qa/                      # Same structure as dev\n│   └── prod/                    # Same structure as dev\n├── docs/                        # Internal documentation\n│   ├── prps/                    # Product Requirements & Plans\n│   ├── runbooks/                # Emergency procedures\n│   └── tasks/                   # Task breakdown documents\n└── .github/workflows/           # CI/CD pipelines\n    ├── validate-and-plan.yml\n    ├── validate-plan-apply.yml\n    ├── argocd-deploy.yml\n    ├── manual-org-initial-setup.yml\n    ├── manual-studio-install.yml\n    ├── manual-validate-plan.yml\n    └── terraform-drift-detection.yml\n```\n\n## Infrastructure Components\n\n### What Terraform Creates\n\n| Component | Resource | Description |\n|-----------|----------|-------------|\n| **Service Accounts** | `google_service_account` | GCP SA for CrewAI workloads with Workload Identity |\n| **IAM Bindings** | `google_*_iam_member` | Cloud SQL, GCS, Secret Manager, Workload Identity |\n| **Namespaces** | `kubernetes_namespace` | `crewai-{env}` (platform) + `crewai-crews-{env}` (crew workloads) |\n| **Network Policies** | `kubernetes_network_policy` | Discrete per-concern policies for fine-grained traffic control |\n| **Resource Quotas** | `kubernetes_resource_quota` | Per-namespace CPU/memory limits for both namespaces |\n| **GCS Buckets** | `google_storage_bucket` | Data bucket + logs bucket with versioning |\n| **HMAC Keys** | `google_storage_hmac_key` | S3-compatible access for CrewAI |\n| **Cloud SQL DB** | `google_sql_database` | Three databases: production, cable, oauth |\n| **Cloud SQL User** | `google_sql_user` | Service account-based IAM authentication |\n| **Secrets** | `google_secret_manager_secret` | DB credentials, HMAC keys, OAuth config, GitHub App |\n| **K8s Secrets** | `kubernetes_secret` | Populated from Secret Manager for pod consumption |\n| **TLS Certificates** | `google_certificate_manager_*` | Managed SSL certificates for Gateway API |\n\n### Remote Modules Used\n\nTerraform composes modules from [`kix-platform-modules`](https://github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules):\n\n```hcl\n# GCS bucket with versioning and lifecycle policies\nmodule \"gcs_data\" {\n  source = \"github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/data-services/gcs?ref=main\"\n\n  project_id  = var.gke_project_id\n  bucket_name = \"${var.gke_project_id}-crewai-data\"\n  location    = var.region\n  # ...\n}\n\n# Single-secret-per-call pattern for Secret Manager\nmodule \"secret_db_password\" {\n  source = \"github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/secret-manager?ref=main\"\n\n  project_id  = var.gke_project_id\n  secret_id   = \"crewai-db-password\"\n  secret_data = random_password.db.result\n}\n\n# Note: Namespaces are created by ArgoCD (CreateNamespace=true)\n# Terraform creates resources within the namespace\n```\n\n## Environment Configuration\n\n### Development\n\n| Setting | Value |\n|---------|-------|\n| **GKE Cluster** | `kd-ix-eur-dev-gke` |\n| **Domain** | `crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com` |\n| **Platform Namespace** | `crewai-dev` |\n| **Crew Namespace** | `crewai-crews-dev` |\n| **Replicas** | 1 web, 1 worker (HPA: 1-5) |\n| **Resources** | 1000m/2Gi requests, 2000m/4Gi limits |\n| **State Bucket** | `kd-tfstate-dev` |\n| **State Prefix** | `services/crewai/resources` |\n\n### QA\n\n| Setting | Value |\n|---------|-------|\n| **GKE Cluster** | `kd-ix-eur-qa-gke` |\n| **Domain** | `crewai.qa.ix.konecta-digital.com` |\n| **Platform Namespace** | `crewai-qa` |\n| **Crew Namespace** | `crewai-crews-qa` |\n| **Replicas** | 2 web, 2 worker |\n| **Resources** | 1500m/4Gi requests, 3000m/8Gi limits |\n| **State Bucket** | `kd-tfstate-qa` |\n| **State Prefix** | `services/crewai/resources` |\n\n### Production\n\n| Setting | Value |\n|---------|-------|\n| **GKE Cluster** | `kd-ix-eur-prod-gke` |\n| **Domain** | `crewai.ix.konecta-digital.com` |\n| **Platform Namespace** | `crewai-prod` |\n| **Crew Namespace** | `crewai-crews-prod` |\n| **Replicas** | 3+ web, 3+ worker (HPA max 10) |\n| **Resources** | 2000m/6Gi requests, 4000m/12Gi limits |\n| **PDB** | Min 2 available |\n| **State Bucket** | `kd-tfstate-prod` |\n| **State Prefix** | `services/crewai/resources` |\n\n## Dual Namespace Architecture\n\nEach environment uses two namespaces for workload isolation:\n\n| Environment | Platform Namespace | Crew Namespace |\n|-------------|-------------------|----------------|\n| Dev | `crewai-dev` | `crewai-crews-dev` |\n| QA | `crewai-qa` | `crewai-crews-qa` |\n| Prod | `crewai-prod` | `crewai-crews-prod` |\n\n### Platform Namespace\n\nContains the core platform components:\n- **Web** — Main application server\n- **Worker** — Background job processing\n- **BuildKit** — Container image building for crews\n- **Cloud SQL Proxy** — Database connectivity sidecar\n\n### Crew Namespace\n\nContains deployed crew workloads with:\n- **Resource quotas** — Dev/QA: 20 CPU/40Gi; Prod: 80 CPU/160Gi\n- **Per-container limits** — Max: 4 CPU/8Gi; Default: 500m/1Gi\n- **Pod Security Standards** — Baseline (dev), Restricted (prod)\n- **RBAC** — Platform SA has crew management permissions\n\n### Cross-Namespace Communication\n\nNetwork policies control traffic between namespaces:\n- Platform pods can reach crew pods for management\n- Crew pods can reach platform BuildKit and Worker\n- DNS resolution is allowed to `kube-system`\n- GCP metadata server access for Workload Identity\n\n## Secrets Management\n\nCrewAI uses a dual-layer secrets architecture:\n\n### Layer 1: GCP Secret Manager (Terraform-managed)\n\nTerraform creates secrets in GCP Secret Manager:\n\n```hcl\nresource \"google_secret_manager_secret\" \"db_password\" {\n  secret_id = \"crewai-db-password-${var.environment}\"\n\n  replication {\n    auto {}\n  }\n}\n\nresource \"google_secret_manager_secret_version\" \"db_password\" {\n  secret=[REDACTED].db_password.id\n  secret_data = random_password.db.result\n}\n```\n\n### Layer 2: Kubernetes Secrets\n\nSecrets are consumed by pods through two mechanisms:\n\n1. **Terraform-created K8s secrets** — Direct population from Secret Manager values\n2. **External Secrets Operator (ESO)** — For GitHub App credentials and other secrets managed via `ClusterSecretStore`\n\n```hcl\nresource \"kubernetes_secret\" \"crewai_credentials\" {\n  metadata {\n    name      = \"crewai-credentials\"\n    namespace = local.namespace\n  }\n\n  data = {\n    AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID     = google_storage_hmac_key.crewai.access_id\n    AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY = google_storage_hmac_key.crewai.secret\n    DB_PASSWORD           = random_password.db.result\n  }\n}\n```\n\n### Secrets Created\n\n| Secret Name | Contents | Used By |\n|-------------|----------|---------|\n| `crewai-credentials` | HMAC keys, DB password | Web, Worker pods |\n| `crewai-database-config` | DB host, port, name, user | Web, Worker pods |\n| `crewai-registry` | CrewAI OCI registry auth | BuildKit, image pulls |\n| `crewai-github-app` | GitHub App ID, private key (via ESO) | GitHub integration |\n| `crewai-rails-secrets` | Rails encryption key, secret key base | Web pods |\n\n## Kubernetes Resources\n\n### Namespaces\n\nEach environment provisions two namespaces:\n\n| Environment | Platform Namespace | Crew Namespace | Purpose |\n|-------------|-------------------|----------------|---------|\n| **dev** | `crewai-dev` | `crewai-crews-dev` | Platform + crew isolation |\n| **qa** | `crewai-qa` | `crewai-crews-qa` | Platform + crew isolation |\n| **prod** | `crewai-prod` | `crewai-crews-prod` | Platform + crew isolation |\n\n### Network Policies\n\nNetwork policies follow a **discrete per-concern pattern**, with separate policies for each traffic type rather than a single monolithic policy:\n\n| Policy | Namespace | Purpose |\n|--------|-----------|---------|\n| `default-deny` | Platform | Deny all ingress by default |\n| `allow-gcp-health-checks` | Platform | GCP LB health check IPs (35.191.0.0/16, 130.211.0.0/22) |\n| `allow-dns` | Both | DNS resolution to kube-system |\n| `allow-gcp-metadata` | Both | GCP metadata server (169.254.169.254) for Workload Identity |\n| `allow-k8s-api` | Platform | Kubernetes API server access for replicated SDK |\n| `allow-crews` | Platform | Cross-namespace traffic to/from crew namespace |\n| `allow-external-https` | Both | Egress to external HTTPS (GCP APIs, registries) |\n\n```yaml\n# Example: GCP health check IPs (mandatory for load balancer)\ningress:\n  - from:\n      - ipBlock:\n          cidr: 35.191.0.0/16\n      - ipBlock:\n          cidr: 130.211.0.0/22\n    ports:\n      - port: 8080\n        protocol: TCP\n```\n\n### Autoscaling (HPA)\n\nHorizontal Pod Autoscaler is configured for web and worker deployments:\n\n```yaml\n# HPA configuration in values.yaml\nweb:\n  autoscaling:\n    enabled: true\n    minReplicas: 1\n    maxReplicas: 5\n    targetCPUUtilizationPercentage: 70\n    targetMemoryUtilizationPercentage: 80\n\nworker:\n  autoscaling:\n    enabled: true\n    minReplicas: 1\n    maxReplicas: 5\n    targetCPUUtilizationPercentage: 70\n    targetMemoryUtilizationPercentage: 80\n```\n\n### PodDisruptionBudgets\n\nPDBs ensure minimum availability during voluntary disruptions (node upgrades, deployments):\n\n```yaml\npodDisruptionBudget:\n  web:\n    enabled: true\n    minAvailable: 1\n  worker:\n    enabled: true\n    minAvailable: 1\n```\n\n### Resource Quotas\n\nPer-namespace limits prevent resource exhaustion:\n\n```yaml\nspec:\n  hard:\n    requests.cpu: \"10\"\n    requests.memory: \"20Gi\"\n    limits.cpu: \"20\"\n    limits.memory: \"40Gi\"\n    pods: \"50\"\n```\n\n### Pod Security Standards\n\nCrewAI uses `baseline` Pod Security Standards (not `restricted`) to support BuildKit's Linux capabilities requirements.\n\n## Gateway API Configuration\n\nCrewAI uses GKE Gateway API (not Ingress) for traffic management:\n\n```yaml\n# Gateway (GKE L7 Global External Load Balancer)\napiVersion: gateway.networking.k8s.io/v1\nkind: Gateway\nmetadata:\n  name: crewai-gateway\nspec:\n  gatewayClassName: gke-l7-global-external-managed\n  listeners:\n    - name: https\n      protocol: HTTPS\n      port: 443\n      tls:\n        mode: Terminate\n        certificateRefs:\n          - name: crewai-certificate\n\n# HTTPRoute for traffic routing\napiVersion: gateway.networking.k8s.io/v1\nkind: HTTPRoute\nmetadata:\n  name: crewai-route\nspec:\n  parentRefs:\n    - name: crewai-gateway\n  rules:\n    - backendRefs:\n        - name: crewai-web\n          port: 443\n```\n\nThe gateway Helm chart also includes HealthCheckPolicy, BackendConfig, PodDisruptionBudget, and ReferenceGrant resources.\n\n## Making Infrastructure Changes\n\n### 1. Clone the Repository\n\n```bash\ngit clone git@github.com:konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai.git\ncd kix-services-crewai\n```\n\n### 2. Make Changes\n\nEdit the appropriate files in `environments/{env}/terraform/`:\n\n```bash\n# Example: Update resource quotas for dev\nvim environments/dev/terraform/main.tf\n```\n\n### 3. Create a Pull Request\n\n```bash\ngit checkout -b feature/update-quotas\ngit add .\ngit commit -m \"feat: increase dev resource quotas\"\ngit push origin feature/update-quotas\n```\n\n### 4. PR Validation\n\nGitHub Actions automatically runs on PR:\n- `terraform fmt` check\n- `terraform init` (no backend)\n- `terraform validate`\n- Trivy security scan\n\n### 5. Merge and Deploy\n\nAfter PR approval:\n1. Merge to `develop` (for dev) or `main` (for qa)\n2. Go to **Actions** → **validate-plan-apply**\n3. Click **Run workflow**\n4. Select environment and branch\n5. Review plan output\n6. Approve deployment at the approval gate\n\n## Terraform State Management\n\n| Environment | State Bucket | Prefix |\n|-------------|--------------|--------|\n| dev | `kd-tfstate-dev` | `services/crewai/resources` |\n| qa | `kd-tfstate-qa` | `services/crewai/resources` |\n| prod | `kd-tfstate-prod` | `services/crewai/resources` |\n\n```hcl\n# backend.tf\nterraform {\n  backend \"gcs\" {\n    bucket = \"kd-tfstate-dev\"  # Environment-specific\n    prefix = \"services/crewai/resources\"\n  }\n}\n```\n\n## Required Providers\n\n```hcl\nterraform {\n  required_version = \">= 1.5.0\"\n\n  required_providers {\n    google = {\n      source  = \"hashicorp/google\"\n      version = \"~> 5.0\"\n    }\n    kubernetes = {\n      source  = \"hashicorp/kubernetes\"\n      version = \"~> 2.23\"\n    }\n    random = {\n      source  = \"hashicorp/random\"\n      version = \"~> 3.5\"\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n## Troubleshooting\n\n### Terraform Plan Fails\n\n```bash\n# Check formatting\nterraform fmt -check -recursive\n\n# Validate configuration\nterraform validate\n\n# Check for module version issues\nterraform init -upgrade\n```\n\n### Secret Manager Access Denied\n\nVerify the GitHub Actions service account has permissions:\n\n```bash\ngcloud secrets get-iam-policy crewai-db-password-dev \\\n  --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-platform\n```\n\n### Namespace Already Exists\n\nIf Terraform tries to create a namespace that exists:\n\n```bash\n# Import existing resource\nterraform import 'module.gke_config.kubernetes_namespace.main' crewai-dev\n```\n\n## Additional Resources\n\n- [kix-services-crewai Repository](https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai)\n- [kix-platform-modules Repository](https://github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules)\n- [Terraform GCP Provider Docs](https://registry.terraform.io/providers/hashicorp/google/latest/docs)\n- [GKE Gateway API Docs](https://cloud.google.com/kubernetes-engine/docs/concepts/gateway-api)\n"
}

     5→description: Terraform infrastructure configuration for CrewAI in kix-services-crewai
     6→---
     7→
     8→# CrewAI Infrastructure Setup
     9→
    10→CrewAI infrastructure is managed through Terraform in the [`kix-services-crewai`](https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai) repository. This guide explains the infrastructure components and how they're configured.
    11→
    12→## Repository Structure
    13→
    14→```
    15→kix-services-crewai/
    16→├── environments/
    17→│   ├── dev/
    18→│   │   ├── terraform/           # Consolidated Infrastructure as Code
    19→│   │   │   ├── main.tf              # Infrastructure + Secrets + IAM
    20→│   │   │   ├── variables.tf         # Input variables
    21→│   │   │   ├── outputs.tf           # Terraform outputs
    22→│   │   │   ├── versions.tf          # Provider versions
    23→│   │   │   └── backend.tf           # Remote state in GCS
    24→│   │   ├── argocd/
    25→│   │   │   └── application.yaml # ArgoCD Application manifest
    26→│   │   └── helm/
    27→│   │       └── values.yaml      # Gateway API, HPA, network policies
    28→│   ├── qa/                      # Same structure as dev
    29→│   └── prod/                    # Same structure as dev
    30→├── docs/                        # Internal documentation
    31→│   ├── prps/                    # Product Requirements & Plans
    32→│   ├── runbooks/                # Emergency procedures
    33→│   └── tasks/                   # Task breakdown documents
    34→└── .github/workflows/           # CI/CD pipelines
    35→    ├── validate-and-plan.yml
    36→    ├── validate-plan-apply.yml
    37→    ├── argocd-deploy.yml
    38→    ├── manual-org-initial-setup.yml
    39→    ├── manual-studio-install.yml
    40→    ├── manual-validate-plan.yml
    41→    └── terraform-drift-detection.yml
    42→```
    43→
    44→## Infrastructure Components
    45→
    46→### What Terraform Creates
    47→
    48→| Component | Resource | Description |
    49→|-----------|----------|-------------|
    50→| **Service Accounts** | `google_service_account` | GCP SA for CrewAI workloads with Workload Identity |
    51→| **IAM Bindings** | `google_*_iam_member` | Cloud SQL, GCS, Secret Manager, Workload Identity |
    52→| **Namespaces** | `kubernetes_namespace` | `crewai-{env}` (platform) + `crewai-crews-{env}` (crew workloads) |
    53→| **Network Policies** | `kubernetes_network_policy` | Discrete per-concern policies for fine-grained traffic control |
    54→| **Resource Quotas** | `kubernetes_resource_quota` | Per-namespace CPU/memory limits for both namespaces |
    55→| **GCS Buckets** | `google_storage_bucket` | Data bucket + logs bucket with versioning |
    56→| **HMAC Keys** | `google_storage_hmac_key` | S3-compatible access for CrewAI |
    57→| **Cloud SQL DB** | `google_sql_database` | Three databases: production, cable, oauth |
    58→| **Cloud SQL User** | `google_sql_user` | Service account-based IAM authentication |
    59→| **Secrets** | `google_secret_manager_secret` | DB credentials, HMAC keys, OAuth config, GitHub App |
    60→| **K8s Secrets** | `kubernetes_secret` | Populated from Secret Manager for pod consumption |
    61→| **TLS Certificates** | `google_certificate_manager_*` | Managed SSL certificates for Gateway API |
    62→
    63→### Remote Modules Used
    64→
    65→Terraform composes modules from [`kix-platform-modules`](https://github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules):
    66→
    67→```hcl
    68→# GCS bucket with versioning and lifecycle policies
    69→module "gcs_data" {
    70→  source = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/data-services/gcs?ref=main"
    71→
    72→  project_id  = var.gke_project_id
    73→  bucket_name = "${var.gke_project_id}-crewai-data"
    74→  location    = var.region
    75→  # ...
    76→}
    77→
    78→# Single-secret-per-call pattern for Secret Manager
    79→module "secret_db_password" {
    80→  source = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/secret-manager?ref=main"
    81→
    82→  project_id  = var.gke_project_id
    83→  secret_id   = "crewai-db-password"
    84→  secret_data = random_password.db.result
    85→}
    86→
    87→# Note: Namespaces are created by ArgoCD (CreateNamespace=true)
    88→# Terraform creates resources within the namespace
    89→```
    90→
    91→## Environment Configuration
    92→
    93→### Development
    94→
    95→| Setting | Value |
    96→|---------|-------|
    97→| **GKE Cluster** | `kd-ix-eur-dev-gke` |
    98→| **Domain** | `crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com` |
    99→| **Platform Namespace** | `crewai-dev` |
   100→| **Crew Namespace** | `crewai-crews-dev` |
   101→| **Replicas** | 1 web, 1 worker (HPA: 1-5) |
   102→| **Resources** | 1000m/2Gi requests, 2000m/4Gi limits |
   103→| **State Bucket** | `kd-tfstate-dev` |

   107→

   111→| **Domain** | `crewai.qa.ix.konecta-digital.com` |

   113→| **Crew Namespace** | `crewai-crews-qa` |

   119→### Production

   125→| **Platform Namespace** | `crewai-prod` |
   126→| **Crew Namespace** | `crewai-crews-prod` |
   127→| **Replicas** | 3+ web, 3+ worker (HPA max 10) |

   132→
   133→## Dual Namespace Architecture
   134→
   135→Each environment uses two namespaces for workload isolation:
   136→
   137→| Environment | Platform Namespace | Crew Namespace |
   138→|-------------|-------------------|----------------|
   139→| Dev | `crewai-dev` | `crewai-crews-dev` |
   140→| QA | `crewai-qa` | `crewai-crews-qa` |
   141→| Prod | `crewai-prod` | `crewai-crews-prod` |
   142→
   143→### Platform Namespace
   144→
   145→Contains the core platform components:
   146→- **Web** — Main application server
   147→- **Worker** — Background job processing
   148→- **BuildKit** — Container image building for crews
   149→- **Cloud SQL Proxy** — Database connectivity sidecar
   150→
   151→### Crew Namespace
   152→
   153→Contains deployed crew workloads with:
   154→- **Resource quotas** — Dev/QA: 20 CPU/40Gi; Prod: 80 CPU/160Gi
   155→- **Per-container limits** — Max: 4 CPU/8Gi; Default: 500m/1Gi
   156→- **Pod Security Standards** — Baseline (dev), Restricted (prod)
   157→- **RBAC** — Platform SA has crew management permissions
   158→
   159→### Cross-Namespace Communication
   160→
   161→Network policies control traffic between namespaces:
   162→- Platform pods can reach crew pods for management
   163→- Crew pods can reach platform BuildKit and Worker
   164→- DNS resolution is allowed to `kube-system`
   165→- GCP metadata server access for Workload Identity
   166→
   167→## Secrets Management
   168→
   169→CrewAI uses a dual-layer secrets architecture:
   170→
   171→### Layer 1: GCP Secret Manager (Terraform-managed)
   172→
   173→Terraform creates secrets in GCP Secret Manager:
   174→
   175→```hcl
   176→resource "google_secret_manager_secret" "db_password" {
   177→  secret_id = "crewai-db-password-${var.environment}"
   178→
   179→  replication {
   180→    auto {}
   181→  }
   182→}
   183→
   184→resource "google_secret_manager_secret_version" "db_password" {
   185→  secret=[REDACTED].db_password.id
   186→  secret_data = random_password.db.result
   187→}
   188→```
   189→
   190→### Layer 2: Kubernetes Secrets
   191→
   192→Secrets are consumed by pods through two mechanisms:
   193→
   194→1. **Terraform-created K8s secrets** — Direct population from Secret Manager values
   195→2. **External Secrets Operator (ESO)** — For GitHub App credentials and other secrets managed via `ClusterSecretStore`
   196→
   197→```hcl
   198→resource "kubernetes_secret" "crewai_credentials" {
   199→  metadata {
   200→    name      = "crewai-credentials"
   201→    namespace = local.namespace
   202→  }
   203→
   204→  data = {
   205→    AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID     = google_storage_hmac_key.crewai.access_id
   206→    AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY = google_storage_hmac_key.crewai.secret
   207→    DB_PASSWORD           = random_password.db.result
   208→  }
   209→}
   210→```
   211→
   212→### Secrets Created
   213→
   214→| Secret Name | Contents | Used By |
   215→|-------------|----------|---------|
   216→| `crewai-credentials` | HMAC keys, DB password | Web, Worker pods |
   217→| `crewai-database-config` | DB host, port, name, user | Web, Worker pods |
   218→| `crewai-registry` | CrewAI OCI registry auth | BuildKit, image pulls |
   219→| `crewai-github-app` | GitHub App ID, private key (via ESO) | GitHub integration |
   220→| `crewai-rails-secrets` | Rails encryption key, secret key base | Web pods |
   221→
   222→## Kubernetes Resources
   223→
   224→### Namespaces
   225→
   226→Each environment provisions two namespaces:
   227→
   228→| Environment | Platform Namespace | Crew Namespace | Purpose |
   229→|-------------|-------------------|----------------|---------|
   230→| **dev** | `crewai-dev` | `crewai-crews-dev` | Platform + crew isolation |
   231→| **qa** | `crewai-qa` | `crewai-crews-qa` | Platform + crew isolation |
   232→| **prod** | `crewai-prod` | `crewai-crews-prod` | Platform + crew isolation |
   233→
   234→### Network Policies
   235→
   236→Network policies follow a **discrete per-concern pattern**, with separate policies for each traffic type rather than a single monolithic policy:
   237→
   238→| Policy | Namespace | Purpose |
   239→|--------|-----------|---------|
   240→| `default-deny` | Platform | Deny all ingress by default |
   241→| `allow-gcp-health-checks` | Platform | GCP LB health check IPs (35.191.0.0/16, 130.211.0.0/22) |
   242→| `allow-dns` | Both | DNS resolution to kube-system |
   243→| `allow-gcp-metadata` | Both | GCP metadata server (169.254.169.254) for Workload Identity |
   244→| `allow-k8s-api` | Platform | Kubernetes API server access for replicated SDK |
   245→| `allow-crews` | Platform | Cross-namespace traffic to/from crew namespace |
   246→| `allow-external-https` | Both | Egress to external HTTPS (GCP APIs, registries) |
   247→
   248→```yaml
   249→# Example: GCP health check IPs (mandatory for load balancer)
   250→ingress:
   251→  - from:
   252→      - ipBlock:
   253→          cidr: 35.191.0.0/16
   254→      - ipBlock:
   255→          cidr: 130.211.0.0/22
   256→    ports:
   257→      - port: 8080
   258→        protocol: TCP
   259→```
   260→
   261→### Autoscaling (HPA)
   262→
   263→Horizontal Pod Autoscaler is configured for web and worker deployments:
   264→
   265→```yaml
   266→# HPA configuration in values.yaml
   267→web:
   268→  autoscaling:
   269→    enabled: true
   270→    minReplicas: 1
   271→    maxReplicas: 5
   272→    targetCPUUtilizationPercentage: 70
   273→    targetMemoryUtilizationPercentage: 80
   274→
   275→worker:
   276→  autoscaling:
   277→    enabled: true
   278→    minReplicas: 1
   279→    maxReplicas: 5
   280→    targetCPUUtilizationPercentage: 70
   281→    targetMemoryUtilizationPercentage: 80
   282→```
   283→
   284→### PodDisruptionBudgets
   285→
   286→PDBs ensure minimum availability during voluntary disruptions (node upgrades, deployments):
   287→
   288→```yaml
   289→podDisruptionBudget:
   290→  web:
   291→    enabled: true
   292→    minAvailable: 1
   293→  worker:
   294→    enabled: true
   295→    minAvailable: 1
   296→```
   297→
   298→### Resource Quotas
   299→
   300→Per-namespace limits prevent resource exhaustion:
   301→
   302→```yaml
   303→spec:
   304→  hard:
   305→    requests.cpu: "10"
   306→    requests.memory: "20Gi"
   307→    limits.cpu: "20"
   308→    limits.memory: "40Gi"
   309→    pods: "50"
   310→```
   311→
   312→### Pod Security Standards
   313→
   314→CrewAI uses `baseline` Pod Security Standards (not `restricted`) to support BuildKit's Linux capabilities requirements.
   315→
   316→## Gateway API Configuration
   317→
   318→CrewAI uses GKE Gateway API (not Ingress) for traffic management:
   319→
   320→```yaml
   321→# Gateway (GKE L7 Global External Load Balancer)
   322→apiVersion: gateway.networking.k8s.io/v1
   323→kind: Gateway
   324→metadata:
   325→  name: crewai-gateway
   326→spec:
   327→  gatewayClassName: gke-l7-global-external-managed
   328→  listeners:
   329→    - name: https
   330→      protocol: HTTPS
   331→      port: 443
   332→      tls:
   333→        mode: Terminate
   334→        certificateRefs:
   335→          - name: crewai-certificate
   336→
   337→# HTTPRoute for traffic routing
   338→apiVersion: gateway.networking.k8s.io/v1
   339→kind: HTTPRoute
   340→metadata:
   341→  name: crewai-route
   342→spec:
   343→  parentRefs:
   344→    - name: crewai-gateway
   345→  rules:
   346→    - backendRefs:
   347→        - name: crewai-web
   348→          port: 443
   349→```
   350→
   351→The gateway Helm chart also includes HealthCheckPolicy, BackendConfig, PodDisruptionBudget, and ReferenceGrant resources.
   352→
   353→## Making Infrastructure Changes
   354→
   355→### 1. Clone the Repository
   356→
   357→```bash
   358→git clone git@github.com:konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai.git
   359→cd kix-services-crewai
   360→```
   361→
   362→### 2. Make Changes
   363→
   364→Edit the appropriate files in `environments/{env}/terraform/`:
   365→
   366→```bash
   367→# Example: Update resource quotas for dev
   368→vim environments/dev/terraform/main.tf
   369→```
   370→
   371→### 3. Create a Pull Request
   372→
   373→```bash
   374→git checkout -b feature/update-quotas
   375→git add .
   376→git commit -m "feat: increase dev resource quotas"
   377→git push origin feature/update-quotas
   378→```
   379→
   380→### 4. PR Validation
   381→
   382→GitHub Actions automatically runs on PR:
   383→- `terraform fmt` check
   384→- `terraform init` (no backend)
   385→- `terraform validate`
   386→- Trivy security scan
   387→
   388→### 5. Merge and Deploy
   389→
   390→After PR approval:
   391→1. Merge to `develop` (for dev) or `main` (for qa)
   392→2. Go to **Actions** → **validate-plan-apply**
   393→3. Click **Run workflow**
   394→4. Select environment and branch
   395→5. Review plan output
   396→6. Approve deployment at the approval gate
   397→
   398→## Terraform State Management
   399→
   400→| Environment | State Bucket | Prefix |
   401→|-------------|--------------|--------|
   402→| dev | `kd-tfstate-dev` | `services/crewai/resources` |
   403→| qa | `kd-tfstate-qa` | `services/crewai/resources` |
   404→| prod | `kd-tfstate-prod` | `services/crewai/resources` |
   405→
   406→```hcl
   407→# backend.tf
   408→terraform {
   409→  backend "gcs" {
   410→    bucket = "kd-tfstate-dev"  # Environment-specific
   411→    prefix = "services/crewai/resources"
   412→  }
   413→}
   414→```
   415→
   416→## Required Providers
   417→
   418→```hcl
   419→terraform {
   420→  required_version = ">= 1.5.0"
   421→
   422→  required_providers {
   423→    google = {
   424→      source  = "hashicorp/google"
   425→      version = "~> 5.0"
   426→    }
   427→    kubernetes = {
   428→      source  = "hashicorp/kubernetes"
   429→      version = "~> 2.23"
   430→    }
   431→    random = {
   432→      source  = "hashicorp/random"
   433→      version = "~> 3.5"
   434→    }
   435→  }
   436→}
   437→```
   438→
   439→## Troubleshooting
   440→
   441→### Terraform Plan Fails
   442→
   443→```bash
   444→# Check formatting
   445→terraform fmt -check -recursive
   446→
   447→# Validate configuration
   448→terraform validate
   449→
   450→# Check for module version issues
   451→terraform init -upgrade
   452→```
   453→
   454→### Secret Manager Access Denied
   455→
   456→Verify the GitHub Actions service account has permissions:
   457→
   458→```bash
   459→gcloud secrets get-iam-policy crewai-db-password-dev \
   460→  --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-platform
   461→```
   462→
   463→### Namespace Already Exists
   464→
   465→If Terraform tries to create a namespace that exists:
   466→
   467→```bash
   468→# Import existing resource
   469→terraform import 'module.gke_config.kubernetes_namespace.main' crewai-dev
   470→```
   471→
   472→## Additional Resources
   473→
   474→- [kix-services-crewai Repository](https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai)
   475→- [kix-platform-modules Repository](https://github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules)
   476→- [Terraform GCP Provider Docs](https://registry.terraform.io/providers/hashicorp/google/latest/docs)
   477→- [GKE Gateway API Docs](https://cloud.google.com/kubernetes-engine/docs/concepts/gateway-api)
   478→

  "file_path": "/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-docs/docs/crewai/install.md",
  "content": "---\ntitle: Deployment Guide\nsidebar_label: Deployment Guide\nsidebar_position: 3\ndescription: Deploy CrewAI Platform using ArgoCD and GitHub Actions\n---\n\n# CrewAI Platform Deployment\n\nCrewAI is deployed using **ArgoCD** for GitOps-based application delivery, with **GitHub Actions** managing infrastructure changes. This guide covers the deployment workflow.\n\n## Deployment Architecture\n\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│                    kix-services-crewai                           │\n├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤\n│                                                                   │\n│  GitHub Actions                    ArgoCD                        │\n│  ┌──────────────────────┐         ┌──────────────────────┐      │\n│  │ validate-plan-apply  │         │ Multi-source App     │      │\n│  │ ├─ terraform fmt     │         │ ┌──────────────────┐ │      │\n│  │ ├─ terraform init    │         │ │ Source 1: OCI    │ │      │\n│  │ ├─ terraform plan    │────────▶│ │ CrewAI Helm      │ │      │\n│  │ ├─ approval gate     │         │ │ chart v0.3.13    │ │      │\n│  │ └─ terraform apply   │         │ └──────────────────┘ │      │\n│  └──────────────────────┘         │ ┌──────────────────┐ │      │\n│                                   │ │ Source 2: Git    │ │      │\n│  ┌──────────────────────┐         │ │ Gateway API +    │ │      │\n│  │ Operational Workflows│         │ │ local Helm       │ │      │\n│  │ ├─ org-initial-setup │         │ └──────────────────┘ │      │\n│  │ ├─ studio-install    │         │ ┌──────────────────┐ │      │\n│  │ ├─ argocd-deploy     │         │ │ Source 3: Git    │ │      │\n│  │ └─ drift-detection   │         │ │ values.yaml      │ │      │\n│  └──────────────────────┘         │ └──────────────────┘ │      │\n│                                   └──────────────────────┘      │\n│                                                                   │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n## Prerequisites\n\nBefore deploying CrewAI:\n\n| Requirement | Description |\n|-------------|-------------|\n| **Repository Access** | Write access to [kix-services-crewai](https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai) |\n| **GitHub Actions** | Permissions to run workflows |\n| **GCP Access** | IAM roles for target environment project |\n| **ArgoCD Access** | Ability to view applications in ArgoCD dashboard |\n| **CrewAI Registry** | Credentials stored in GCP Secret Manager |\n\n## Environments\n\n| Environment | Branch | Auto-Sync | Change Window |\n|-------------|--------|-----------|---------------|\n| **dev** | `develop` | Yes | 24/7 |\n| **qa** | `main` | Yes | 24/7 |\n| **prod** | `v*` tags | Manual | Tue-Thu 20:00-23:00 UTC |\n\n### Branch-Environment Protection\n\nThe CI/CD pipeline enforces strict branch-to-environment mapping:\n\n```\nBranch      → Allowed Environments\ndevelop     → dev only\nmain        → dev, qa only\nv* tags     → prod only\n```\n\n## CI/CD Workflows\n\n### Automated Workflows\n\n| Workflow | Trigger | Description |\n|----------|---------|-------------|\n| **validate-and-plan** | PR to `main`/`develop` modifying `environments/*/terraform/**` | Detects changed Terraform directories, runs validation + security scan + plan |\n| **validate-plan-apply** | Manual (`workflow_dispatch`) | Full deploy pipeline: validate → plan → approval gate → apply |\n| **argocd-deploy** | Manual (`workflow_dispatch`) | Deploy or sync ArgoCD applications |\n| **terraform-drift-detection** | Schedule (nightly) | Drift detection with GitHub issue creation |\n\n### Operational Workflows\n\n| Workflow | Trigger | Description |\n|----------|---------|-------------|\n| **manual-org-initial-setup** | Manual (`workflow_dispatch`) | Initialize internal organization and set up admin permissions |\n| **manual-studio-install** | Manual (`workflow_dispatch`) | Install Studio V2 agent and runner |\n| **manual-validate-plan** | Manual / called by nightly | Validate + plan for a single environment (no apply) |\n\n## ArgoCD Application Configuration\n\nCrewAI uses ArgoCD's **multi-source** pattern, pulling from three sources:\n\n### Source 1: CrewAI OCI Helm Chart\n\n```yaml\n- repoURL: oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable\n  chart: crewai-platform\n  targetRevision: 0.3.13\n  helm:\n    valueFiles:\n      - $values/environments/dev/helm/values.yaml\n```\n\n### Source 2: Local Gateway/ESO Helm Chart\n\n```yaml\n- repoURL: https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai\n  path: environments/dev/helm/crewai\n  targetRevision: develop\n```\n\n### Source 3: Values Reference\n\n```yaml\n- repoURL: https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai\n  targetRevision: develop\n  ref: values\n```\n\n## Deployment Workflow\n\n### Step 1: Make Changes\n\nClone the repository and make your changes:\n\n```bash\ngit clone git@github.com:konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai.git\ncd kix-services-crewai\n\n# Create a feature branch\ngit checkout -b feature/your-change\n```\n\n**Common changes:**\n\n| Change Type | File Location |\n|-------------|---------------|\n| Helm values | `environments/{env}/helm/values.yaml` |\n| ArgoCD app config | `environments/{env}/argocd/application.yaml` |\n| Infrastructure | `environments/{env}/terraform/main.tf` |\n| CrewAI version | `application.yaml` → `targetRevision` |\n\n### Step 2: Create Pull Request\n\n```bash\ngit add .\ngit commit -m \"feat: description of change\"\ngit push origin feature/your-change\n```\n\nGitHub Actions automatically validates on PR:\n- Terraform format check\n- Terraform validate\n- Trivy security scan\n- SARIF upload to Code Scanning\n\n### Step 3: Merge to Target Branch\n\nAfter PR approval:\n- Merge to `develop` for dev deployments\n- Merge to `main` for qa deployments\n- Create `v*` tag for prod deployments\n\n### Step 4: Run Deployment Workflow\n\n1. Go to **Actions** → **validate-plan-apply**\n2. Click **Run workflow**\n3. Select:\n   - **Environment**: dev, qa, or prod\n   - **Branch/Tag**: matches your target\n4. Click **Run workflow**\n\n### Step 5: Review and Approve\n\nThe workflow runs in stages:\n\n```\n[Validate] ──▶ [Plan] ──▶ [Approval Gate] ──▶ [Apply]\n```\n\n1. **Validate**: Format check, init, validate, Trivy scan\n2. **Plan**: Generate Terraform plan with resource counts\n3. **Approval Gate**: GitHub Environment protection (requires approval)\n4. **Apply**: Execute Terraform apply\n\n### Step 6: ArgoCD Sync\n\nAfter Terraform apply:\n- **dev/qa**: ArgoCD auto-syncs within 3 minutes\n- **prod**: Manual sync required in ArgoCD dashboard\n\n**Verify in ArgoCD:**\n\n```bash\n# Check application status\nargocd app get crewai-dev\n\n# Force sync if needed\nargocd app sync crewai-dev\n```\n\n## Helm Values Configuration\n\n### Values Structure\n\nThe Helm values file (`values.yaml`) is organized into two parts:\n\n| Section | Description |\n|---------|-------------|\n| **Part A: Upstream CrewAI chart** | Replicas, resources, service accounts, environment variables |\n| **Part B: Local gateway chart** | Gateway API, health checks, network policies, PDB |\n\n### Key Configuration\n\n```yaml\n# Part A: CrewAI Platform Configuration\nweb:\n  replicaCount: 1  # dev: 1, qa: 2, prod: 3\n  resources:\n    requests:\n      cpu: \"1000m\"\n      memory: \"2Gi\"\n    limits:\n      cpu: \"2000m\"\n      memory: \"4Gi\"\n  autoscaling:\n    enabled: true\n    minReplicas: 1\n    maxReplicas: 5\n    targetCPUUtilizationPercentage: 70\n    targetMemoryUtilizationPercentage: 80\n\nworker:\n  replicaCount: 1\n  resources:\n    requests:\n      cpu: \"1000m\"\n      memory: \"2Gi\"\n    limits:\n      cpu: \"2000m\"\n      memory: \"4Gi\"\n  autoscaling:\n    enabled: true\n    minReplicas: 1\n    maxReplicas: 5\n    targetCPUUtilizationPercentage: 70\n    targetMemoryUtilizationPercentage: 80\n\n# Service Account (Terraform-managed)\nserviceAccount:\n  create: false\n  name: crewai-platform-sa\n\n# External Database (Terraform-managed)\npostgres:\n  enabled: false\n\nenvVars:\n  DB_HOST: \"10.29.0.2\"  # Private IP from Terraform\n  DB_PORT: \"5432\"\n  DB_NAME: \"crewai_plus_production\"\n\n# External Storage (Terraform-managed)\nminio:\n  enabled: false\n\nenvVars:\n  STORAGE_SERVICE: \"amazon\"\n  AWS_ENDPOINT: \"https://storage.googleapis.com\"\n  AWS_REGION: \"europe-west1\"\n  AWS_BUCKET: \"kd-ix-eur-dev-platform-crewai-data\"\n  LOGS_BUCKET: \"kd-ix-eur-dev-platform-crewai-logs\"\n\n# Crew workloads namespace\ncrewNamespace: \"crewai-crews-dev\"\n\n# Secrets (from K8s secrets created by Terraform)\nexistingSecrets:\n  credentials: crewai-credentials\n  databaseConfig: crewai-database-config\n```\n\n:::warning Important\n- Set `serviceAccount.create: false` — Terraform creates the SA\n- Set `postgres.enabled: false` — Use external Cloud SQL\n- Set `minio.enabled: false` — Use external GCS buckets\n- Set `crewNamespace` to match the crew namespace for your environment\n:::\n\n## Upgrading CrewAI Version\n\nTo upgrade the CrewAI Helm chart version:\n\n### 1. Update Application Manifest\n\nEdit `environments/{env}/argocd/application.yaml`:\n\n```yaml\nsources:\n  - repoURL: oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable\n    chart: crewai-platform\n    targetRevision: 0.3.14  # ← Update version\n```\n\n### 2. Create PR and Deploy\n\n```bash\ngit checkout -b feat/upgrade-crewai-0.3.14\ngit add environments/dev/argocd/application.yaml\ngit commit -m \"feat: upgrade CrewAI to 0.3.14 in dev\"\ngit push origin feat/upgrade-crewai-0.3.14\n```\n\n### 3. Validate in Dev First\n\nAlways upgrade dev → qa → prod in sequence.\n\n## Monitoring Deployment\n\n### ArgoCD Dashboard\n\nAccess the ArgoCD UI to monitor:\n- Sync status\n- Resource health\n- Pod status\n- Events\n\n```bash\n# Port-forward to ArgoCD (if needed)\nkubectl port-forward svc/argocd-server -n argocd 8080:443\n\n# Or use CLI\nargocd app get crewai-dev\nargocd app logs crewai-dev\n```\n\n### Pod Status\n\n```bash\n# Check pods in platform namespace\nkubectl get pods -n crewai-dev\n\n# Check pods in crew namespace\nkubectl get pods -n crewai-crews-dev\n\n# Check logs\nkubectl logs -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev\n\n# Describe pod for events\nkubectl describe pod <pod-name> -n crewai-dev\n\n# Check HPA status\nkubectl get hpa -n crewai-dev\n```\n\n### GitHub Actions\n\nMonitor workflow runs:\n1. Go to **Actions** tab\n2. Select **validate-plan-apply** workflow\n3. View job logs for each stage\n\n## Rollback Procedures\n\n### Option 1: Git Revert (Recommended)\n\n```bash\n# Revert the problematic commit\ngit revert HEAD\ngit push origin develop\n\n# Run validate-plan-apply workflow\n# ArgoCD will sync the reverted state\n```\n\n### Option 2: ArgoCD Rollback\n\n```bash\n# List revision history\nargocd app history crewai-dev\n\n# Rollback to previous revision\nargocd app rollback crewai-dev <revision-number>\n```\n\n### Option 3: Kubectl Rollback (Emergency)\n\n```bash\n# Rollback deployment directly (bypasses GitOps)\nkubectl rollout undo deployment/crewai-web -n crewai-dev\n\n# Note: This will be overwritten on next ArgoCD sync\n```\n\n## Nightly Quality Checks\n\nThe `terraform-drift-detection` workflow runs on a nightly schedule:\n\n- Validates `develop` → `dev` configuration\n- Validates `main` → `qa` configuration\n- Runs drift detection for all environments\n- Creates GitHub issues if drift is found\n\n## Troubleshooting\n\n### ArgoCD Sync Failed\n\n```bash\n# Check sync status\nargocd app get crewai-dev\n\n# View sync errors\nargocd app sync crewai-dev --dry-run\n\n# Force hard refresh\nargocd app get crewai-dev --hard-refresh\n```\n\n### Pods Not Starting\n\n```bash\n# Check events\nkubectl get events -n crewai-dev --sort-by='.lastTimestamp'\n\n# Check pod status\nkubectl describe pod -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev\n```\n\n### ImagePullBackOff\n\nRegistry authentication issue:\n\n```bash\n# Check the registry secret\nkubectl get secret crewai-registry -n crewai-dev -o yaml\n\n# Verify secret exists and is correct in Terraform\n```\n\n### Database Connection Failed\n\n```bash\n# Check database config secret\nkubectl get secret crewai-database-config -n crewai-dev -o yaml\n\n# Test connectivity from pod\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- \\\n  psql -h $DB_HOST -U $DB_USER -d $DB_NAME\n```\n\n## Next Steps\n\nAfter successful deployment:\n\n1. **[Post-Installation Setup](post-install)** — Initialize organization and configure admin access\n2. **Verify Access** — Test the application URL\n3. **Configure Monitoring** — Set up alerts in Grafana\n\n## Additional Resources\n\n- [kix-services-crewai Repository](https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai)\n- [ArgoCD Documentation](https://argo-cd.readthedocs.io/)\n- [CrewAI Enterprise Portal](https://enterprise.crewai.com)\n"
}
```

   178→
   179→```
   180→[Validate] ──▶ [Plan] ──▶ [Approval Gate] ──▶ [Apply]
   181→```
   182→
   183→1. **Validate**: Format check, init, validate, Trivy scan
   184→2. **Plan**: Generate Terraform plan with resource counts
   185→3. **Approval Gate**: GitHub Environment protection (requires approval)
   186→4. **Apply**: Execute Terraform apply
   187→
   188→### Step 6: ArgoCD Sync
   189→
   190→After Terraform apply:
   191→- **dev/qa**: ArgoCD auto-syncs within 3 minutes
   192→- **prod**: Manual sync required in ArgoCD dashboard
   193→
   194→**Verify in ArgoCD:**
   195→
   196→```bash
   197→# Check application status
   198→argocd app get crewai-dev
   199→
   200→# Force sync if needed
   201→argocd app sync crewai-dev
   202→```
   203→
   204→## Helm Values Configuration
   205→
   206→### Values Structure
   207→
   208→The Helm values file (`values.yaml`) is organized into two parts:
   209→
   210→| Section | Description |
   211→|---------|-------------|
   212→| **Part A: Upstream CrewAI chart** | Replicas, resources, service accounts, environment variables |
   213→| **Part B: Local gateway chart** | Gateway API, health checks, network policies, PDB |
   214→
   215→### Key Configuration
   216→
   217→```yaml
   218→# Part A: CrewAI Platform Configuration
   219→web:
   220→  replicaCount: 1  # dev: 1, qa: 2, prod: 3
   221→  resources:
   222→    requests:
   223→      cpu: "1000m"
   224→      memory: "2Gi"
   225→    limits:
   226→      cpu: "2000m"
   227→      memory: "4Gi"
   228→  autoscaling:
   229→    enabled: true
   230→    minReplicas: 1
   231→    maxReplicas: 5
   232→    targetCPUUtilizationPercentage: 70
   233→    targetMemoryUtilizationPercentage: 80
   234→
   235→worker:
   236→  replicaCount: 1
   237→  resources:
   238→    requests:
   239→      cpu: "1000m"
   240→      memory: "2Gi"
   241→    limits:
   242→      cpu: "2000m"
   243→      memory: "4Gi"
   244→  autoscaling:
   245→    enabled: true
   246→    minReplicas: 1
   247→    maxReplicas: 5
   248→    targetCPUUtilizationPercentage: 70
   249→    targetMemoryUtilizationPercentage: 80
   250→
   251→# Service Account (Terraform-managed)
   252→serviceAccount:
   253→  create: false
   254→  name: crewai-platform-sa
   255→
   256→# External Database (Terraform-managed)
   257→postgres:
   258→  enabled: false
   259→
   260→envVars:
   261→  DB_HOST: "10.29.0.2"  # Private IP from Terraform
   262→  DB_PORT: "5432"
   263→  DB_NAME: "crewai_plus_production"
   264→
   265→# External Storage (Terraform-managed)
   266→minio:
   267→  enabled: false
   268→
   269→envVars:
   270→  STORAGE_SERVICE: "amazon"
   271→  AWS_ENDPOINT: "https://storage.googleapis.com"
   272→  AWS_REGION: "europe-west1"
   273→  AWS_BUCKET: "kd-ix-eur-dev-platform-crewai-data"
   274→  LOGS_BUCKET: "kd-ix-eur-dev-platform-crewai-logs"
   275→
   276→# Crew workloads namespace
   277→crewNamespace: "crewai-crews-dev"
   278→
   279→# Secrets (from K8s secrets created by Terraform)
   280→existingSecrets:
   281→  credentials: crewai-credentials
   282→  databaseConfig: crewai-database-config
   283→```
   284→
   285→:::warning Important
   286→- Set `serviceAccount.create: false` — Terraform creates the SA
   287→- Set `postgres.enabled: false` — Use external Cloud SQL
   288→- Set `minio.enabled: false` — Use external GCS buckets
   289→- Set `crewNamespace` to match the crew namespace for your environment
   290→:::
   291→
   292→## Upgrading CrewAI Version
   293→
   294→To upgrade the CrewAI Helm chart version:
   295→
   296→### 1. Update Application Manifest
   297→
   298→Edit `environments/{env}/argocd/application.yaml`:
   299→
   300→```yaml
   301→sources:
   302→  - repoURL: oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable
   303→    chart: crewai-platform
   304→    targetRevision: 0.3.14  # ← Update version
   305→```
   306→
   307→### 2. Create PR and Deploy
   308→
   309→```bash
   310→git checkout -b feat/upgrade-crewai-0.3.14
   311→git add environments/dev/argocd/application.yaml
   312→git commit -m "feat: upgrade CrewAI to 0.3.14 in dev"
   313→git push origin feat/upgrade-crewai-0.3.14
   314→```
   315→
   316→### 3. Validate in Dev First
   317→
   318→Always upgrade dev → qa → prod in sequence.
   319→
   320→## Monitoring Deployment
   321→
   322→### ArgoCD Dashboard
   323→
   324→Access the ArgoCD UI to monitor:
   325→- Sync status
   326→- Resource health
   327→- Pod status
   328→- Events
   329→
   330→```bash
   331→# Port-forward to ArgoCD (if needed)
   332→kubectl port-forward svc/argocd-server -n argocd 8080:443
   333→
   334→# Or use CLI
   335→argocd app get crewai-dev
   336→argocd app logs crewai-dev
   337→```
   338→
   339→### Pod Status
   340→
   341→```bash
   342→# Check pods in platform namespace
   343→kubectl get pods -n crewai-dev
   344→
   345→# Check pods in crew namespace
   346→kubectl get pods -n crewai-crews-dev
   347→
   348→# Check logs
   349→kubectl logs -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev
   350→
   351→# Describe pod for events
   352→kubectl describe pod <pod-name> -n crewai-dev
   353→
   354→# Check HPA status
   355→kubectl get hpa -n crewai-dev
   356→```
   357→
   358→### GitHub Actions
   359→
   360→Monitor workflow runs:
   361→1. Go to **Actions** tab
   362→2. Select **validate-plan-apply** workflow
   363→3. View job logs for each stage
   364→
   365→## Rollback Procedures
   366→
   367→### Option 1: Git Revert (Recommended)
   368→
   369→```bash
   370→# Revert the problematic commit
   371→git revert HEAD
   372→git push origin develop
   373→
   374→# Run validate-plan-apply workflow
   375→# ArgoCD will sync the reverted state
   376→```
   377→
   378→### Option 2: ArgoCD Rollback
   379→
   380→```bash
   381→# List revision history
   382→argocd app history crewai-dev
   383→
   384→# Rollback to previous revision
   385→argocd app rollback crewai-dev <revision-number>
   386→```
   387→
   388→### Option 3: Kubectl Rollback (Emergency)
   389→
   390→```bash
   391→# Rollback deployment directly (bypasses GitOps)
   392→kubectl rollout undo deployment/crewai-web -n crewai-dev
   393→
   394→# Note: This will be overwritten on next ArgoCD sync
   395→```
   396→
   397→## Nightly Quality Checks
   398→
   399→The `terraform-drift-detection` workflow runs on a nightly schedule:
   400→
   401→- Validates `develop` → `dev` configuration
   402→- Validates `main` → `qa` configuration
   403→- Runs drift detection for all environments
   404→- Creates GitHub issues if drift is found
   405→
   406→## Troubleshooting
   407→
   408→### ArgoCD Sync Failed
   409→
   410→```bash
   411→# Check sync status
   412→argocd app get crewai-dev
   413→
   414→# View sync errors
   415→argocd app sync crewai-dev --dry-run
   416→
   417→# Force hard refresh
   418→argocd app get crewai-dev --hard-refresh
   419→```
   420→
   421→### Pods Not Starting
   422→
   423→```bash
   424→# Check events
   425→kubectl get events -n crewai-dev --sort-by='.lastTimestamp'
   426→
   427→# Check pod status
   428→kubectl describe pod -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev
   429→```
   430→
   431→### ImagePullBackOff
   432→
   433→Registry authentication issue:
   434→
   435→```bash
   436→# Check the registry secret
   437→kubectl get secret crewai-registry -n crewai-dev -o yaml
   438→
   439→# Verify secret exists and is correct in Terraform
   440→```
   441→
   442→### Database Connection Failed
   443→
   444→```bash
   445→# Check database config secret
   446→kubectl get secret crewai-database-config -n crewai-dev -o yaml
   447→
   448→# Test connectivity from pod
   449→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- \
   450→  psql -h $DB_HOST -U $DB_USER -d $DB_NAME
   451→```
   452→
   453→## Next Steps
   454→
   455→After successful deployment:
   456→
   457→1. **[Post-Installation Setup](post-install)** — Initialize organization and configure admin access
   458→2. **Verify Access** — Test the application URL
   459→3. **Configure Monitoring** — Set up alerts in Grafana
   460→
   461→## Additional Resources
   462→
   463→- [kix-services-crewai Repository](https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai)
   464→- [ArgoCD Documentation](https://argo-cd.readthedocs.io/)
   465→- [CrewAI Enterprise Portal](https://enterprise.crewai.com)
   466→
```

  "content": "---\ntitle: Post-Installation Setup\nsidebar_label: Post-Installation Setup\nsidebar_position: 4\ndescription: Post installation steps for CrewAI Platform\n---\n\n# Post-Installation Setup for CrewAI Platform\n\n## Overview\n\nAfter successfully deploying the CrewAI Platform via ArgoCD, these initial setup steps are necessary to configure the core system, set up permissions, and prepare the environment for use.\n\n:::warning Important\nComplete these setup steps immediately after deployment. Skipping any steps may result in missing functionality or access issues.\n:::\n\n## Environment Namespaces\n\nCommands in this guide use environment-specific namespaces:\n\n| Environment | Platform Namespace | Crew Namespace | Domain |\n|-------------|-------------------|----------------|--------|\n| **dev** | `crewai-dev` | `crewai-crews-dev` | `crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com` |\n| **qa** | `crewai-qa` | `crewai-crews-qa` | `crewai.qa.ix.konecta-digital.com` |\n| **prod** | `crewai-prod` | `crewai-crews-prod` | `crewai.ix.konecta-digital.com` |\n\nReplace `crewai-dev` with the appropriate namespace for your environment.\n\n## Initial Setup Tasks\n\nThese tasks must be run immediately after installation to configure the internal organization and administration access.\n\n### Option A: GitHub Actions Workflow (Recommended)\n\nThe `manual-org-initial-setup` workflow automates the organization initialization and admin permission setup:\n\n1. Go to **Actions** → **manual-org-initial-setup**\n2. Click **Run workflow**\n3. Select:\n   - **Environment**: dev, qa, or prod\n   - **Admin email**: Your administrator's email address\n4. Click **Run workflow**\n\nThis workflow performs Steps 1 and 2 below automatically.\n\n### Option B: Manual kubectl Commands\n\nIf you need to run the setup steps manually:\n\n#### Step 1: Initialize Internal Organization\n\nRun the following command to initialize the required internal organization, typically named \"Default.\"\n\n```bash\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- \\\n  rake studio:install_internal_organization\n```\n\n#### Step 2: Set Up Default Permissions\n\nConfigure the default permission set and assign the initial owner role. **Replace `admin@company.com` with your desired administrator's email address.**\n\n```bash\n# Replace with your admin email\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- \\\n  rake factory:setup_permissions_defaults 'factory:add_owner[2,admin@company.com]'\n```\n\n### Step 3: Fix Missing 'Operate' Tab (Troubleshoot)\n\nIf the **\"Operate\"** tab is missing in the Web UI, you may need to manually assign the `Owner` role to your user via the Rails console.\n\n**1. Shell into the `crewai-web` pod:**\n```bash\nkubectl exec --stdin --tty deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- /bin/bash\n```\n\n**2. Start the Rails console:**\n```bash\nbin/rails c\n```\n\n**3. Define the role assignment function (paste this code block):**\n```ruby\ndef assign_owner_role(user_email)\n  user = User.find_by(email: user_email)\n  organization = Organization.find_by(name: \"Default\")\n  owner_role = organization.roles.find_by(name: \"Owner\")\n  membership = organization.memberships.find_by(user_id: user.id)\n  membership.update!(role_id: owner_role.id)\nend\n```\n\n**4. Run the assignment function (replace with your user email):**\n```ruby\nassign_owner_role(\"nathan.booth@konecta.com\")\n```\n\n**5. Exit the Rails shell (Ctrl+D) and refresh your browser.**\n\n## Enable Studio V2 (Optional)\n\n:::info What is Studio V2?\nStudio V2 is the next-generation interface and agent capability for CrewAI, providing enhanced features and improved user experience.\n:::\n\n### Option A: GitHub Actions Workflow (Recommended)\n\nThe `manual-studio-install` workflow automates the full Studio V2 installation:\n\n1. Go to **Actions** → **manual-studio-install**\n2. Click **Run workflow**\n3. Select:\n   - **Environment**: dev, qa, or prod\n4. Click **Run workflow**\n\nThe workflow handles:\n- Deleting any existing Studio crew deployments\n- Installing the Studio Agent\n- Waiting for the agent to come online\n- Syncing and re-indexing tools\n- Installing the Studio Runner\n\n### Option B: Manual Installation\n\nIf you need to run the installation manually:\n\n#### Step 1: Check LLM Connection and Delete Old Crews\n\nEnsure you have an LLM connection named **`studio-v2`** configured. Then, shell into the web-service pod and delete any old Studio crew deployments.\n\n**1. Shell into the web-service pod and run:**\n```bash\nkubectl exec --stdin --tty deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- /bin/bash\nbin/rails c\n```\n\n**2. Paste and run the following commands in the Rails console:**\n```ruby\nProvisioner::DeleteProvisionedCrewJob.perform_later(Deployment.find_by_slug(Studio::Install::Const::STUDIO_V2_AGENT_SLUG).id)\nProvisioner::DeleteProvisionedCrewJob.perform_later(Deployment.find_by_slug(Studio::Install::Const::STUDIO_RUNNER_AGENT_SLUG).id)\n```\n\n#### Step 2: Install the Studio Agent\n\nExit the Rails shell (Ctrl+D) and install the core Studio Agent.\n\n```bash\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- \\\n  bash -c \"INTERNAL_ORGANIZATION_NAME=CrewAI ORGANIZATION_ID=1 rake studio:agent:install\"\n```\n\n#### Step 3: Verify Agent Status and Sync Tools\n\nContinuously check the agent status until it reports **\"Crew is Online\"**. Then, sync and re-index the available tools.\n\n```bash\n# Check status - wait until it says \"Crew is Online\"\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- rake studio:agent:status\n\n# Sync and re-index tools\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- rake studio:tools:sync_crewai_tools\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- rake studio:tools:reindex_tools\n```\n\n#### Step 4: Install the Studio Runner\n\nInstall the Studio Runner agent, which handles the execution environment. Wait for the deployment process to fully complete before testing.\n\n```bash\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- \\\n  bash -c \"INTERNAL_ORGANIZATION_NAME=CrewAI rake studio:runner:install\"\n```\n\n## Accessing the Application\n\n### Production URLs (Gateway API)\n\nAccess CrewAI using the configured domain:\n\n| Environment | URL |\n|-------------|-----|\n| **dev** | https://crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com |\n| **qa** | https://crewai.qa.ix.konecta-digital.com |\n| **prod** | https://crewai.ix.konecta-digital.com |\n\n### Development Access (Port Forwarding)\n\nFor local testing without going through the load balancer:\n\n```bash\n# Forward web service port\nkubectl port-forward svc/crewai-web -n crewai-dev 2603:80\n\n# Access in browser\nopen http://localhost:2603\n```\n\n### Check Gateway Status\n\nVerify the Gateway API is configured correctly:\n\n```bash\n# Check Gateway status\nkubectl get gateway -n crewai-dev\n\n# Check HTTPRoute\nkubectl get httproute -n crewai-dev\n\n# Check backend health\nkubectl get gcpbackendpolicy -n crewai-dev\n```\n\n## Verification Steps\n\nAfter completing all setup tasks, verify the platform's readiness:\n\n1. **Access the Web UI** at the environment URL\n2. **Log in** with your configured authentication provider (WorkOS)\n3. **Verify admin access** by confirming the presence of the \"Operate\" tab\n4. **Test basic functionality** by creating a test crew or project\n5. **Check pods are healthy:**\n   ```bash\n   kubectl get pods -n crewai-dev\n   kubectl get pods -n crewai-crews-dev\n   ```\n6. **Check background workers** for successful job processing:\n   ```bash\n   kubectl logs -l app=crewai-worker -n crewai-dev --tail=50\n   ```\n7. **Check HPA status:**\n   ```bash\n   kubectl get hpa -n crewai-dev\n   ```\n\n## Verification Checklist\n\n```markdown\n## Post-Installation Verification\n\n### Core Setup\n- [ ] Internal organization initialized\n- [ ] Default permissions configured\n- [ ] Admin user has Owner role\n- [ ] \"Operate\" tab visible in Web UI\n\n### Application Access\n- [ ] Web UI accessible via domain URL\n- [ ] Authentication working (WorkOS login)\n- [ ] Can create test project/crew\n\n### Pods & Services\n- [ ] All pods in Running state (platform namespace)\n- [ ] No CrashLoopBackOff errors\n- [ ] Worker pods processing jobs\n- [ ] Gateway API routing traffic correctly\n- [ ] HPA responding to load\n\n### Crew Namespace\n- [ ] Crew namespace exists and is healthy\n- [ ] Resource quotas applied\n- [ ] Network policies in place\n- [ ] RBAC bindings correct\n\n### Studio V2 (if enabled)\n- [ ] LLM connection \"studio-v2\" configured\n- [ ] Studio Agent status: \"Crew is Online\"\n- [ ] Tools synced and indexed\n- [ ] Studio Runner installed\n```\n\n:::success Congratulations!\nYour CrewAI platform is now fully installed and configured. Start creating and deploying crews!\n\nFor more information, visit the [Troubleshooting Guide](troubleshooting) or the [FAQ](faq).\n:::\n"
}

   139→**2. Paste and run the following commands in the Rails console:**
   140→```ruby
   141→Provisioner::DeleteProvisionedCrewJob.perform_later(Deployment.find_by_slug(Studio::Install::Const::STUDIO_V2_AGENT_SLUG).id)
   142→Provisioner::DeleteProvisionedCrewJob.perform_later(Deployment.find_by_slug(Studio::Install::Const::STUDIO_RUNNER_AGENT_SLUG).id)
   143→```
   144→
   145→#### Step 2: Install the Studio Agent
   146→
   147→Exit the Rails shell (Ctrl+D) and install the core Studio Agent.
   148→
   149→```bash
   150→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- \
   151→  bash -c "INTERNAL_ORGANIZATION_NAME=CrewAI ORGANIZATION_ID=1 rake studio:agent:install"
   152→```
   153→
   154→#### Step 3: Verify Agent Status and Sync Tools
   155→
   156→Continuously check the agent status until it reports **"Crew is Online"**. Then, sync and re-index the available tools.
   157→
   158→```bash
   159→# Check status - wait until it says "Crew is Online"
   160→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- rake studio:agent:status
   161→
   162→# Sync and re-index tools
   163→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- rake studio:tools:sync_crewai_tools
   164→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- rake studio:tools:reindex_tools
   165→```
   166→
   167→#### Step 4: Install the Studio Runner
   168→
   169→Install the Studio Runner agent, which handles the execution environment. Wait for the deployment process to fully complete before testing.
   170→
   171→```bash
   172→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- \
   173→  bash -c "INTERNAL_ORGANIZATION_NAME=CrewAI rake studio:runner:install"
   174→```
   175→
   176→## Accessing the Application
   177→
   178→### Production URLs (Gateway API)
   179→
   180→Access CrewAI using the configured domain:
   181→
   182→| Environment | URL |
   183→|-------------|-----|
   184→| **dev** | https://crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com |
   185→| **qa** | https://crewai.qa.ix.konecta-digital.com |
   186→| **prod** | https://crewai.ix.konecta-digital.com |
   187→
   188→### Development Access (Port Forwarding)
   189→
   190→For local testing without going through the load balancer:
   191→
   192→```bash
   193→# Forward web service port
   194→kubectl port-forward svc/crewai-web -n crewai-dev 2603:80
   195→
   196→# Access in browser
   197→open http://localhost:2603
   198→```
   199→
   200→### Check Gateway Status
   201→
   202→Verify the Gateway API is configured correctly:
   203→
   204→```bash
   205→# Check Gateway status
   206→kubectl get gateway -n crewai-dev
   207→
   208→# Check HTTPRoute
   209→kubectl get httproute -n crewai-dev
   210→
   211→# Check backend health
   212→kubectl get gcpbackendpolicy -n crewai-dev
   213→```
   214→
   215→## Verification Steps
   216→
   217→After completing all setup tasks, verify the platform's readiness:
   218→
   219→1. **Access the Web UI** at the environment URL
   220→2. **Log in** with your configured authentication provider (WorkOS)
   221→3. **Verify admin access** by confirming the presence of the "Operate" tab
   222→4. **Test basic functionality** by creating a test crew or project
   223→5. **Check pods are healthy:**
   224→   ```bash
   225→   kubectl get pods -n crewai-dev
   226→   kubectl get pods -n crewai-crews-dev
   227→   ```
   228→6. **Check background workers** for successful job processing:
   229→   ```bash
   230→   kubectl logs -l app=crewai-worker -n crewai-dev --tail=50
   231→   ```
   232→7. **Check HPA status:**
   233→   ```bash
   234→   kubectl get hpa -n crewai-dev
   235→   ```
   236→
   237→## Verification Checklist
   238→
   239→```markdown
   240→## Post-Installation Verification
   241→
   242→### Core Setup
   243→- [ ] Internal organization initialized
   244→- [ ] Default permissions configured
   245→- [ ] Admin user has Owner role
   246→- [ ] "Operate" tab visible in Web UI
   247→
   248→### Application Access
   249→- [ ] Web UI accessible via domain URL
   250→- [ ] Authentication working (WorkOS login)
   251→- [ ] Can create test project/crew
   252→
   253→### Pods & Services
   254→- [ ] All pods in Running state (platform namespace)
   255→- [ ] No CrashLoopBackOff errors
   256→- [ ] Worker pods processing jobs
   257→- [ ] Gateway API routing traffic correctly
   258→- [ ] HPA responding to load
   259→
   260→### Crew Namespace
   261→- [ ] Crew namespace exists and is healthy
   262→- [ ] Resource quotas applied
   263→- [ ] Network policies in place
   264→- [ ] RBAC bindings correct
   265→
   266→### Studio V2 (if enabled)
   267→- [ ] LLM connection "studio-v2" configured
   268→- [ ] Studio Agent status: "Crew is Online"
   269→- [ ] Tools synced and indexed
   270→- [ ] Studio Runner installed
   271→```
   272→
   273→:::success Congratulations!
   274→Your CrewAI platform is now fully installed and configured. Start creating and deploying crews!
   275→
   276→For more information, visit the [Troubleshooting Guide](troubleshooting) or the [FAQ](faq).
   277→:::
   278→
```
