{
  "file_path": "/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-docs/docs/crewai/troubleshooting.md",
  "content": "---\ntitle: Troubleshooting Guide\nsidebar_label: Troubleshooting\nsidebar_position: 5\ndescription: Solutions for common issues encountered during CrewAI deployment and operation\n---\n\n# Troubleshooting Guide\n\nThis guide provides solutions for common issues encountered during CrewAI deployment and operation.\n\n:::tip\nUse the debug commands reference at the bottom of this page for quick troubleshooting commands. Remember to use the correct namespace: `crewai-dev`, `crewai-qa`, or `crewai-prod` for platform services, and `crewai-crews-dev`, `crewai-crews-qa`, or `crewai-crews-prod` for crew workloads.\n:::\n\n## ArgoCD & GitOps Issues\n\n### ArgoCD Sync Failed\n\n#### Issue: Application stuck in 'OutOfSync' or 'Degraded' state\n\n**Cause**: Resource conflict, invalid manifest, or dependency issue\n\n**Solution**:\n```bash\n# Check sync status and errors\nargocd app get crewai-dev\n\n# View detailed sync errors\nargocd app sync crewai-dev --dry-run\n\n# Force hard refresh from Git\nargocd app get crewai-dev --hard-refresh\n\n# Check ArgoCD logs\nkubectl logs -l app.kubernetes.io/name=argocd-application-controller -n argocd\n```\n\n#### Issue: 'ComparisonError' in ArgoCD\n\n**Cause**: Resource managed by both ArgoCD and another controller (e.g., Terraform)\n\n**Solution**:\n- Check `application.yaml` for proper `ignoreDifferences` configuration\n- Verify the resource isn't being modified by Terraform and ArgoCD simultaneously\n- Add ignored fields:\n  ```yaml\n  spec:\n    ignoreDifferences:\n      - group: \"\"\n        kind: Secret\n        jsonPointers:\n          - /data\n  ```\n\n### Terraform Issues\n\n#### Issue: 'Resource already exists' error\n\n**Cause**: Resource created outside of Terraform or state mismatch\n\n**Solution**:\n```bash\n# Import existing resource\nterraform import 'kubernetes_namespace.crewai' crewai-dev\n\n# Or for module resources\nterraform import 'module.gke_config.kubernetes_namespace.main' crewai-dev\n```\n\n#### Issue: 'Secret Manager access denied'\n\n**Cause**: GitHub Actions service account lacks permissions\n\n**Solution**:\n```bash\n# Verify IAM policy\ngcloud secrets get-iam-policy crewai-db-password-dev \\\n  --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-platform\n\n# Add missing binding\ngcloud secrets add-iam-policy-binding crewai-db-password-dev \\\n  --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-platform \\\n  --role=roles/secretmanager.secretAccessor \\\n  --member=\"serviceAccount:github-actions@kd-ix-eur-dev-platform.iam.gserviceaccount.com\"\n```\n\n## Deployment Issues\n\n### ImagePullBackOff\n\n#### Issue: Pods stuck in 'ImagePullBackOff' state\n\n**Cause**: Registry authentication failure or image doesn't exist\n\n**Solution**:\n```bash\n# Check the registry secret exists\nkubectl get secret crewai-registry -n crewai-dev\n\n# Verify secret has correct data\nkubectl get secret crewai-registry -n crewai-dev -o jsonpath='{.data.\\.dockerconfigjson}' | base64 -d\n\n# Check pod events for specific error\nkubectl describe pod -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev\n\n# Verify image exists in CrewAI registry (requires auth)\n```\n\n**Common causes**:\n- `crewai-registry` secret not created by Terraform\n- CrewAI registry credentials expired\n- Wrong image tag in ArgoCD application\n\n### Database Connection Issues\n\n#### Issue: 'Unable to connect to database' errors\n\n**Cause**: Database credentials incorrect or database unreachable\n\n**Solution**:\n```bash\n# Check database config secret\nkubectl get secret crewai-database-config -n crewai-dev -o yaml\n\n# Verify DB_HOST is correct (should be Cloud SQL private IP)\nkubectl get secret crewai-database-config -n crewai-dev \\\n  -o jsonpath='{.data.DB_HOST}' | base64 -d\n\n# Test connection from pod\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- \\\n  psql -h $DB_HOST -U $DB_USER -d $DB_NAME\n\n# Check Cloud SQL instance is running\ngcloud sql instances list --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-platform\n\n# Check Cloud SQL Proxy sidecar logs\nkubectl logs deploy/crewai-web -c cloud-sql-proxy -n crewai-dev\n```\n\n#### Issue: 'crewai-database-config' ConfigMap not found\n\n**Cause**: Terraform hasn't created the Kubernetes secret\n\n**Solution**:\n```bash\n# Verify secret exists\nkubectl get secret crewai-database-config -n crewai-dev\n\n# If missing, check Terraform state\ncd kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/terraform\nterraform state list | grep secret\n\n# Re-run Terraform apply if needed\n# Use GitHub Actions validate-plan-apply workflow\n```\n\n## Authentication & Authorization Issues\n\n### WorkOS Authentication\n\n#### Issue: 'Authentication failed' or blank login page\n\n**Cause**: WorkOS credentials incorrect or misconfigured\n\n**Solution**:\n- Verify `WORKOS_CLIENT_ID` matches the WorkOS dashboard value\n- Confirm `WORKOS_AUTHKIT_DOMAIN` is correctly formatted (e.g., `your-domain.authkit.app`)\n- Check that `WORKOS_API_KEY` is properly stored in Kubernetes secrets\n- Verify WorkOS organization is active and AuthKit is enabled\n- Check browser console for authentication errors\n- Review application logs:\n  ```bash\n  kubectl logs -f deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev\n  ```\n\n#### Issue: 'Invalid or expired session' errors or 'key must be 32 bytes' error\n\n**Cause**: `WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD` incorrect format or session key mismatch\n\n:::warning IMPORTANT\n`WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD` requires a **32-character string**, NOT 32 bytes\n:::\n\n**Solution**:\n- Generate correctly using: `openssl rand -hex 16` (generates 16 bytes as 32 hex characters)\n- **Do NOT use**: `openssl rand -base64 32` (generates 32 bytes, encodes to 44 characters - will fail)\n- Verify the password is exactly 32 characters long\n- Ensure the same password is used across all replicas\n- Check that secrets are properly synced from Cloud Secret Manager\n- Restart pods to pick up updated secrets:\n  ```bash\n  kubectl rollout restart deployment/crewai-web -n crewai-dev\n  ```\n\n#### Issue: 'Magic Auth' not appearing as sign-in option\n\n**Cause**: AuthKit not configured with Magic Auth method\n\n**Solution**:\n- Enable Magic Auth in WorkOS AuthKit settings\n- Refresh browser cache or open in incognito mode\n- Verify AuthKit domain is correctly configured\n\n#### Issue: Google Workspace OIDC sign-in fails\n\n**Cause**: Google OAuth not properly configured on GCP Project\n\n**Solution**:\n- Verify Google OAuth consent screen is configured\n- Check OAuth 2.0 credentials are created and authorized for AuthKit domain\n- Confirm `your-domain.authkit.app` is added to authorized redirect URIs\n- Review GCP Console > APIs & Services > Credentials\n\n### GitHub App Authentication\n\n#### Issue: GitHub integration not working\n\n**Cause**: GitHub App credentials misconfigured or callback URL mismatch\n\n**Solution**:\n```bash\n# Check GitHub App secret exists (managed by ESO)\nkubectl get secret crewai-github-app -n crewai-dev\n\n# Verify GITHUB_CALLBACK_URL matches GitHub App settings\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- env | grep GITHUB\n\n# Check ESO sync status\nkubectl get externalsecret -n crewai-dev\n```\n\n**Common causes**:\n- `GITHUB_CALLBACK_URL` doesn't match the URL configured in GitHub App settings\n- `GITHUB_APP_ID` vs `GITHUB_INSTALLATION_ID` confusion — use the correct value\n- ESO `ClusterSecretStore` not syncing from GCP Secret Manager\n\n## Network & Gateway Issues\n\n### Network Policy Issues\n\n#### Issue: Pods unable to communicate after network policy changes\n\n**Cause**: Discrete per-concern network policies may be missing a required rule\n\nCrewAI uses a **discrete per-concern network policy pattern** where each traffic type has its own policy. Missing any one policy can block specific traffic.\n\n**Solution**:\n```bash\n# List all network policies in namespace\nkubectl get networkpolicies -n crewai-dev\n\n# Describe specific policy to see rules\nkubectl describe networkpolicy allow-gcp-health-checks -n crewai-dev\n\n# Verify all expected policies exist\nkubectl get networkpolicies -n crewai-dev -o name\n# Expected policies:\n#   default-deny\n#   allow-gcp-health-checks\n#   allow-dns\n#   allow-gcp-metadata\n#   allow-k8s-api\n#   allow-crews\n#   allow-external-https\n```\n\n**Common missing policies**:\n- `allow-dns` — Pods can't resolve hostnames\n- `allow-gcp-metadata` — Workload Identity fails (169.254.169.254)\n- `allow-k8s-api` — Replicated SDK can't reach K8s API server\n- `allow-crews` — Cross-namespace communication blocked\n\n#### Issue: Replicated SDK timeout or connectivity errors\n\n**Cause**: Network policy blocking egress to Kubernetes API server\n\n**Solution**:\nEnsure the `allow-k8s-api` network policy exists and targets all pods in the namespace:\n```bash\n# Check the policy\nkubectl get networkpolicy allow-k8s-api -n crewai-dev -o yaml\n\n# The podSelector should be {} (empty) to match all pods including replicated SDK\n```\n\n### Gateway API Issues\n\n#### Issue: Application not accessible via domain\n\n**Cause**: Gateway, HTTPRoute, or certificate misconfiguration\n\n**Solution**:\n```bash\n# Check Gateway status\nkubectl get gateway -n crewai-dev\n\n# Check HTTPRoute\nkubectl get httproute -n crewai-dev\n\n# Check GCPBackendPolicy for health check configuration\nkubectl get gcpbackendpolicy -n crewai-dev -o yaml\n\n# Check certificate status\nkubectl get managedcertificate -n crewai-dev\n\n# View Gateway events\nkubectl describe gateway crewai-gateway -n crewai-dev\n```\n\n#### Issue: 502 Bad Gateway errors\n\n**Cause**: Backend pods not healthy or health check failing\n\n**Solution**:\n```bash\n# Check pod health\nkubectl get pods -n crewai-dev\n\n# Check if pods are passing health checks\nkubectl describe pod -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev\n\n# Verify health check endpoint works\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- curl localhost:8080/health\n\n# Check GCPBackendPolicy health check configuration\nkubectl get gcpbackendpolicy -n crewai-dev -o yaml\n```\n\n### DNS Resolution\n\n#### Issue: Domain name not resolving\n\n**Cause**: DNS records not configured or propagation delay\n\n**Solution**:\n```bash\n# Test DNS resolution\nnslookup crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com\ndig crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com\n\n# Check Cloud DNS records\ngcloud dns record-sets list --zone=ix-konecta-digital-com\n\n# Verify Gateway external IP\nkubectl get gateway crewai-gateway -n crewai-dev -o jsonpath='{.status.addresses[0].value}'\n```\n\n## Crew Namespace Issues\n\n### Crew pods not starting\n\n#### Issue: Deployed crews stuck in Pending or failing\n\n**Cause**: Resource quota exceeded or RBAC permissions missing\n\n**Solution**:\n```bash\n# Check resource quota usage\nkubectl describe resourcequota -n crewai-crews-dev\n\n# Check if platform SA has permissions in crew namespace\nkubectl auth can-i --list --as=system:serviceaccount:crewai-dev:crewai-platform-sa -n crewai-crews-dev\n\n# Check RBAC bindings\nkubectl get rolebinding -n crewai-crews-dev\n```\n\n### Crew pods can't reach platform services\n\n#### Issue: Crews unable to communicate with platform web/worker\n\n**Cause**: Network policy blocking cross-namespace traffic\n\n**Solution**:\n```bash\n# Check network policies in crew namespace\nkubectl get networkpolicies -n crewai-crews-dev\n\n# Test connectivity from crew pod\nkubectl exec -it <crew-pod> -n crewai-crews-dev -- \\\n  curl -v http://crewai-web.crewai-dev.svc.cluster.local:443\n```\n\n## Storage Issues\n\n### GCS Access Issues\n\n#### Issue: 'Access Denied' when writing to storage bucket\n\n**Cause**: HMAC credentials invalid or service account lacks permissions\n\n**Solution**:\n```bash\n# Check credentials secret\nkubectl get secret crewai-credentials -n crewai-dev -o yaml\n\n# Verify HMAC key is valid\ngcloud storage hmac list --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-platform\n\n# Test access from pod\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- \\\n  aws s3 ls s3://kd-ix-eur-dev-platform-crewai-data/\n\n# Check bucket exists\ngcloud storage buckets describe gs://kd-ix-eur-dev-platform-crewai-data\n```\n\n## Kubernetes Pod Issues\n\n### Pods Not Starting\n\n#### Issue: Pods stuck in 'Pending' state\n\n**Cause**: Insufficient resources or node issues\n\n**Solution**:\n```bash\n# Check node availability\nkubectl get nodes\n\n# Describe pod to see pending reason\nkubectl describe pod -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev\n\n# Check resource requests vs available\nkubectl describe nodes | grep -A 5 \"Allocated resources\"\n\n# Check namespace quota\nkubectl get resourcequota -n crewai-dev\n```\n\n#### Issue: Pods crashing with 'CrashLoopBackOff'\n\n**Cause**: Application startup failure or configuration error\n\n**Solution**:\n```bash\n# Check pod logs\nkubectl logs -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev\n\n# Check previous logs (if restarted)\nkubectl logs -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev --previous\n\n# Check environment variables\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- env | sort\n\n# Check events\nkubectl get events -n crewai-dev --sort-by='.lastTimestamp'\n```\n\n### Secrets Issues\n\n#### Issue: Environment variables not populated from secrets\n\n**Cause**: Secret not created or not mounted correctly\n\n**Solution**:\n```bash\n# List secrets in namespace\nkubectl get secrets -n crewai-dev\n\n# Check specific secret exists and has data\nkubectl describe secret crewai-credentials -n crewai-dev\n\n# Verify pod has secret mounted\nkubectl get pod -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev -o yaml | grep -A 10 envFrom\n\n# Check ESO sync status for ESO-managed secrets\nkubectl get externalsecret -n crewai-dev\n\n# Restart pods to pick up secret changes\nkubectl rollout restart deployment/crewai-web -n crewai-dev\n```\n\n## Debug Commands Reference\n\n:::tip Quick Reference\nQuick reference for common debugging commands\n:::\n\n```bash\n# ============== ArgoCD ==============\nargocd app get crewai-dev\nargocd app sync crewai-dev\nargocd app logs crewai-dev\nargocd app history crewai-dev\n\n# ============== Platform Pods ==============\nkubectl get pods -n crewai-dev\nkubectl logs -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev\nkubectl logs deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev --previous\nkubectl describe pod -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev\n\n# ============== Crew Pods ==============\nkubectl get pods -n crewai-crews-dev\nkubectl describe resourcequota -n crewai-crews-dev\nkubectl get networkpolicies -n crewai-crews-dev\n\n# ============== Services ==============\nkubectl get svc -n crewai-dev\nkubectl get gateway -n crewai-dev\nkubectl get httproute -n crewai-dev\n\n# ============== Autoscaling ==============\nkubectl get hpa -n crewai-dev\nkubectl describe hpa -n crewai-dev\nkubectl get pdb -n crewai-dev\n\n# ============== Network Policies ==============\nkubectl get networkpolicies -n crewai-dev\nkubectl describe networkpolicy -n crewai-dev\n\n# ============== Secrets ==============\nkubectl get secrets -n crewai-dev\nkubectl describe secret crewai-credentials -n crewai-dev\nkubectl describe secret crewai-database-config -n crewai-dev\nkubectl get externalsecret -n crewai-dev\n\n# ============== Events ==============\nkubectl get events -n crewai-dev --sort-by='.lastTimestamp'\n\n# ============== Exec into Pod ==============\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- /bin/bash\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- env\n\n# ============== Port Forward ==============\nkubectl port-forward svc/crewai-web -n crewai-dev 2603:80\n\n# ============== Rollout ==============\nkubectl rollout status deployment/crewai-web -n crewai-dev\nkubectl rollout restart deployment/crewai-web -n crewai-dev\nkubectl rollout undo deployment/crewai-web -n crewai-dev\n```\n\n## Support Resources\n\n| Resource | Link |\n|----------|------|\n| kix-services-crewai Repository | [GitHub](https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai) |\n| Infrastructure Setup | [Infrastructure Guide](gcp-setup) |\n| Deployment Guide | [Deployment Guide](install) |\n| ArgoCD Documentation | [argo-cd.readthedocs.io](https://argo-cd.readthedocs.io/) |\n| kubectl Cheat Sheet | [Kubernetes Docs](https://kubernetes.io/docs/reference/kubectl/cheatsheet/) |\n\n### Reporting Issues\n\nWhen reporting issues, include:\n- Complete error messages and stack traces\n- Output of relevant kubectl commands\n- Kubernetes events (`kubectl get events -n crewai-dev`)\n- Application logs (sanitized for sensitive data)\n- ArgoCD application status\n- Terraform plan output (if infrastructure-related)\n- CrewAI version and environment (dev/qa/prod)\n- Network policy list (`kubectl get networkpolicies -n crewai-dev`)\n"
}
```

     7→
     8→# Troubleshooting Guide
     9→
    10→This guide provides solutions for common issues encountered during CrewAI deployment and operation.
    11→
    12→:::tip
    13→Use the debug commands reference at the bottom of this page for quick troubleshooting commands. Remember to use the correct namespace: `crewai-dev`, `crewai-qa`, or `crewai-prod` for platform services, and `crewai-crews-dev`, `crewai-crews-qa`, or `crewai-crews-prod` for crew workloads.
    14→:::
    15→
    16→## ArgoCD & GitOps Issues
    17→
    18→### ArgoCD Sync Failed
    19→
    20→#### Issue: Application stuck in 'OutOfSync' or 'Degraded' state
    21→
    22→**Cause**: Resource conflict, invalid manifest, or dependency issue
    23→
    24→**Solution**:
    25→```bash
    26→# Check sync status and errors
    27→argocd app get crewai-dev
    28→
    29→# View detailed sync errors
    30→argocd app sync crewai-dev --dry-run
    31→
    32→# Force hard refresh from Git
    33→argocd app get crewai-dev --hard-refresh
    34→
    35→# Check ArgoCD logs
    36→kubectl logs -l app.kubernetes.io/name=argocd-application-controller -n argocd
    37→```
    38→
    39→#### Issue: 'ComparisonError' in ArgoCD
    40→
    41→**Cause**: Resource managed by both ArgoCD and another controller (e.g., Terraform)
    42→
    43→**Solution**:
    44→- Check `application.yaml` for proper `ignoreDifferences` configuration
    45→- Verify the resource isn't being modified by Terraform and ArgoCD simultaneously
    46→- Add ignored fields:
    47→  ```yaml
    48→  spec:
    49→    ignoreDifferences:
    50→      - group: ""
    51→        kind: Secret
    52→        jsonPointers:
    53→          - /data
    54→  ```
    55→
    56→### Terraform Issues
    57→
    58→#### Issue: 'Resource already exists' error
    59→
    60→**Cause**: Resource created outside of Terraform or state mismatch
    61→
    62→**Solution**:
    63→```bash
    64→# Import existing resource
    65→terraform import 'kubernetes_namespace.crewai' crewai-dev
    66→
    67→# Or for module resources
    68→terraform import 'module.gke_config.kubernetes_namespace.main' crewai-dev
    69→```
    70→
    71→#### Issue: 'Secret Manager access denied'
    72→
    73→**Cause**: GitHub Actions service account lacks permissions
    74→
    75→**Solution**:
    76→```bash
    77→# Verify IAM policy
    78→gcloud secrets get-iam-policy crewai-db-password-dev \
    79→  --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-platform
    80→
    81→# Add missing binding
    82→gcloud secrets add-iam-policy-binding crewai-db-password-dev \
    83→  --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-platform \
    84→  --role=roles/secretmanager.secretAccessor \
    85→  --member="serviceAccount:github-actions@kd-ix-eur-dev-platform.iam.gserviceaccount.com"
    86→```
    87→
    88→## Deployment Issues
    89→
    90→### ImagePullBackOff
    91→
    92→#### Issue: Pods stuck in 'ImagePullBackOff' state
    93→
    94→**Cause**: Registry authentication failure or image doesn't exist
    95→
    96→**Solution**:
    97→```bash
    98→# Check the registry secret exists
    99→kubectl get secret crewai-registry -n crewai-dev
   100→
   101→# Verify secret has correct data
   102→kubectl get secret crewai-registry -n crewai-dev -o jsonpath='{.data.\.dockerconfigjson}' | base64 -d
   103→
   104→# Check pod events for specific error
   105→kubectl describe pod -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev
   106→
   107→# Verify image exists in CrewAI registry (requires auth)
   108→```
   109→
   110→**Common causes**:
   111→- `crewai-registry` secret not created by Terraform
   112→- CrewAI registry credentials expired
   113→- Wrong image tag in ArgoCD application
   114→
   115→### Database Connection Issues
   116→
   117→#### Issue: 'Unable to connect to database' errors
   118→
   119→**Cause**: Database credentials incorrect or database unreachable
   120→
   121→**Solution**:
   122→```bash
   123→# Check database config secret
   124→kubectl get secret crewai-database-config -n crewai-dev -o yaml
   125→
   126→# Verify DB_HOST is correct (should be Cloud SQL private IP)
   127→kubectl get secret crewai-database-config -n crewai-dev \
   128→  -o jsonpath='{.data.DB_HOST}' | base64 -d
   129→
   130→# Test connection from pod
   131→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- \
   132→  psql -h $DB_HOST -U $DB_USER -d $DB_NAME
   133→
   134→# Check Cloud SQL instance is running
   135→gcloud sql instances list --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-platform
   136→
   137→# Check Cloud SQL Proxy sidecar logs
   138→kubectl logs deploy/crewai-web -c cloud-sql-proxy -n crewai-dev
   139→```
   140→
   141→#### Issue: 'crewai-database-config' ConfigMap not found
   142→
   143→**Cause**: Terraform hasn't created the Kubernetes secret
   144→
   145→**Solution**:
   146→```bash
   147→# Verify secret exists
   148→kubectl get secret crewai-database-config -n crewai-dev
   149→
   150→# If missing, check Terraform state
   151→cd kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/terraform
   152→terraform state list | grep secret
   153→
   154→# Re-run Terraform apply if needed
   155→# Use GitHub Actions validate-plan-apply workflow
   156→```
   157→
   158→## Authentication & Authorization Issues
   159→
   160→### WorkOS Authentication
   161→
   162→#### Issue: 'Authentication failed' or blank login page
   163→
   164→**Cause**: WorkOS credentials incorrect or misconfigured
   165→
   166→**Solution**:
   167→- Verify `WORKOS_CLIENT_ID` matches the WorkOS dashboard value
   168→- Confirm `WORKOS_AUTHKIT_DOMAIN` is correctly formatted (e.g., `your-domain.authkit.app`)
   169→- Check that `WORKOS_API_KEY` is properly stored in Kubernetes secrets
   170→- Verify WorkOS organization is active and AuthKit is enabled
   171→- Check browser console for authentication errors
   172→- Review application logs:
   173→  ```bash
   174→  kubectl logs -f deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev
   175→  ```
   176→
   177→#### Issue: 'Invalid or expired session' errors or 'key must be 32 bytes' error
   178→
   179→**Cause**: `WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD` incorrect format or session key mismatch
   180→
   181→:::warning IMPORTANT
   182→`WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD` requires a **32-character string**, NOT 32 bytes
   183→:::
   184→
   185→**Solution**:
   186→- Generate correctly using: `openssl rand -hex 16` (generates 16 bytes as 32 hex characters)
   187→- **Do NOT use**: `openssl rand -base64 32` (generates 32 bytes, encodes to 44 characters - will fail)
   188→- Verify the password is exactly 32 characters long
   189→- Ensure the same password is used across all replicas
   190→- Check that secrets are properly synced from Cloud Secret Manager
   191→- Restart pods to pick up updated secrets:
   192→  ```bash
   193→  kubectl rollout restart deployment/crewai-web -n crewai-dev
   194→  ```
   195→
   196→#### Issue: 'Magic Auth' not appearing as sign-in option
   197→
   198→**Cause**: AuthKit not configured with Magic Auth method
   199→
   200→**Solution**:
   201→- Enable Magic Auth in WorkOS AuthKit settings
   202→- Refresh browser cache or open in incognito mode
   203→- Verify AuthKit domain is correctly configured
   204→
   205→#### Issue: Google Workspace OIDC sign-in fails
   206→
   207→**Cause**: Google OAuth not properly configured on GCP Project
   208→
   209→**Solution**:
   210→- Verify Google OAuth consent screen is configured
   211→- Check OAuth 2.0 credentials are created and authorized for AuthKit domain
   212→- Confirm `your-domain.authkit.app` is added to authorized redirect URIs
   213→- Review GCP Console > APIs & Services > Credentials
   214→
   215→### GitHub App Authentication
   216→
   217→#### Issue: GitHub integration not working
   218→
   219→**Cause**: GitHub App credentials misconfigured or callback URL mismatch
   220→
   221→**Solution**:
   222→```bash
   223→# Check GitHub App secret exists (managed by ESO)
   224→kubectl get secret crewai-github-app -n crewai-dev
   225→
   226→# Verify GITHUB_CALLBACK_URL matches GitHub App settings
   227→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- env | grep GITHUB
   228→
   229→# Check ESO sync status
   230→kubectl get externalsecret -n crewai-dev
   231→```
   232→
   233→**Common causes**:
   234→- `GITHUB_CALLBACK_URL` doesn't match the URL configured in GitHub App settings
   235→- `GITHUB_APP_ID` vs `GITHUB_INSTALLATION_ID` confusion — use the correct value
   236→- ESO `ClusterSecretStore` not syncing from GCP Secret Manager
   237→
   238→## Network & Gateway Issues
   239→
   240→### Network Policy Issues
   241→
   242→#### Issue: Pods unable to communicate after network policy changes
   243→
   244→**Cause**: Discrete per-concern network policies may be missing a required rule
   245→
   246→CrewAI uses a **discrete per-concern network policy pattern** where each traffic type has its own policy. Missing any one policy can block specific traffic.
   247→
   248→**Solution**:
   249→```bash
   250→# List all network policies in namespace
   251→kubectl get networkpolicies -n crewai-dev
   252→
   253→# Describe specific policy to see rules
   254→kubectl describe networkpolicy allow-gcp-health-checks -n crewai-dev
   255→
   256→# Verify all expected policies exist
   257→kubectl get networkpolicies -n crewai-dev -o name
   258→# Expected policies:
   259→#   default-deny
   260→#   allow-gcp-health-checks
   261→#   allow-dns
   262→#   allow-gcp-metadata
   263→#   allow-k8s-api
   264→#   allow-crews
   265→#   allow-external-https
   266→```
   267→
   268→**Common missing policies**:
   269→- `allow-dns` — Pods can't resolve hostnames
   270→- `allow-gcp-metadata` — Workload Identity fails (169.254.169.254)
   271→- `allow-k8s-api` — Replicated SDK can't reach K8s API server
   272→- `allow-crews` — Cross-namespace communication blocked
   273→
   274→#### Issue: Replicated SDK timeout or connectivity errors
   275→
   276→**Cause**: Network policy blocking egress to Kubernetes API server
   277→
   278→**Solution**:
   279→Ensure the `allow-k8s-api` network policy exists and targets all pods in the namespace:
   280→```bash
   281→# Check the policy
   282→kubectl get networkpolicy allow-k8s-api -n crewai-dev -o yaml
   283→
   284→# The podSelector should be {} (empty) to match all pods including replicated SDK
   285→```
   286→
   287→### Gateway API Issues
   288→
   289→#### Issue: Application not accessible via domain
   290→
   291→**Cause**: Gateway, HTTPRoute, or certificate misconfiguration
   292→
   293→**Solution**:
   294→```bash
   295→# Check Gateway status
   296→kubectl get gateway -n crewai-dev
   297→
   298→# Check HTTPRoute
   299→kubectl get httproute -n crewai-dev
   300→
   301→# Check GCPBackendPolicy for health check configuration
   302→kubectl get gcpbackendpolicy -n crewai-dev -o yaml
   303→
   304→# Check certificate status
   305→kubectl get managedcertificate -n crewai-dev
   306→
   307→# View Gateway events
   308→kubectl describe gateway crewai-gateway -n crewai-dev
   309→```
   310→
   311→#### Issue: 502 Bad Gateway errors
   312→
   313→**Cause**: Backend pods not healthy or health check failing
   314→
   315→**Solution**:
   316→```bash
   317→# Check pod health
   318→kubectl get pods -n crewai-dev
   319→
   320→# Check if pods are passing health checks
   321→kubectl describe pod -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev
   322→
   323→# Verify health check endpoint works
   324→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- curl localhost:8080/health
   325→
   326→# Check GCPBackendPolicy health check configuration
   327→kubectl get gcpbackendpolicy -n crewai-dev -o yaml
   328→```
   329→
   330→### DNS Resolution
   331→
   332→#### Issue: Domain name not resolving
   333→
   334→**Cause**: DNS records not configured or propagation delay
   335→
   336→**Solution**:
   337→```bash
   338→# Test DNS resolution
   339→nslookup crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com
   340→dig crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com
   341→
   342→# Check Cloud DNS records
   343→gcloud dns record-sets list --zone=ix-konecta-digital-com
   344→
   345→# Verify Gateway external IP
   346→kubectl get gateway crewai-gateway -n crewai-dev -o jsonpath='{.status.addresses[0].value}'
   347→```
   348→
   349→## Crew Namespace Issues
   350→
   351→### Crew pods not starting
   352→
   353→#### Issue: Deployed crews stuck in Pending or failing
   354→
   355→**Cause**: Resource quota exceeded or RBAC permissions missing
   356→
   357→**Solution**:
   358→```bash
   359→# Check resource quota usage
   360→kubectl describe resourcequota -n crewai-crews-dev
   361→
   362→# Check if platform SA has permissions in crew namespace
   363→kubectl auth can-i --list --as=system:serviceaccount:crewai-dev:crewai-platform-sa -n crewai-crews-dev
   364→
   365→# Check RBAC bindings
   366→kubectl get rolebinding -n crewai-crews-dev
   367→```
   368→
   369→### Crew pods can't reach platform services
   370→
   371→#### Issue: Crews unable to communicate with platform web/worker
   372→
   373→**Cause**: Network policy blocking cross-namespace traffic
   374→
   375→**Solution**:
   376→```bash
   377→# Check network policies in crew namespace
   378→kubectl get networkpolicies -n crewai-crews-dev
   379→
   380→# Test connectivity from crew pod
   381→kubectl exec -it <crew-pod> -n crewai-crews-dev -- \
   382→  curl -v http://crewai-web.crewai-dev.svc.cluster.local:443
   383→```
   384→
   385→## Storage Issues
   386→
   387→### GCS Access Issues
   388→
   389→#### Issue: 'Access Denied' when writing to storage bucket
   390→
   391→**Cause**: HMAC credentials invalid or service account lacks permissions
   392→
   393→**Solution**:
   394→```bash
   395→# Check credentials secret
   396→kubectl get secret crewai-credentials -n crewai-dev -o yaml
   397→
   398→# Verify HMAC key is valid
   399→gcloud storage hmac list --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-platform
   400→
   401→# Test access from pod
   402→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- \
   403→  aws s3 ls s3://kd-ix-eur-dev-platform-crewai-data/
   404→
   405→# Check bucket exists
   406→gcloud storage buckets describe gs://kd-ix-eur-dev-platform-crewai-data
   407→```
   408→
   409→## Kubernetes Pod Issues
   410→
   411→### Pods Not Starting
   412→
   413→#### Issue: Pods stuck in 'Pending' state
   414→
   415→**Cause**: Insufficient resources or node issues
   416→
   417→**Solution**:
   418→```bash
   419→# Check node availability
   420→kubectl get nodes
   421→
   422→# Describe pod to see pending reason
   423→kubectl describe pod -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev
   424→
   425→# Check resource requests vs available
   426→kubectl describe nodes | grep -A 5 "Allocated resources"
   427→
   428→# Check namespace quota
   429→kubectl get resourcequota -n crewai-dev
   430→```
   431→
   432→#### Issue: Pods crashing with 'CrashLoopBackOff'
   433→
   434→**Cause**: Application startup failure or configuration error
   435→
   436→**Solution**:
   437→```bash
   438→# Check pod logs
   439→kubectl logs -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev
   440→
   441→# Check previous logs (if restarted)
   442→kubectl logs -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev --previous
   443→
   444→# Check environment variables
   445→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- env | sort
   446→
   447→# Check events
   448→kubectl get events -n crewai-dev --sort-by='.lastTimestamp'
   449→```
   450→
   451→### Secrets Issues
   452→
   453→#### Issue: Environment variables not populated from secrets
   454→
   455→**Cause**: Secret not created or not mounted correctly
   456→
   457→**Solution**:
   458→```bash
   459→# List secrets in namespace
   460→kubectl get secrets -n crewai-dev
   461→
   462→# Check specific secret exists and has data
   463→kubectl describe secret crewai-credentials -n crewai-dev
   464→
   465→# Verify pod has secret mounted
   466→kubectl get pod -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev -o yaml | grep -A 10 envFrom
   467→
   468→# Check ESO sync status for ESO-managed secrets
   469→kubectl get externalsecret -n crewai-dev
   470→
   471→# Restart pods to pick up secret changes
   472→kubectl rollout restart deployment/crewai-web -n crewai-dev
   473→```
   474→
   475→## Debug Commands Reference
   476→
   477→:::tip Quick Reference
   478→Quick reference for common debugging commands
   479→:::
   480→
   481→```bash
   482→# ============== ArgoCD ==============
   483→argocd app get crewai-dev
   484→argocd app sync crewai-dev
   485→argocd app logs crewai-dev
   486→argocd app history crewai-dev
   487→
   488→# ============== Platform Pods ==============
   489→kubectl get pods -n crewai-dev
   490→kubectl logs -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev
   491→kubectl logs deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev --previous
   492→kubectl describe pod -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev
   493→
   494→# ============== Crew Pods ==============
   495→kubectl get pods -n crewai-crews-dev
   496→kubectl describe resourcequota -n crewai-crews-dev
   497→kubectl get networkpolicies -n crewai-crews-dev
   498→
   499→# ============== Services ==============
   500→kubectl get svc -n crewai-dev
   501→kubectl get gateway -n crewai-dev
   502→kubectl get httproute -n crewai-dev
   503→
   504→# ============== Autoscaling ==============
   505→kubectl get hpa -n crewai-dev
   506→kubectl describe hpa -n crewai-dev
   507→kubectl get pdb -n crewai-dev
   508→
   509→# ============== Network Policies ==============
   510→kubectl get networkpolicies -n crewai-dev
   511→kubectl describe networkpolicy -n crewai-dev
   512→
   513→# ============== Secrets ==============
   514→kubectl get secrets -n crewai-dev
   515→kubectl describe secret crewai-credentials -n crewai-dev
   516→kubectl describe secret crewai-database-config -n crewai-dev
   517→kubectl get externalsecret -n crewai-dev
   518→
   519→# ============== Events ==============
   520→kubectl get events -n crewai-dev --sort-by='.lastTimestamp'
   521→
   522→# ============== Exec into Pod ==============
   523→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- /bin/bash
   524→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- env
   525→
   526→# ============== Port Forward ==============
   527→kubectl port-forward svc/crewai-web -n crewai-dev 2603:80
   528→
   529→# ============== Rollout ==============
   530→kubectl rollout status deployment/crewai-web -n crewai-dev
   531→kubectl rollout restart deployment/crewai-web -n crewai-dev
   532→kubectl rollout undo deployment/crewai-web -n crewai-dev
   533→```

   544→
   545→### Reporting Issues
   546→
   547→When reporting issues, include:
   548→- Complete error messages and stack traces
   549→- Output of relevant kubectl commands
   550→- Kubernetes events (`kubectl get events -n crewai-dev`)
   551→- Application logs (sanitized for sensitive data)
   552→- ArgoCD application status
   553→- Terraform plan output (if infrastructure-related)
   554→- CrewAI version and environment (dev/qa/prod)
   555→- Network policy list (`kubectl get networkpolicies -n crewai-dev`)
   556→
```

  "old_string": "### How do I scale my deployment?\n\n**Horizontal Scaling (Recommended):**\n```yaml\n# In my-values.yaml\nweb:\n  replicaCount: 3  # Increase for more capacity\n\nworker:\n  replicaCount: 5  # Scale workers independently\n```\n\n**Auto-scaling with HPA:**\n```yaml\nweb:\n  autoscaling:\n    enabled: true\n    minReplicas: 3\n    maxReplicas: 10\n    targetCPUUtilizationPercentage: 70\n```\n\nAfter updating values:\n```bash\nhelm upgrade crewai-platform oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable/crewai-platform \\\n  --values my-values.yaml\n```",
  "new_string": "### How do I scale my deployment?\n\nCrewAI includes built-in autoscaling via Horizontal Pod Autoscalers (HPA) with PodDisruptionBudgets for availability.\n\n**Default HPA Configuration (enabled for all environments):**\n```yaml\nweb:\n  autoscaling:\n    enabled: true\n    minReplicas: 1    # dev: 1, qa: 2, prod: 3\n    maxReplicas: 5     # dev: 5, qa: 5, prod: 10\n    targetCPUUtilizationPercentage: 70\n    targetMemoryUtilizationPercentage: 80\n\nworker:\n  autoscaling:\n    enabled: true\n    minReplicas: 1\n    maxReplicas: 5\n    targetCPUUtilizationPercentage: 70\n    targetMemoryUtilizationPercentage: 80\n```\n\n**PodDisruptionBudgets** ensure minimum availability during node upgrades and deployments:\n```yaml\npodDisruptionBudget:\n  web:\n    enabled: true\n    minAvailable: 1    # prod: 2\n  worker:\n    enabled: true\n    minAvailable: 1\n```\n\n**Monitor autoscaling:**\n```bash\nkubectl get hpa -n crewai-dev\nkubectl describe hpa -n crewai-dev\nkubectl get pdb -n crewai-dev\n```\n\nAfter updating values, commit to the appropriate branch and let ArgoCD sync the changes."
}
```

  "old_string": "## Operations & Troubleshooting",
  "new_string": "### What is the crew namespace?\n\nEach environment uses **two namespaces** for workload isolation:\n\n| Namespace | Purpose |\n|-----------|---------|\n| `crewai-{env}` | Platform services (web, worker, buildkit, cloud-sql-proxy) |\n| `crewai-crews-{env}` | Deployed crew workloads with separate resource quotas |\n\nThis separation provides security boundaries, resource isolation, RBAC granularity, and operational clarity. Network policies control cross-namespace communication using a discrete per-concern pattern.\n\n**Check crew namespace status:**\n```bash\nkubectl get all -n crewai-crews-dev\nkubectl describe resourcequota -n crewai-crews-dev\nkubectl get networkpolicies -n crewai-crews-dev\n```\n\n### What are the crew namespace resource limits?\n\n| Environment | CPU Request/Limit | Memory Request/Limit | Max Pods | Storage |\n|-------------|-------------------|----------------------|----------|---------|\n| Dev/QA | 20/40 cores | 40Gi/80Gi | 100 | 200Gi |\n| Prod | 80/160 cores | 160Gi/320Gi | 300 | 1Ti |\n\nPer-container defaults: 500m CPU / 1Gi memory (max: 4 CPU / 8Gi memory).\n\n## Operations & Troubleshooting"
}
```

  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "### How do I upgrade to a new version?\n\n**Step 1: Backup Configuration**\n```bash\ncp my-values.yaml my-values.yaml.backup.$(date +%Y%m%d)\n```\n\n**Step 2: Check Available Versions**\n```bash\nhelm search repo registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable --versions\n```\n\n**Step 3: Perform Upgrade**\n```bash\nhelm upgrade crewai-platform oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable/crewai-platform \\\n  --version NEW_VERSION \\\n  --values my-values.yaml\n```\n\n**Step 4: Monitor Rollout**\n```bash\nkubectl rollout status deployment/crewai-platform-web\nkubectl get pods -w\n```\n\nSee the [Deployment Guide - Upgrading CrewAI Version](install#upgrading-crewai-version) for detailed upgrade instructions.",
  "new_string": "### How do I upgrade to a new version?\n\nCrewAI version upgrades are managed through ArgoCD application manifests:\n\n**Step 1: Update the ArgoCD application manifest**\n\nEdit `environments/{env}/argocd/application.yaml` and update `targetRevision`:\n\n```yaml\nsources:\n  - repoURL: oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable\n    chart: crewai-platform\n    targetRevision: 0.3.14  # ← New version\n```\n\n**Step 2: Create PR and deploy**\n\n```bash\ngit checkout -b feat/upgrade-crewai-0.3.14\ngit add environments/dev/argocd/application.yaml\ngit commit -m \"feat: upgrade CrewAI to 0.3.14 in dev\"\ngit push origin feat/upgrade-crewai-0.3.14\n```\n\n**Step 3: Monitor rollout**\n\nArgoCD auto-syncs for dev/qa. Check status:\n```bash\nargocd app get crewai-dev\nkubectl get pods -n crewai-dev -w\n```\n\nAlways upgrade dev → qa → prod in sequence. See the [Deployment Guide - Upgrading CrewAI Version](install#upgrading-crewai-version) for detailed instructions."
}

All documentation has been updated and the build passes. Here's a summary of all changes on the `feature/crewai-docs-update` branch:

## Changes Summary

**6 documentation files updated** across all CrewAI docs (593 additions, 209 deletions):

### Key updates based on kix-services-crewai recent changes:

1. **Dual Namespace Architecture** — Documented the `crewai-{env}` + `crewai-crews-{env}` pattern across all docs. Added crew namespace sections to index, gcp-setup, troubleshooting, and FAQ.

2. **Consolidated Terraform Structure** — Updated repo structure diagrams to reflect the consolidated `main.tf` pattern replacing the previous multi-file layout (service-accounts.tf, kubernetes.tf, etc.).

3. **Network Policies Rewrite** — Documented the new discrete per-concern network policy pattern (separate policies for DNS, GCP health checks, K8s API, metadata server, cross-namespace traffic). Added troubleshooting for missing policies and replicated SDK timeouts.

4. **Autoscaling (HPA) & PodDisruptionBudgets** — Added HPA configuration details (1-5 replicas, 70% CPU/80% mem targets) and PDB (minAvailable: 1) across gcp-setup, install, and FAQ.

5. **New CI/CD Workflows** — Documented `argocd-deploy`, `manual-org-initial-setup`, `manual-studio-install`, `manual-validate-plan`, and `terraform-drift-detection` workflows.

6. **GitHub App Integration & ESO** — Added GitHub App credentials managed via External Secrets Operator, including troubleshooting for callback URL mismatches and GITHUB_APP_ID confusion.

7. **Post-Install Automation** — Added GitHub Actions workflow options (recommended) alongside manual kubectl commands for org setup and Studio V2 installation.

8. **Updated Namespace References** — All kubectl commands now use `crewai-dev` instead of the old `crewai` namespace naming.

I've thoroughly explored the `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-docs/docs/app-migration/` directory and the full documentation structure. Here's what I found:

### What the Documentation Covers

#### 1. **Scope & Overview** (index.md)
- **24 microservices** migration from Cloud Run to GKE
- **Categorized by type**: MCP Servers (14), Business Apps (4), Data Pipelines (3), Bots (1), Prototypes (2)
- **Out of scope**: CrewAI flows/tools, infrastructure repos, config/proxies
- **External dependencies**: Cloud SQL, Firestore, Vertex AI, GCS, Pub/Sub, Google APIs (remain managed services)

#### 2. **Current Architecture** (current-state.md)
- **Current stack**: Cloud Run + GKE Autopilot (Kong, LiteLLM, ArgoCD)
- **Service inventory** with full catalog tables
- **GCP project mappings**: `kd-lab-464110`, `kd-corp-dgt-svc-apps-dev`
- **Current CI/CD**: Cloud Build (8 services) + GitHub Actions (Terraform only)
- **Limitations identified**: Fragmented CI/CD, no GitOps, cold starts, fragmented observability, no service catalog

#### 3. **Target Architecture** (target-architecture.md)
- **High-level diagram**: Dual load balancers, KIX cluster, shared services, managed GCP services
- **Dual ingress architecture**: Kong (APIs) + GKE Gateway API + IAP (frontends)
- **Repository model**: Multi-org (konecta-ix-platform, konecta-ix-mcp-servers, konecta-ix-apps, konecta-ix-workloads, konecta-ix-demos, konecta-ix-services)
- **CI/CD flow**: Service repo → Standard pipeline → ArgoCD GitOps
- **Key design decisions**: repo-per-service, centralized GitOps, shared base Helm chart, cross-org workflow sharing

#### 4. **Migration Phases** (migration-phases.md)
- **Phase 1**: Platform Foundation + Pilot (IQ Portal validation)
- **Phase 2**: MCP Servers (14 services)
- **Phase 3**: Business Applications (4 services)
- **Phase 4**: Data Pipelines & Cleanup
- **Priority tiers**: P0 (IQ Portal), P1 (Kong, LiteLLM, MCP Gateway), P2-P4 (remaining services)
- **Includes migration checklist template**

#### 5. **Kubernetes & Helm** (kubernetes-helm.md)
- **Three-layer Helm architecture**: Base chart library (platform) → Service chart → Environment values
- **Namespace strategy**: 16 namespaces (environment × domain: dev/qa/prod × api-services/mcp-servers/web-apps/shared-*)
- **Resource profiles**: API Service (100m CPU, 256Mi RAM), API Heavy (250m, 512Mi), Frontend (50m, 128Mi)
- **Cloud SQL Proxy sidecar pattern** (authenticated via Workload Identity)
- **Multi-tier app pattern**: Nuxt + FastAPI + cloud-sql-proxy in single pod
- **HPA configuration** by tier (1-20 replicas)
- **ArgoCD ApplicationSet strategy** with git directory generator

#### 6. **Ingress & Networking** (ingress-networking.md)
- **Path 1**: API (Kong) with Cloud Armor WAF
- **Path 2**: Frontend (GKE Gateway API + IAP) with Cloud Armor WAF
- **Internal**: LiteLLM via Kong (NetworkPolicy enforced)
- **Network policies**: Default deny, GCP health check IPs (CRITICAL: 35.191.0.0/16, 130.211.0.0/22)
- **TLS via cert-manager + Let's Encrypt**
- **DNS domains**: dev.api.konecta.io, dev.app.konecta.io (per environment)
- **Service discovery**: cluster.local DNS

#### 7. **Docker Security** (docker-security.md)
- **Base image policy**: Python 3.x-slim, Node.js 20-alpine, distroless for Node.js
- **Image pinning to digest** (updated monthly via Renovate)
- **Multi-stage builds** (builder ≠ runtime)
- **Security hardening**: Non-root user (UID 65532), read-only filesystem, capability dropping
- **Dependency management**: pip-compile with hashes, npm ci (never npm install)
- **OCI labels**: Build metadata, version, commit SHA
- **Size targets**: Python < 200MB, Node.js < 80MB

#### 8. **CI/CD Pipeline** (cicd-pipeline.md)
- **Shared workflows repository**: `kix-platform-ci-workflows`
- **8-stage pipeline**: Lint → Secret Scan → SCA → SAST → Build → Container Scan & Sign → Push → Deploy
- **Security gates** (all fail-stop): Trivy secret detection, Dependency-Track SCA, SonarQube SAST, Trivy image scan, Cosign signing
- **Image tagging**: `sha-{7-char}` (immutable), `v{major}.{minor}.{patch}` (releases), `{env}-latest` (promotions)
- **OIDC authentication**: GitHub → GCP Workload Identity (no JSON keys)
- **Vulnerability SLAs**: Critical 4h→24h→48h, High 24h→7d→14d

#### 9. **GitOps & Promotion** (gitops-promotion.md)
- **Cross-repo flow**: Service repo → standard pipeline → dispatch to gitops repo
- **GitOps repo structure**: `kix-platform-infrastructure/environments/{dev,qa,prod}/{service}.yaml`
- **ArgoCD ApplicationSet** with git directory generator
- **Sync policies**: Dev (auto-sync 24/7), QA (manual 08:00-18:00 UTC), Prod (manual Tue-Thu 20:00-23:00 UTC)
- **Environment promotion**: Dev → QA → Prod with DAST baseline scan before prod promotion
- **Rollback procedures**: Automatic on error rate, manual via git revert or kubectl
- **Sync waves**: Wave 0 (infra), Wave 5 (config), Wave 10 (deployments), Wave 15 (ingress)

#### 10. **Backstage & Onboarding** (backstage-onboarding.md)
- **Capabilities**: Service catalog, Software Templates, TechDocs, CI/CD visibility, deployment status, K8s status, security posture
- **Two onboarding paths**: Via Backstage template (recommended) or manual from template repo
- **catalog-info.yaml** annotations: GitHub, ArgoCD, Kubernetes, TechDocs, SonarQube
- **4 Software Templates**: Python API, Node.js API, MCP Server, Nuxt Frontend
- **Automatic discovery**: Every 30 minutes from all 6 GitHub orgs
- **TechDocs**: mkdocs-based documentation rendered in Backstage
- **Onboarding checklist**: 13-step process from repo setup to verification

#### 11. **Security Architecture** (security.md)
- **Image security**: Multi-stage, base image policy, Trivy scanning, Cosign signing, SBOM generation
- **Supply chain**: Dependency pinning with hashes, pip/npm audit, Renovate, GitHub Actions hardening
- **Runtime security**: Pod Security Standards (restricted profile), non-root, read-only filesystem, capability dropping
- **Secrets management**: External Secrets Operator → K8s Secrets, 1-hour refresh interval
- **Workload Identity**: No service account keys, per-namespace SecretStore with GCP Workload Identity
- **Network security**: Default deny ingress, explicit allow rules, private cluster, Cloud NAT
- **Admission control**: Kyverno policies (image signature verification, no `:latest` tag, resource limits, registry restrictions)
- **RBAC**: Namespace isolation, ArgoCD project RBAC
- **Audit logging**: GCP Admin Activity + Data Access + System Events, 400 days hot storage
- **Compliance**: EU data residency (europe-west1, europe-west4), organization policy constraints

#### 12. **Observability** (observability.md)
- **Stack**: Prometheus (metrics) + Grafana (dashboards) + Jaeger (tracing) + Cloud Logging (logs) + OpenTelemetry (instrumentation)
- **Application metrics**: Counter/Histogram via prometheus_client, `/metrics` endpoint
- **ServiceMonitor pattern**: 30s scrape interval per service
- **Distributed tracing**: OpenTelemetry SDK + OTel Collector DaemonSet → Jaeger
- **Structured logging**: JSON format via structlog, Cloud Logging integration
- **Dashboards**: Platform Overview, Service Health, Kong Gateway, LiteLLM Proxy, Database Connections
- **Alerting**: PrometheusRule-based, routing to Slack/PagerDuty by severity
- **Key alerts**: Pod CrashLoopBackOff, High Error Rate (>5% for 3m), High Latency, HPA at Max, Certificate Expiry, ArgoCD Sync Failure
- **SLOs**: Availability 99.9%, Latency P95<500ms, Error Rate<0.1%, Saturation CPU<80%

#### 13. **Operations & Risks** (operations-risks.md)
- **Cost model**: Cloud Run (pay-per-request) vs. GKE (reserved capacity) trade-offs
- **Cost mitigation**: GKE Autopilot, low minReplicas, KEDA scale-to-zero (post-migration), right-sizing, spot pods
- **Operational overhead**: Reduced (unified CI/CD, GitOps, unified monitoring) + new (Helm maintenance, K8s complexity)
- **Disaster recovery**: Multi-region (eu-west1 primary, eu-west4 secondary), DNS failover, RTO<15m, RPO near-zero
- **Risk register**: 13 risks analyzed (resource sizing, secret exposure, sync storms, team familiarity, Kong config, shared workflow drift, LiteLLM routing, connection exhaustion, cert expiry, namespace exhaustion, Kong SPOF, GitOps corruption, unsigned images, secret leaks, lateral movement, CI compromise)
- **Runbooks**: Scale a service, force sync ArgoCD, restart pods, view logs, debug network, rollback production, disable a service, emergency secret rotation
- **Health checks**: Daily, weekly, monthly checklists

#### 14. **Appendix & Reference** (appendix-reference.md)
- **Technology stack**: GKE Autopilot, Helm 3.13+, ArgoCD 2.8+, GitHub Actions, Artifact Registry, Kong, LiteLLM, ESO, Kyverno, Trivy, Cosign, Prometheus, Grafana, Jaeger, Terraform
- **Namespace map**: 17 namespaces (shared-argocd, shared-kong, shared-litellm, shared-backstage, shared-monitoring, shared-devsecops, shared-cert-manager, shared-secrets, dev/qa/prod × api-services/mcp-servers/web-apps)
- **Repository mapping**: Platform repos (infrastructure, modules, ci-workflows, service-template, backstage, docs) + service repos (22 services across 6 GitHub orgs)
- **Multi-tier applications**: kix-usecase-iq-portal (Nuxt 3000 + Express 8000 + sql-proxy 5432), kix-app-hrfraud (same pattern)
- **Branch → Environment**: develop=dev, staging=qa, main=prod
- **DNS domains**: dev/qa/prod × api.konecta.io / app.konecta.io
- **Change windows**: Dev 24/7 auto-approve, QA 08:00-18:00 UTC, Prod Tue-Thu 20:00-23:00 UTC (no auto-approve)
- **GCP projects**: 8 projects (dev/qa/prod × foundation/platform + shared-platform + lab)
- **Quick reference commands**: kubectl, argocd, helm, gh CLI

### Key Documentation Characteristics

1. **Completeness**: 14 comprehensive guide documents + 1 detailed service guide (IQ Portal) covering the entire migration process from architecture to operations
2. **Audience**: Platform engineers (architecture, detailed technical), developers (how-to guides), leadership (overview and phases)
3. **Practical**: Includes checklists, code examples, commands, configurations, templates
4. **Structured**: Consistent cross-referencing, numbered steps, tables for quick reference
5. **Security-focused**: 13-point security requirements, admission control policies, audit logging
6. **Risk-aware**: 13-item risk register with mitigation strategies
7. **Operational**: Cost analysis, disaster recovery procedures, runbook templates, SLOs

    33→2. **Target State** — Kubernetes architecture and configuration
    34→3. **Pre-requisites** — Required access and platform components

    47→
    48→## Using the Guides
    49→
    50→### Before Starting
    51→
    52→1. Review the [Migration Overview](../index.md) for context
    53→2. Ensure all [pre-requisites](../migration-phases#phase-1-platform-foundation--pilot) are met
    54→3. Verify access to required GitHub organisations and GCP projects
    55→4. Coordinate with the platform team for any infrastructure dependencies
    56→
    57→### During Migration
    58→
    59→1. Follow the guide step-by-step
    60→2. Use the verification checklists at each stage
    61→3. Document any deviations or issues encountered
    62→4. Commit changes incrementally
    63→
    64→### After Migration
    65→
    66→1. Complete all verification steps
    67→2. Monitor the service in the new environment
    68→3. Decommission the Cloud Run service after confidence period
    69→4. Update the service's documentation
    70→
    71→## Contributing Guides
    72→
    73→When adding a new service guide:
    74→
    75→1. Copy the IQ Portal guide as a template
    76→2. Update all service-specific details
    77→3. Include complete environment variable mapping
    78→4. Add any service-specific considerations
    79→5. Submit PR for review
    80→
    81→## Support
    82→
    83→- **Slack**: #platform-engineering
    84→- **Issues**: [kix-platform-docs GitHub Issues](https://github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-docs/issues)
    85→
```

     1→---
     2→title: Target Architecture
     3→sidebar_label: Target Architecture
     4→sidebar_position: 3
     5→description: Target GKE architecture, repository model, and CI/CD flow
     6→---
     7→
     8→# Target Architecture
     9→
    10→This document describes the target state architecture on kix-infrastructure.
    11→
    12→## High-Level Architecture
    13→
    14→```
    15→┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    16→│                           INTERNET                                        │
    17→└──────────┬───────────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────┘
    18→           │ HTTPS (API traffic)                   │ HTTPS (Frontend traffic)
    19→           │                                       │
    20→┌──────────▼───────────────────┐   ┌───────────────▼───────────────────────┐
    21→│  GCP LB + Cloud Armor (WAF)  │   │  GCP LB + Gateway API + IAP +         │
    22→│   (API ingress via Kong)     │   │  Cloud Armor (Frontend ingress)       │
    23→└──────────┬───────────────────┘   └───────────────┬───────────────────────┘
    24→           │                                       │
    25→┌──────────▼───────────────────────────────────────▼────────────────────────┐
    26→│              KIX-INFRASTRUCTURE GKE CLUSTER (eu-west1)                    │
    27→│                        kd-ix-eur-{env}-gke                                │
    28→│  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
    29→│  │  shared-kong (Kong Gateway)                                        │   │
    30→│  │    └── routes to {env}-api-services, {env}-mcp-servers             │   │
    31→│  │    └── routes to shared-litellm (LLM Proxy) → internal only        │   │
    32→│  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
    33→│                                                                           │
    34→│  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │
    35→│  │  Application Namespaces (per environment × domain)                  │  │
    36→│  │                                                                     │  │
    37→│  │  {env}-api-services     │ FastAPI backends, business apps,          │  │
    38→│  │                           bots, data pipelines                      │  │
    39→│  │  {env}-mcp-servers      │ MCP protocol servers (SSE)                │  │
    40→│  │                                                                     │  │
    41→│  │  {env}-web-apps         │ Nuxt 3 frontends  → Frontend LB           │  │
    42→│  │                           (GKE Gateway API + IAM auth)              │  │
    43→│  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │
    44→│  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
    45→│  │  shared-monitoring: Prometheus / Grafana / Jaeger / OTel           │   │
    46→│  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
    47→│                                                                           │
    48→│  ┌───────────────┐  ┌──────────────┐  ┌───────────────┐  ┌──────────────┐ │
    49→│  │ shared-litellm│  │ shared-argocd│  │ shared-secrets│  │  shared-     │ │
    50→│  │ (behind Kong) │  │ (GitOps CD)  │  │ (ESO + CSI)   │  │  backstage   │ │
    51→│  └───────────────┘  └──────────────┘  └───────────────┘  └──────────────┘ │
    52→└───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    53→         │                    │                    │                    │
    54→         ▼                    ▼                    ▼                    ▼
    55→┌─────────────┐  ┌───────────────────┐  ┌─────────────────────┐ ┌─────────────────┐
    56→│  Cloud SQL  │  │  Vertex AI / GCS  │  │  GCP Secret Manager │ │  Cloud Storage  │
    57→│ (PostgreSQL)│  │  Pub/Sub / APIs   │  │                     │ │    (Buckets)    │
    58→└─────────────┘  └───────────────────┘  └─────────────────────┘ └─────────────────┘
    59→```
    60→
    61→## Dual Ingress Architecture
    62→
    63→Two separate ingress paths with independent load balancers:
    64→
    65→| Path | Flow | Purpose |
    66→|------|------|---------|
    67→| **API** | Internet → GCP LB + Cloud Armor → Kong Ingress → `{env}-api-services` / `{env}-mcp-servers` | Machine-to-machine, API consumers |
    68→| **Frontend** | Internet → GCP LB + Cloud Armor + GKE Gateway API + IAP → `{env}-web-apps` | Browser users, human authentication |
    69→
    70→## Repository Model
    71→
    72→Services are organised across multiple GitHub Enterprise organisations under **Konecta Digital**. A central GitOps repo in the platform org holds all Helm value overrides and ArgoCD configuration.
    73→
    74→### Organisation Structure
    75→
    76→```
    77→GitHub Enterprise: Konecta Digital
    78→│
    79→├── Org: konecta-ix-platform (Platform Engineering)
    80→│   ├── kix-platform-infrastructure     # GCP infra + ArgoCD apps + GitOps values
    81→│   │   ├── argocd/                     # ApplicationSets
    82→│   │   └── environments/               # Per-env Helm value overrides for ALL services
    83→│   │       ├── dev/{service}.yaml
    84→│   │       ├── qa/{service}.yaml
    85→│   │       └── prod/{service}.yaml
    86→│   ├── kix-platform-modules            # Shared Terraform modules + Helm base chart
    87→│   │   ├── terraform/                  # Existing Terraform modules
    88→│   │   └── helm/base-service-chart/    # Shared Helm library chart (OCI)
    89→│   ├── kix-platform-ci-workflows       # Shared reusable GitHub Actions workflows
    90→│   │   └── .github/workflows/
    91→│   │       ├── standard-pipeline.yaml
    92→│   │       ├── _build-image.yaml
    93→│   │       ├── _scan-and-sign.yaml
    94→│   │       └── ...
    95→│   ├── kix-platform-service-template   # Template repo for bootstrapping
    96→│   ├── kix-platform-backstage          # Backstage config, plugins, catalog
    97→│   └── kix-platform-docs               # Documentation (TechDocs)
    98→│
    99→├── Org: konecta-ix-mcp-servers (MCP Integration Team)
   100→│   ├── kix-mcp-gateway
   101→│   ├── kix-mcp-ragengine
   102→│   └── kix-mcp-*                       # One per server (~14 repos)
   103→│
   104→├── Org: konecta-ix-apps (Apps Team)
   105→│   ├── kix-app-hrfraud
   106→│   └── kix-app-*
   107→│
   108→├── Org: konecta-ix-workloads (Use Case Teams)
   109→│   ├── kix-usecase-iq-portal
   110→│   └── kix-usecase-*
   111→│
   112→├── Org: konecta-ix-demos (Demo Team)
   113→│   └── kix-demo-*
   114→│
   115→└── Org: konecta-ix-services (Platform Services)
   116→    ├── kix-services-kong
   117→    ├── kix-services-litellm
   118→    ├── kix-services-monitoring
   119→    └── kix-services-backstage
   120→```
   121→
   122→### Service Repository Structure
   123→
   124→Each service repo follows a standard layout:
   125→
   126→```
   127→kix-mcp-gateway/
   128→├── src/                          # Application source code
   129→├── Dockerfile                    # Multi-stage Docker build
   130→├── catalog-info.yaml             # Backstage service catalog descriptor
   131→├── chart/                        # Service-specific Helm chart
   132→│   ├── Chart.yaml                # Depends on base-service-chart (OCI)
   133→│   ├── values.yaml               # Service defaults
   134→│   └── templates/                # Only if custom K8s resources needed
   135→├── docs/                         # TechDocs content (Backstage)
   136→│   └── index.md
   137→└── .github/workflows/
   138→    └── ci.yaml                   # Calls reusable workflows
   139→```
   140→
   141→## CI/CD Flow
   142→
   143→```
   144→Developer Push → Service Repo (any org) → Standard Pipeline
   145→  (e.g. konecta-ix-mcp-servers/kix-mcp-gateway)
   146→       │
   147→       ▼  Calls reusable workflows from:
   148→       konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-ci-workflows@v1
   149→       ├── Lint + Unit Tests
   150→       ├── Docker Build (BuildKit, multi-stage)
   151→       ├── Trivy Scan + SBOM (Syft)
   152→       ├── Push to Artifact Registry
   153→       └── Cosign Sign (OIDC)
   154→                │
   155→                ▼  Dispatch to:
   156→       konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-infrastructure
   157→       Update environments/dev/{service}.yaml with new image tag
   158→                │
   159→                ▼
   160→       ArgoCD watches kix-platform-infrastructure
   161→         ├── Dev: Auto-sync
   162→         ├── QA: Manual sync (08:00-18:00 UTC)
   163→         └── Prod: Manual sync (Tue-Thu 20:00-23:00 UTC)
   164→
   165→       Backstage (shared-backstage)
   166→         ├── Discovers catalog-info.yaml from all service repos
   167→         ├── Shows deployment status (ArgoCD + K8s plugins)
   168→         └── Aggregates docs, CI status, security findings
   169→```
   170→
   171→## Key Design Decisions
   172→
   173→| Decision | Rationale |
   174→|----------|-----------|
   175→| **Repo-per-service** | Team autonomy, clear ownership, independent release cycles |
   176→| **Centralised GitOps** | Single source of truth for what's deployed where |
   177→| **Shared base Helm chart** | Consistency, reduced maintenance, enforced standards |
   178→| **Cross-org workflow sharing** | DRY principle, single place to update pipeline logic |
   179→| **Dual ingress** | Different auth models for APIs vs. frontends |
   180→| **No service mesh (initially)** | Reduce complexity during migration; revisit post-migration |
   181→
   182→## Key Differences from Cloud Run
   183→
   184→| Aspect | Cloud Run | kix-infrastructure |
   185→|--------|-----------|-------------------|
   186→| **DB connection** | Unix socket via VPC connector | Cloud SQL Auth Proxy sidecar (TCP) |
   187→| **Secrets** | Secret Manager direct access | External Secrets Operator → K8s Secrets |
   188→| **Scaling** | 0-10 instances (cold starts) | HPA 1-5+ replicas (always warm) |
   189→| **Ingress** | Direct HTTPS | Dual: Kong (API) + Gateway API + IAP (frontend) |
   190→| **CI/CD** | Cloud Build + Bitbucket | GitHub Actions + ArgoCD |
   191→| **Health checks** | Dockerfile HEALTHCHECK | K8s liveness/readiness probes |
   192→| **Observability** | Fragmented | Unified Prometheus/Grafana/Jaeger |
   193→
   194→---
   195→
   196→**Next**: [Migration Phases](migration-phases) — Phased approach to migration.
   197→
```

     7→
     8→# Appendix & Reference
     9→
    10→This document provides reference tables for the migration including technology stack, namespace mapping, and repository organisation.
    11→
    12→## Technology Stack Summary
    13→
    14→### Core Platform
    15→
    16→| Component | Technology | Version |
    17→|-----------|------------|---------|
    18→| **Container Orchestration** | GKE Autopilot | Latest |
    19→| **Package Management** | Helm | 3.13+ |
    20→| **GitOps CD** | ArgoCD | 2.8+ |
    21→| **CI** | GitHub Actions | N/A |
    22→| **Container Registry** | GCP Artifact Registry | N/A |
    23→| **API Gateway** | Kong | Existing |
    24→| **LLM Proxy** | LiteLLM | Existing |
    25→
    26→### Security & Secrets
    27→
    28→| Component | Technology | Version |
    29→|-----------|------------|---------|
    30→| **Secrets** | External Secrets Operator + GCP Secret Manager | Latest |
    31→| **Policy Engine** | Kyverno | 1.11+ |
    32→| **Certificate Management** | cert-manager + Let's Encrypt | Latest |
    33→| **Vulnerability Scanning** | Trivy | 0.48+ |
    34→| **Image Signing** | Cosign (Sigstore) | 2.2+ |
    35→| **SBOM Generation** | Syft | 0.80+ |
    36→
    37→### Observability
    38→
    39→| Component | Technology | Version |
    40→|-----------|------------|---------|
    41→| **Metrics** | Prometheus | Latest |
    42→| **Dashboards** | Grafana | Latest |
    43→| **Tracing** | Jaeger + OpenTelemetry | Latest |
    44→| **Logging** | GCP Cloud Logging | N/A |
    45→| **Developer Portal** | Backstage | Latest |
    46→
    47→### Infrastructure
    48→
    49→| Component | Technology | Configuration |
    50→|-----------|------------|---------------|
    51→| **IaC** | Terraform | >= 1.5.0 |
    52→| **Kubernetes** | GKE Autopilot | Auto-scaling, managed control plane |
    53→| **Relational DB** | Cloud SQL PostgreSQL | HA with failover replica |
    54→| **Object Storage** | Google Cloud Storage | Regional, lifecycle policies |
    55→| **Identity** | Workload Identity | No service account keys |
    56→| **Network** | VPC with Private Google Access | No public IPs |
    57→| **WAF** | Cloud Armor | DDoS protection |
    58→
    59→## Namespace Map
    60→
    61→### Namespace Structure
    62→
    63→```
    64→shared-argocd          (existing)
    65→shared-kong            (existing)
    66→shared-litellm         (existing)
    67→shared-backstage       (new — developer portal)
    68→shared-monitoring      (new)
    69→shared-devsecops       (new — SonarQube, Dependency-Track)
    70→shared-cert-manager    (new)
    71→shared-secrets         (new — ESO)
    72→
    73→dev-api-services       dev-mcp-servers       dev-web-apps
    74→qa-api-services        qa-mcp-servers        qa-web-apps
    75→prod-api-services      prod-mcp-servers      prod-web-apps
    76→```
    77→
    78→### Namespace Purposes
    79→
    80→| Namespace | Purpose | Services |
    81→|-----------|---------|----------|
    82→| `shared-argocd` | GitOps CD | ArgoCD server, controllers |
    83→| `shared-kong` | API Gateway | Kong Gateway, plugins |
    84→| `shared-litellm` | LLM Proxy | LiteLLM proxy (Kong-only access) |
    85→| `shared-backstage` | Developer Portal | Backstage app, plugins |
    86→| `shared-monitoring` | Observability | Prometheus, Grafana, Jaeger, OTel Collector |
    87→| `shared-devsecops` | Security Tools | SonarQube, Dependency-Track |
    88→| `shared-cert-manager` | TLS Management | cert-manager, ClusterIssuers |
    89→| `shared-secrets` | Secrets Management | External Secrets Operator |
    90→| `{env}-api-services` | Backend APIs | FastAPI backends, bots, pipelines |
    91→| `{env}-mcp-servers` | MCP Servers | FastMCP protocol servers |
    92→| `{env}-web-apps` | Frontend Apps | Nuxt 3 frontends |
    93→
    94→## Repository & Namespace Mapping

   179→
   180→## Quick Reference Commands
   181→
   182→### Kubernetes
   183→
   184→```bash
   185→# List all pods in a namespace
   186→kubectl get pods -n dev-api-services
   187→
   188→# Describe a deployment
   189→kubectl describe deployment kix-mcp-gateway -n dev-mcp-servers
   190→
   191→# View logs
   192→kubectl logs -n dev-mcp-servers -l app=kix-mcp-gateway -c app -f
   193→
   194→# Port forward for debugging
   195→kubectl port-forward -n dev-mcp-servers svc/kix-mcp-gateway 8080:8080
   196→
   197→# Exec into container
   198→kubectl exec -it deploy/kix-mcp-gateway -n dev-mcp-servers -c app -- /bin/sh
   199→```
   200→
   201→### ArgoCD
   202→
   203→```bash
   204→# Login
   205→argocd login argocd.konecta.io --sso
   206→
   207→# List applications
   208→argocd app list
   209→
   210→# Sync an application
   211→argocd app sync kix-mcp-gateway-dev
   212→
   213→# View app details
   214→argocd app get kix-mcp-gateway-dev
   215→
   216→# Force refresh
   217→argocd app get kix-mcp-gateway-dev --hard-refresh
   218→```
   219→
   220→### Helm
   221→
   222→```bash
   223→# Template locally
   224→helm template kix-mcp-gateway ./chart -f values.yaml
   225→
   226→# Diff against deployed
   227→helm diff upgrade kix-mcp-gateway ./chart -n dev-mcp-servers
   228→
   229→# List releases
   230→helm list -n dev-mcp-servers
   231→```
   232→
   233→### GitHub CLI
   234→
   235→```bash
   236→# Create repo from template
   237→gh repo create konecta-ix-mcp-servers/kix-mcp-newservice \
   238→  --template konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-service-template \
   239→  --private
   240→
   241→# Trigger workflow
   242→gh workflow run ci.yaml -R konecta-ix-mcp-servers/kix-mcp-gateway
   243→
   244→# View workflow runs
   245→gh run list -R konecta-ix-mcp-servers/kix-mcp-gateway
   246→```
   247→
   248→---
   249→
   250→**Service Guides**: See [Service Migration Guides](service-guides/) for detailed per-service migration instructions.
   251→
```
